# Engine Architecture & Implementation Lessons

1. **Legacy C++ Parity**: Always refer back to the exact legacy TFS C++ source code when answering questions, reproducing behaviors, or making architectural decisions for edge cases. Do not assume behavior; find the correct `.cpp` file using `grep_search` in the legacy `src/` directory and mirror its logic. 
*(Added: April 2026 after spawning parser error logic mismatch)*

2. **TFS RSA is raw modular exponentiation, not PKCS#1 decrypt**: The `rsa` crate’s `rsa::hazmat::rsa_decrypt` applies blinding and PKCS#1-style semantics for standard encrypted messages. TFS (`src/rsa.cpp`) uses CryptoPP `CalculateInverse` — effectively **c^d mod n** on the 128-byte integer with **no** PKCS#1 unpadding. Those two paths are not equivalent; using the hazmat path against OTClient ciphertext can decrypt to garbage even with the correct key. The fix is **`BigUint::modpow(&d, &n)`** (or equivalent) and then fixed-width 128-byte big-endian encoding of the result, matching C++ `Integer::Encode`. See `crates/tfs-rust-net/src/rsa.rs`.  
*(Added: April 2026 — handshake “wrong key” / PKCS#1 first-byte errors)*

3. **Login RSA offset depends on client protocol version and OTClient branch**: Do not assume a single “legacy” layout. For example **protocol 1098 / OTClient v8** `ProtocolLogin:sendLoginPacket` prepends **`ClientEnterAccount` (u8 opcode 0x01)** and fields such as **u32 client version**, **u16 content revision**, padding, **spr/pic signatures**, and **u8 preview state** before the RSA block — so the ciphertext starts at **offset 26** from the start of the frame body **after** the 4-byte Adler checksum prefix (i.e. `body[26..158]` when `body[0..4]` is checksum). Older layouts use **15** (game), **25** (login ≥971), or **20** (login before 971). When in doubt, read the **actual client source** (e.g. OTClient v8 Lua) rather than guessing offsets. See `crates/tfs-rust-net/src/game_first_packet.rs`.  
*(Added: April 2026 — RSA decrypt OK only after fixing offset for 1098)*

4. **Shutdown save vs logout save**: On normal disconnect, `PlayerSaveData` is built on the `LocalSet` game thread and passed into `tokio::spawn` (I/O off the sim loop; DB is `Send`). On **SIGINT**, the same `build_player_save_data` + `PlayerStore::save_player` must **finish before process exit** — use a dedicated `GameCommand::Shutdown` branch and **`await`** a bounded work queue (`JoinSet` with a max in-flight cap of 8 matches the plan’s `buffer_unordered(8)`). Do not block the game port `accept` loop on the main `LocalSet` future: `spawn` both listeners so `await`ing the `spawn_local` game task can run to completion, then `abort` the acceptors. *(`tasks/config-and-save-plan.md` P4, April 2026)*

5. **Config.lua vs environment (network + DB)**: `NetConfig` / `DbConfig` in `config.rs` mirror TFS C++ `configmanager.cpp` keys and defaults. For containers and one-off tests, **env still overrides**: `DATABASE_URL` replaces the `mysql://…` string built from `mysqlHost` / `mysqlUser` / …; `TFS_LOGIN_ADDR` and `TFS_GAME_ADDR` override bind addresses; `TFS_GAME_PORT` and `TFS_PUBLIC_IP` override what clients are told for the game server. If unset, values come from `config.lua` (and `TFS_CONFIG` chooses which file to load). *(`tasks/config-and-save-plan.md` P1–P2 + P5, April 2026)*

6. **Password hashing (bcrypt + legacy SHA1)**: Rust auth deviates from TFS 1.4.2 plain SHA1. Stored format is self-describing: 40 lowercase hex = legacy SHA1; `$2a$`/`$2b$`/`$2y$` = bcrypt. Successful SHA1 login upgrades to bcrypt on the spot. **Both** bcrypt verify and bcrypt hash run on `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` (I/O path, not game thread); sync `hash_bcrypt` / `verify_password_sync` are for tests and blocking callers only. `$2b$` hashes verify under PHP `password_verify`. Set `legacySha1Enabled = false` in `config.lua` once migration is complete. **Startup runs SQLx migrations** (`run_migrations` in `run_server.rs`). For existing C++ TFS databases, `heal_and_adopt_existing_schema` clears failed `_sqlx_migrations` rows and marks the baseline applied without re-running DDL; only incremental Rust migrations (e.g. password widen) execute. Baseline seed uses `INSERT IGNORE` into `server_config`. *(`crates/tfs-rust-db/src/migrations.rs`, May 2026)*

7. **Player `queryDestination` BFS order**: For `CONST_SLOT_WHEREEVER`, C++ scans equipment slots 1–10 before BFS into bags — stackable merge on a worn stack wins over an empty slot; container roots are queued only after the slot pass. Rust mirrors this in `player_query_destination_wherever` using `ContainerIterator` for deep `queryMaxCount` the same way as C++ `ContainerIterator` in `Player::queryMaxCount`. Store inbox (slot 11) is intentionally excluded (`CONST_SLOT_LAST` = ammo). *(`player_inventory_query_add.rs`, May 2026)*

8. **Walk-to-item throw pre-check (intentional deviation)**: C++ `game.cpp` ~970–983 does not pre-validate throw reachability before `getPathTo` + `setNextWalkActionTask`; the player walks to the item and may fail at execution. Rust `throw_dest_reachable_after_walk_to_item` rejects early with `DestinationOutOfReach` when no adjacent stand tile can throw to the destination — approved UX improvement; valid throws are unchanged. **`try_walk_to_and_action` empty path**: when `getPathMatching` returns an empty dir list (already within `maxTargetDist`), C++ still calls `startAutoWalk([])` → `onWalkComplete` → 400 ms `walkTask`. Returning `false` from `try_walk_to_and_action` incorrectly sends `ThereIsNoWay`; schedule via `on_player_walk_complete` instead. **`dirNeighbors` index**: C++ uses `parent - current` (direction *came from*), not travel direction — `DIRECTION_WEST` when parent is west of current. *(`3951145` walk/pathfinding port, May 2026)*

9. **`playerLookAt` uses `STACKPOS_LOOK`, not `STACKPOS_MOVE`**: C++ `Game::internalGetThing(..., STACKPOS_LOOK)` ignores client `stackPos` on map tiles and calls `Tile::getTopVisibleThing` — creature first, then first non-`lookThrough` down/top item, then ground. Rust initially wired look to `internal_get_thing_move`, which only returned moveable down items and never ground — so grass/water/trees showed cancel instead of `"You see …"`. Fix: `internal_get_thing_look` + `LookTarget::Ground(u16)` with ephemeral `Item` for `Item::getDescription`. Rust stores tile ground as `TileBody.ground: Option<u16>` (type id), not a `SlotMap` `ItemId`. Creature look text still stubbed until Lua `onLook` / `Creature::getDescription` port. *(`game_world_inventory.rs`, `tile.rs`, May 2026)*

10. **Cross-floor look uses `ProtocolGame::canSee`, not same-z viewport**: C++ allows looking at visible tiles on other floors (surface z 0–7 from any surface tile; underground ±2). Viewport bounds shift by `offsetz = viewer.z - target.z`. Rust `can_see_position` was same-floor-only, blocking look-at on floors above/below; fixed by porting `protocol_can_see` (`protocolgame.cpp` ~796). Look distance still adds +15 per floor via `look_distance_tfs`. *(May 2026)*

11. **Inventory postAdd/postRemove side effects**: C++ funnels equip/deequip, weight, light, stats, container UI, and `Player:onInventoryUpdate` through `Player::postAddNotification` / `postRemoveNotification` with `cylinderlink_t` (`LINK_OWNER` vs `LINK_TOPPARENT`). Rust centralizes in `player_inventory_notifications.rs`; slot mutations use `equip_item_to_inventory_slot` / `unequip_item_from_inventory_slot`; backpack changes use `notify_player_container_tree_changed` (TopParent). Lua inventory update requires `fire_on_player_inventory_update` (ScriptContext + mutation scope) — not a bare `EventDispatcher` call. Trade `checkTradeState`, shop `updateSaleShopList`, and depot-owner container branches remain stubbed. Equip abilities land via native `apply_equip_item_abilities` (lesson 181); `inventory_abilities[]` still guards double-apply. *(`player_inventory_notifications.rs`, May 2026)*

12. **P3 inventory utilities scan scope**: `getItemTypeCount` / `removeItemOfType` iterate `CONST_SLOT_FIRST`..=`CONST_SLOT_LAST` (slots 1–10) only — store inbox (11) excluded, same as `queryDestination`. `removeItemOfType` uses C++ if/else-if: equipped direct match when `!ignoreEquipped`, else container `ContainerIterator` only (no descent after a slot-root match). `Item::countByType` uses ported `get_sub_type` (fluid / stack count / charges). Batch removal reuses `container_remove_thing` and `unequip_item_from_inventory_slot` for notifications. `getWeaponSkill` uses `Player::skill_level` (base + `var_skills`). GM capacity via `groups.xml` → `player_flags.rs` on `GameWorld.groups`. *(`player_inventory_util.rs`, `game_world_inventory.rs`, May 2026)*

13. **P5 Lua inventory API boundaries**: Reads extend `ScriptContext` in `tfs-rust-common` (`ScriptCylinder`, container queries, `find_player_item_by_type`); mutations extend `LuaMutation` with immediate `apply_lua_mutation` in `lua_scope.rs` so scripts see mid-callback state. `ContainerRef` shares `ItemId` with `ItemRef`; mlua metatable `__index` chains Container → Item for compat with `data/lib/compat/compat.lua`. Equip MoveEvents run under `with_equip_mutation_scope` because callbacks may call `moveTo`/`addItem`. `player_query_add` equip probe requires `&mut GameWorld` — propagate `&mut self` through query chain or snapshot item fields before the probe to satisfy borrow checker. MoveEvents loader injects default `onEquipItem`/`onDeEquipItem` when `movements/lib/movements.lua` is empty; `req_level` checked in Rust before Lua. Deferred: full `data/lib` load, XML `inventoryAbilities`, compat `MoveEvent():register()`. *(`tfs-rust-lua`, `lua_scope.rs`, `move_events.rs`, May 2026)*

14. **`is_map_tile` must not test `pos.y & 0x40`**: C++ `Game::internalGetCylinder` treats any `pos.x != 0xFFFF` as a map tile (`game.cpp` ~199). The `0x40` bit in `pos.y` is **only** the open-container encoding when `pos.x == 0xFFFF`. Rust incorrectly required `(pos.y & 0x40) == 0` for map use — tiles with y ≥ 64 (e.g. y=126) were skipped for ladder/grate teleport and fell through to container open with no visible effect. Fix: `is_map_tile = pos.x != 0xFFFF`. *(`container_ui.rs`, May 2026)*

15. **Map `AddCreature` light: `access_player` is GM-only, not `is_self`**: C++ `ProtocolGame::AddCreature` writes light level `0xFF` only when the **viewing** player `isAccessPlayer()` (`protocolgame.cpp` ~3207), and otherwise uses `creature->getCreatureLight()`. Rust wrongly set `access_player: is_self`, so every post-teleport `0x64` map refresh encoded the local player at light `0xFF` (full brightness). Unequipping a torch “fixed” it by sending a separate `0x8D` creature-light packet. Fix: pass `items_light` + `viewer_is_access_player(group.access)`. *(`login_out.rs`, May 2026)*

16. **Phase D.1 walk engine generalization**: C++ `Game::internalMoveCreature(Creature*, Direction)` applies height-based floor change **only when `creature->getPlayer()`** (`game.cpp` ~805); monsters/NPCs use flat `getNextPosition`. Walk timing (`addEventWalk`, `onWalk`, `checkCreatureWalk`) lives on base `Creature` — Rust routes `next_walk_check` / `walk_queue` / `last_step*` through `CreatureBase`. `Player::getStepSpeed` clamps to `PLAYER_MIN/MAX_SPEED`; base creatures use raw `getSpeed()`. Spectator `0x6D` for non-players uses `non_player_wire_id(cid)` (slot key low 32 bits), not player guid. Monster `Tile::queryAdd` uses `canpushcreatures`/`canpushitems` defaults **false** until §5.1 `MonsterType` flags parse; magic-field immunity is simplified to block `TILESTATE_MAGICFIELD` unless `FLAG_IGNOREFIELDDAMAGE`. *(`walk.rs`, May 2026)*

17. **Phase D.2 think cadence**: C++ `Game::checkCreatures` passes a **fixed** `EVENT_CREATURE_THINK_INTERVAL` (1000 ms) to `onThink`, not wall-clock drift. Rust mirrors this in `check_creatures`. Flat sweep over all monsters/NPCs in `SlotMap` replaces C++ bucketed `checkCreatureLists[10]` for now (100 ms stagger deferred). Phase D excludes **players** from the think sweep — player ping/idle kick stays separate; C++ runs `Player::onThink` via the same scheduler. First tick only sets `last_creature_check` baseline (no burst). Follow path refresh gating (`walk_update_ticks` / 200 ms threshold / `is_updating_path`) calls `go_to_follow_creature` (D.4). *(`creature_think.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, May 2026)*

18. **Phase D.3 spawn lifecycle**: C++ `Spawn::startup` uses `internalPlaceCreature` (no `0x6A`); respawn uses `placeCreature` → `sendAddCreature` + `CONST_ME_TELEPORT`. Rust `startup_spawns()` skips appear broadcast; `poll_spawn_respawns` emits `0x6A` per viewer with `check_creature_known`. `findPlayer` checks **spawn block tile** occupants, not zone radius; blocked respawn resets timer (`stall_respawn`). `SpawnManager` returns plans only — `GameWorld` places to avoid borrow conflicts. NPC slots track `current` but `respawns=false`. Monster `<look>` + `<flags>` parsed into `MonsterType` for spawn appearance and AI. *(`spawn.rs`, `spawn_lifecycle.rs`, `monsters.rs`, May 2026)*

19. **Monster DB lookup key is `monsters.xml` index name, not file `name` attr**: C++ `Monsters::loadMonster(file, monsterName)` registers under `monsterName` from the index (`"Red Butterfly"`) while `mType->name` comes from the file (`"Butterfly"` in `red_butterfly.xml`). Rust initially keyed by file attr → spawn `"Red Butterfly"` failed lookup. Fix: parse index `name` + `file` pairs; HashMap key = index name lowercased. *(`monsters.rs`, May 2026)*

20. **Phase D.4 / D.5 monster AI**: C++ `Monster::onThink` calls `Creature::onThink` first — `goToFollowCreature` runs **before** `searchTarget` on the same tick, so a target selected on tick N paths on tick N+1 when `is_updating_path` is set. Rust mirrors order in `monster_on_think` → `creature_on_think` then `monster_native_on_think`. `searchTarget` nearest tie-break uses **Manhattan** sum (`getDistanceX + getDistanceY`), not Chebyshev. Stock TFS idle monsters (`isIdle == true`, empty `targetList`) **do not** random-walk — `getNextStep` returns false immediately (`monster.cpp` ~1226). Active monsters **do** call `addEventWalk()` every think even with an empty queue so `Monster::getNextStep` can random-roam or accept the next flee step (`monster.cpp` ~772). *(`monster_ai.rs`, `creature_think.rs`, May 2026)*

21. **Monster target list uses `Creature::canSee`, not `canSeeCreature` alone**: C++ `Monster::updateTargetList` prunes opponents with `!canSee(creature->getPosition())` — viewport range with z offset (`Map::maxViewportX/Y` = 11), not ghost/invis-only `canSeeCreature`. Rust initially pruned with `can_see_creature` only, so once a player passed within qtree spectator range they stayed in `opponent_ids` forever and the whole cave chased off-screen. Fix: `creature_can_see(viewer_pos, target_pos, 11, 11)` in `monster_creature_visible_to`, `monster_on_creature_found`, and `monster_set_follow_creature` (mirrors `Creature::setFollowCreature`). *(`game_world.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, May 2026)*

22. **Spawn slot guard + tile path flags**: `process_spawn_request` must skip when `slot.current` is already set — otherwise a respawn tick can place a second monster while the first is still alive (orphans the old creature, looks like “respawn on walk off tile”). Failed placement should `stall_respawn` instead of leaving `current` empty with no timer (tight respawn loop). OTBM `apply_item_tile_flags` must set `TILESTATE_NOFIELDBLOCKPATH` / `IMMOVABLENOFIELDBLOCKPATH` from `blockPathFind` per `item.cpp` `hasProperty`, not only `BLOCKPATH`. *(`spawn_lifecycle.rs`, `map/mod.rs`, May 2026)*

23. **Monster move spectator fan-out**: C++ `Map::moveCreature` notifies all spectators via `onCreatureMove` (`map.cpp` ~264–323) — monsters aggro on enter, drop on leave, and re-path instantly when follow target moves (`creature.cpp` ~619–637). Rust previously deferred follow updates to 1 Hz think only. Also: qtree leaf queries return whole 32×32 leaves — must filter each candidate with `creature_can_see`; `updateTargetList` uses multifloor `getSpectators(..., true)`. Blocked path → `!hasFollowPath` → `getRandomStep` after 1 s (`monster.cpp` ~1224). Login should fan out `onCreatureEnter` to nearby monsters. *(`monster_ai.rs`, `walk.rs`, `login.rs`, May 2026)*

24. **Creature position must update per `Map::moveCreature`, not after `queryDestination` chain**: C++ sets position when `newTile.addThing(&creature)` runs, **before** `onCreatureMove` fan-out (`map.cpp` ~293–324). Rust deferred `set_position(final_pos)` until after the full `queryDestination` loop — during floor-change chains the qtree/tile were at the new z but `creature.position()` still read the old z, so `Monster::onCreatureFound` rejected the player (`creature_can_see` z-band) even when `onCreatureMove` saw `canSee(newPos)`. Fix: `set_position(to)` inside `move_creature_on_map` before `monster_dispatch_creature_move`. **Separate rule:** surface z ≤ 7 cannot see underground z ≥ 8 (`creature.cpp` ~45–56) — rats on z 8 legitimately ignore a player standing on z 7 until they step down onto z 8 (bridge/cave entrance). *(`walk.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, May 2026)*

25. **Phase D.4 gap closure — walkToSpawn, config radii, dance step**: C++ triggers `walkToSpawn` from `onCreatureLeave` when `targetList` empty and monster outside `walkToSpawnRadius` (axis box via `Position::areInRange`, default 15) — distinct from `deSpawnRadius` (Chebyshev 50) used by `isInSpawnRange`/teleport. Rust wires `monster_maybe_walk_to_spawn` from opponent removal. Config keys: `deSpawnRadius`, `deSpawnRange`, `walkToSpawnRadius`, `removeOnDespawn` → `MonsterWorldConfig` on `GameWorld`. Out-of-range despawn emits `CONST_ME_POFF` then remove or teleport per config. `getDanceStep` ported for flee/chase when follow queue drains (`monster.cpp` ~1244). `onFollowCreatureComplete` reorders `opponent_ids`. **`updateLookDirection` offset:** Rust `offset_x(monster, target)` = `target.x − monster.x` matches C++ `getOffsetX(attackedCreaturePos, pos)` because Rust helper is `to − from` while C++ is `p1 − p2`. Turn/move spectator packets require `creature_fully_sent_by_conn` entry for the wire id. *(`monster_ai.rs`, `monster_distance_step.rs`, `config.rs`, May 2026)*

26. **Phase D.5 follow repath gate**: C++ `Creature::onCreatureMove` only clears `listWalkDir` and schedules `goToFollowCreature` when `hasFollowPath` is true (`creature.cpp` ~619–643). Rust `monster_on_follow_creature_moved` must early-return when `!has_follow_path`; visibility/follow-clear still runs in the caller. `compute_look_toward_target` must use `offset_x(from, target)` not `(target, from)`. Acceptance: `monster_repaths_when_follow_target_moves`. *(`monster_ai.rs`, May 2026)*

27. **Failed monster step must not synchronously repath**: C++ `onWalk` failure only sets `forceUpdateFollowPath`; repath runs from `onThink` / 200 ms follow refresh. Rust `monster_follow_repath_now` from inside `onWalk`'s failure path caused `repath → creature_start_chase_auto_walk → onWalk → fail → repath` stack overflow (especially at login when crowded monsters block each other). Fix: keep `force_update_follow_path = true` on blocked step; remove synchronous `monster_follow_repath_now` from `walk.rs` failure arm. *(`walk.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, May 2026)*

28. **`monster_on_walk_complete` follow loop on empty A* path**: Logs showed `go_to_follow_creature` with `astar_path len=0` → `creature_start_chase_auto_walk` → `on_walk` (`pop_dir_none`) → `monster_on_walk_complete` → repeat until stack overflow (session 963225). Fix: treat empty `Some(vec![])` as C++ at-goal success (`has_follow_path=true`, no walk arm); only `None` clears path. Do not repath from `on_walk_complete`. *(`monster_ai.rs`, May 2026)*

29. **Monster circling / step-back during chase**: Mishandled empty at-goal path plus `getDanceStep` while **approaching** (`dist > target_distance`); Rust also repathed every `on_walk_complete` (C++ does not). Fix: skip dance when `dist > target_distance`; skip no-op repath at goal; empty at-goal path must call `startAutoWalk()`; never disable melee `getDanceStep`. **Dance loop stall**: after the chase queue drains, a failed dance poll set `stopped_without_reschedule` and never re-armed the walk timer — fix with `monster_should_keep_dance_walk_alive` + `schedule_walk_followup_deadline`. First sidestep after arrival may lag one walk cooldown + `staticAttackChance` roll (e.g. rat 90 → ~10% per poll) — matches C++. *(`monster_ai.rs`, `walk.rs`, May 2026)*

19. **Phase A2 version-keyed incoming opcodes**: 7.72 (`gameserver/src/protocolgame.cpp` `parsePacket` ~L466–528) and 10.98 (repo-root `src/protocolgame.cpp`) share the **same byte value** for every common client→server opcode — the only true divergences are the rule-violation flow (772 = `0x9B/0x9C/0x9D` trio; 1098 = single `0xF2`) and the era-exclusive 1098 blocks (shop `0x79–0x7C`, market `0xF4–0xF8`, quest `0xF0/0xF1`, mount `0xD4`, equip `0x77`, wrap `0x8B`, browse/seek `0xCB/0xCC`, VIP-edit `0xDE`, thank-you `0xE7`, get-object-info `0xF3`). So A2 does **not** need a full second opcode table — it needs a support predicate. Implemented `client::is_supported(op, version)` = `SUPPORTED_V1098 − V772_REMOVED + V772_ADDED`, and `parse_game_opcode` rejects opcodes the active era doesn't dispatch before matching. Server-side, only the **self-appear** opcode is genuinely version-keyed (`0x0A` vs `0x17`); exposed as `server::self_appear(version)` reading `ProtocolCaps::self_appear_opcode` (single source of truth, no second constant). All other send opcodes share bytes and differ only in **layout** — that's the codec's job (A5), not the opcode table. `ProtocolVersion` threads from `GameWireConfig` → `forward_game_packets[_xtea]` → `game_command_from_payload` → `parse_game_packet`. 1098 golden bytes unchanged. *(`protocol_opcodes.rs`, `game_parse.rs`, `protocol_game.rs`, May 2026)*

19. **Phase A3 transport caps — cipher offset, not C++ XTEA slack**: The C++ `XTEA_decrypt` length args differ per era — 1098 `xtea::decrypt(buffer, getLength() - 6, …)` (repo-root `src/protocol.cpp`), 772 `xtea::decrypt(buffer, getLength() - 4, …)` (`gameserver/src/protocol.cpp`). That `-6` vs `-4` is **not** a slack to subtract in Rust: it is a byproduct of C++ `NetworkMessage::getLength()` including the inner-size `u16` while `getBufferPosition()` already points past the (era-dependent) header. Our Rust decrypt instead decrypts the **exact** 8-byte-aligned ciphertext region (`xtea_tfs::decrypt`'s loop is `it + 8 <= length`), so the only thing that actually varies is **where the ciphertext starts**. `INITIAL_BUFFER_POSITION` = outer-size(2) + checksum(4 for 1098 / 0 for 772) + inner-size(2): IBP=8 (1098), IBP=4 (772). The framing layer strips the 2-byte outer size and the inner-size `u16` is the first decrypted word, so `cipher_off = IBP - 4` → **4 for 1098, 0 for 772** = exactly the checksum width. First attempt used `IBP - 2` and every frame failed "game body too short"; the `ProtocolCaps.xtea_length_slack` field (4/6) is therefore informational only — the real driver is `adler_checksum` / `initial_buffer_position`. 1098 output stays byte-identical (offset 4 == the old hardcoded `body[4..]`). Pre-login `0x1F` challenge gated by `caps.prelogin_challenge`; `send_game_challenge` now returns `Option<GameChallenge>` and the echo is verified only when `Some`. *(`protocol_game.rs`, `game_challenge.rs`, `server.rs`, May 2026)*

14. **772 login is account-*number* + inline credentials; no session key (A4)**: The 7.72 "account number" is `accounts.id`, not a separate column — both `loginserverAuthentication` and `gameworldAuthentication` in `gameserver/src/iologindata.cpp` query `SELECT … FROM accounts WHERE id = {accno}`. The game-port first packet (`gameserver/src/protocolgame.cpp onRecvFirstMessage`) is `skipBytes(1)` gm flag + `u32 accountNumber` + `string character` + `string password` + OTCv8 probe — no `acc\npass\ntoken\ntime` session-key string and no `0x1F` challenge echo (both 1098-only, gated by `caps.session_key_login` / `caps.prelogin_challenge`). The login-port char list (`getCharacterList`) writes per-char `name + serverName + u32 inet_addr(ip) + u16 port` and premium as a single `u16` days value (`u16::MAX` for free-premium, else `floor(secsLeft/86400)+1`); `disconnectClient` uses opcode `0x0A` vs 1098 `0x0B`. Modeled identity as `LoginIdentity::{AccountName(String), AccountNumber(u32)}` filled from `ProtocolCaps`, and split the credential parse into pure `parse_{game,login}_credentials` fns so both eras are unit-testable without a socket. `inet_addr` on a little-endian host yields octets in `a.b.c.d` order as a LE `u32`, which `add<uint32_t>` then writes verbatim — so we store `u32::from_le_bytes([a,b,c,d])`. The exact 772 first-packet *framing* offsets (proto-id byte `0x0A`/`0x01` + prelude widths) are tagged `// PROTOCOL:` and must be confirmed against a live 7.72 capture in A6. *(`game_first_packet.rs`, `protocol_login_out.rs`, `account.rs`, May 2026)*

**Phase A5/A6 — `Codec772` layouts + neutral-struct widening**: The 772 wire is mostly *narrower* than 1098, but two neutral wire structs had to **widen** (design §9.5 — never pre-narrow in core, let the codec narrow):
- `AddCreatureWire.speed_half → step_speed`: 772 `AddCreature` writes the **full** `getStepSpeed()` (`gameserver/src/protocolgame.cpp ~L2079`), 1098 writes `getStepSpeed()/2` (`src/protocolgame.cpp ~L3210`). Core (`login_out.rs`) now fills the full value; `Codec1098::write_add_creature` halves it, `Codec772` writes it raw. Keeping the half in core would have lost the low bit for 772.
- New `ContainerOpenWire` (max-width `sendContainer`). The 1098 `0x6E` carries `unlocked`/`pagination`/`u16 size`/`u16 firstIndex`; 772 (`gameserver/src/protocolgame.cpp ~L1326`) has **none** of those — just cid + item + name + `u8` cap + `u8` hasParent + `u8` count + items, count capped at capacity (no pagination). Core was building `0x6E` via the 1098-only `send_container_open` net helper with closures, which bypassed the codec seam entirely; moved it behind `encode_container_open` so the layout diverges only in the codec (helper now `#[deprecated]`).

**772 fluid is a distinct `getLiquidColor` switch, not the 10.x `FLUID_MAP`**: confirmed in `gameserver/src/tools.cpp ~L20` — e.g. fluid type `6 → 4` (772) vs `FLUID_MAP[6] = 9` (10.x). The steering explicitly warns against reusing the table; the values genuinely differ, so `Codec772` has its own `liquid_color_772` match. Never assume the fluid palette is shared.

**Packets with no era equivalent return an empty `NetworkMessage`, dropped at enqueue**: 772 has no `sendBasicData` (`0x9F` is 10.x) and no by-id tile removal (`sendRemoveTileCreature` early-returns when `stackpos >= 10` — there is no `0x6C`+`0xFFFF`+id form). Rather than make every core call site version-aware, those `Codec772` methods return `NetworkMessage::new()` and `GameWorld::enqueue_outgoing` now drops empty packets so the framing layer never emits a zero-length body (1098 never enqueues empty, so it's a no-op there). This keeps the emission call sites protocol-free (R1).

**Genuine 772 send divergences are layout, not opcode** (confirms the A2 lesson from the encoder side): every server→client opcode byte the codec emits is shared with 1098 except self-appear (`0x0A` vs `0x17`, already version-keyed via `ProtocolCaps`). `AddCreature` differs only by *omitted* fields (no creature-type/emblem/2nd-type/speech-bubble/MARK/helpers/walkthrough), `AddOutfit` by omitted addons+mount, stats by widths (`u16` cap=`free/100`, `u32` exp w/ overflow→0, no base-magic/stamina/speed block), skills by widths (`u8`/`u8`% × 7), containers by omitted pagination block, `0x70` by omitted slot index, `0x71`/`0x72` by `u8` vs `u16` slot. All confined to `codec/v772.rs`.

**Deferred (not blocking A5/A6, tagged in the plan)**: (1) `sendIcons 0xA2` is `u8` in 772 vs `u16` in 1098, but it's built by the standalone `outgoing_extra::send_icons` raw helper, not a `ProtocolCodec` method — so it is **not** yet caps-gated; needs to become a codec method before a live 772 client. (2) TVP gates an extra `stackpos` byte on `0x6A` behind `CLIENTOS_OTCLIENT_LINUX` — omitted for the canonical 7.72 client (flagged like OTCv8 quirks for 1098); confirm against a capture before supporting OTClient-on-772. (3) The 1098 login burst still enqueues OTCv8-only preamble (`0x43` features + extended-opcode init) unconditionally — must be caps-gated before a live 772 login. (4) Live 7.72 client smoke test is blocked on 772 content (`items.otb`/`.spr`/`.dat`/OTBM) — wire layouts are frozen as golden tests vs `gameserver/src/` until then. *(`codec/v772.rs`, `codec/wire.rs`, `codec/mod.rs`, `container_ui.rs`, `login_out.rs`, `game_world.rs`, June 2026)*


30. **Track B mechanics profile (B0–B5) — shared code, era-tuned data, two-tier Lua**: Mechanics divergence between 772 and 1098 is modeled exactly like the wire `ProtocolCaps`: a `Copy` `MechanicsProfile` (Tier-1 scalars/enums) + a `FormulaHooks` registry (Tier-2 optional Lua fns), both on `GameWorld.mechanics`, game-thread only. Loaded from `data/formulas/<clientVersion>.lua` at startup (`load_mechanics`); a missing/partial file overlays onto `MechanicsProfile::for_version` defaults so any unset key keeps its era default (R11 — never a bare literal in a Rust formula). Key findings:
    - **Walk quantizer is profile-driven (B1):** TFS `getStepDuration` and CipSoft/TVP walk delay share the speed→raw-duration math; they differ *only* in the final `ceil` step. `walk.rs` quantizes by **`profile.step_beat_ms`** (50 ms for both shipped eras — TVP `gameserver` authority for 772). Do **not** use **`beat_ms`** (772 main-loop timer, 200 ms) for step duration — that field drives `run_game_loop` only (the single unified beat loop; was `run_game_loop_772` before Phase 7). Do **not** swap in 772 decompile's linear `GetSpeed = wp*1000/speed` for the TFS log curve on 1098; on 772 use `StepSpeedModel::LinearGo` + `step_beat_ms` quantizer per `MechanicsProfile`.
    - **Path cost is current-tile-weighted, not edge-weighted (B2):** CipSoft `TShortway::Expand` (`cract.cc:136–155`) adds `Node->Waypoints` (the **current** node's terrain weight) per step, `*3` for diagonal — *not* the destination's. `get_path_matching` gained a `cost_model` + `ground_cost(pos)` closure; `path_step_cost(Fixed)` keeps TFS 10/25 byte-identical, `path_step_cost(TerrainWeighted)` uses `ground_cost(current)` × (3 if diagonal). 1098 routes unchanged.
    - **Level-exp polynomial is identical across eras (B4.5):** TFS `Player::getExpForLevel` (`player.h:171`) is literally `(((L-6)*L+17)*L-12)/6 * 100` — the *same* polynomial as CipSoft `TSkillLevel::GetExpForLevel` (`crskill.cc:352`) with `Delta=100`. My first cubic `(50L³−150L²+400L)/3` form was wrong (gave lvl-2 = 0 instead of 100). Both `LevelExpModel` variants now share the polynomial; only `level_exp_delta` differs (both 100 for the level curve; CipSoft varies Delta per *skill*).
    - **CipSoft damage/armor are integer-floor (B4.2/B4.3):** `ProbeValue = ((rand%100 + rand%100)/2) * (attack*(skill*5+50)) / 10000` (`crskill.cc:535`), bounded by `Max/100` (skill 10/attack 50 ⇒ [0,49]). Fight modes are integer `±(v*2)/10` / `±(v*4)/10` (`crcombat.cc:222`) — modeled as `f64` multipliers (1.2/0.6/0.6/1.8 for 772; 1.2/0.8/0.8/1.2 for 1098) then floored. Randomized armor `(A/2)+rand%(A/2)` only when `A>=2` (`crcombat.cc:303`).
    - **B4 is math-only on a skeleton:** combat execution (`combat/mod.rs`) and condition ticking (Phase G) are still stubs, so B4 builds the formulas as pure, unit-tested, Tier-2-hookable fns in `combat/math.rs` (+ `condition::dot_tick_for_condition`, `spell::spell_damage_scaled` seams) ready to be called when the loops land. Every fn checks its Tier-2 hook first (native fast path when unregistered = zero cost).
    - **Tier-2 hooks own their VM:** `FormulaHooks` holds the formulas `Lua` + cached `mlua::Function`s; `!Send`, game-thread only, populated only for globals the script actually defines. Unset hook = `None` = native default. 1098 outcomes verified unchanged (171 core tests + net/common goldens green). *(`formulas.rs`, `walk.rs`, `pathfinding.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, `spawn_lifecycle.rs`, `combat/math.rs`, `condition.rs`, `spell.rs`, `data/formulas/{772,1098}.lua`, June 2026)*


32. **Login delay (~5 s) — lazy full-map qtree build, not codecs or map burst**: `collect_spatial_spectators` used `map.qtree_mut(z)` for every floor in the multifloor Z span (e.g. z=7 → 0..=9). `qtree_mut` calls `QTreeNode::build(0,0,width,height)` on first touch per floor — seconds on Forgotten-sized maps when most floors have no creatures yet. Fix: only query `qtrees.get_mut(&z)` when that floor already has an index (creatures registered via `register_creature_index`). Also match C++ `Monster::onCreatureFound`: do **not** call `searchTarget`/`setFollowCreature` on enter — only add friend/opponent lists + `updateIdleStatus`; chase stays in `onThink`/move handlers. *(`monster_ai.rs`, `map/mod.rs`, June 2026)*

31. **772 content gate — `items.otb` is OTB major 2 / minor 7, not major 3 (B5 / A6.4 content track)**: Booting with `clientVersion = 772` + a 7.72 `items.otb` failed at content load: `majorVersion must be 3, got 2`. The Rust OTB loader (`crates/tfs-rust-content/src/otb.rs` `validate_items_otb_root_version`) hardcoded the 10.98 gate (major 3 / minor ≥ `CLIENT_VERSION_1098` = 57), but a 7.72 OTB is **major 2 / minor `CLIENT_VERSION_800` = 7** (`gameserver/src/items.cpp` `Items::loadFromOtb`: `majorVersion != 2` and `minorVersion != CLIENT_VERSION_800` both reject). Each C++ tree only accepts *its own* version; the Rust binary serves **both** eras by config, and the OTB file is self-describing, so the fix accepts either era's pair: `(major 3 && minor>=57)` OR `(major 2 && minor==7)`. The item-node `itemattrib_t` attribute IDs are the *same* auto-incremented enum in both `itemloader.h`, so the existing `apply_attr` parse path is era-agnostic — only the root version gate needed widening. The two `otb::tests` (`repo_items_otb_passes_root_validation_and_loads`, `ground_items_have_otb_speed`) are version-agnostic (just load the repo's `items.otb` and assert non-trivial) and started passing once the gate accepted v2. Remaining 772 content prerequisites (`.spr`/`.dat` signatures, OTBM client ids) are still per design §11 — the OTB gate was the first blocker. *(`crates/tfs-rust-content/src/otb.rs`, June 2026)*

33. **772 formulas defaults should mirror shipped Lua policy, not historical hardcodes**: If `data/formulas/772.lua` is changed to `distanceKeep = "perType"` and `attackSpeedMs = 0`, keep `MechanicsProfile::for_version(772)` aligned or fallback/missing-file behavior silently diverges from the deployed formulas file. Also, the right place to expose "actual formulas" for tuning is Tier-2 Lua hooks (`getWeaponDamage`, `getArmorReduction`, `getDefense`, `getSpellDamage`, etc.) as documented templates in `data/formulas/772.lua`; Tier-1 selector keys (`armor`, `damageFormula`, etc.) stay model toggles. *(`data/formulas/772.lua`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/formulas.rs`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/combat/math.rs`, June 2026)*

34. **IdleStimulus Phase A (772) — drain-triggered idle, not think arming**: On `beat_driven_loop`, monster chase/repath/lose-target runs from `idle_stimulus` when the per-creature `CreatureTodo` action queue drains after `Execute` (`CreatureAction::Go`), not from ~1 Hz `monster_native_on_think`. Think keeps list hygiene + spawn range only. Event target enter stays immediate; follow repath on 772 defers via `request_idle_stimulus` (`force_update_follow_path`). Players on 772 still use direct `process_creature_todo` → `on_walk` (no action queue). Step chain: Go execute → `finish_creature_todo_execute` re-enqueues Go or calls idle. P7 `walk_timer_idle` gates duplicate heap entries. Phase B still needed for `Attack` actions + retiring think `creature_on_attacking`. *(`creature_todo.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `walk/mod.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, `creature_think.rs`, June 2026)*

35. **Phase P1 (772) — Asynchronous chase walk routing via ToDo action queue**: Under `beat_driven_loop = true` (772 mechanics), when a monster starts a chase walk (e.g. from `monster_try_apply_chase_path` or follow step triggers), it must NOT execute synchronously or use the 1098 direct auto-walk scheduling model. Instead, it must route through `monster_start_chase_walk` to enqueue a `Go` action (`CreatureAction::Go`) in its `todo.queue` and schedule a wakeup. Furthermore, if a monster stands still (no next step available in `on_walk`), calling `request_idle_stimulus` immediately would cause a 1ms wakeup loop if `walk_delay` is 0. Instead, it must call `schedule_walk_followup_deadline` to wait for a full step duration, preventing spin loops and matching the correct 772 retry cadence. *(`monster_ai.rs`, `walk/mod.rs`, June 2026)*

37. **P1 chase repath cadence (772) — idle-only replan**: Do not call TFS `monster_ensure_follow_band` from 772 `monster_idle_stimulus`. Repath via `monster_idle_chase_needs_repath`: `idle_drain` (segment batch done), `off_band` (empty queue + left follow band), `force_update` (blocked step). Target-move interrupts only when a **non-empty** `walk_queue` is stale (`monster_chase_queue_stale`); empty queue defers to idle — not `force_update` every kite tile. `followRepathWithoutPath` gates 1098 target-move only. *(`idle_stimulus.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, `monster_events.rs`, June 2026)*

36. **Phase P2 (772) — Fleeing and keep-distance (band) movement decision parity**: Under `beat_driven_loop = true` (772 mechanics), a monster’s walk decision must follow a strict CipSoft three-way branch: too close (`dist < target_distance`) or fleeing triggers a flight step using `search_flight_field`; at-band (`dist == target_distance`) triggers a cardinal lateral dance (melee or keep-distance) or standing still; too far (`dist > target_distance`) returns `None` so that the idle stimulus can handle repathing. Unusable attack states must not clamp the keep-distance band to 1 (which is TFS 1098 behavior). Fleeing pathfinding failures when blocked must early-return inside `go_to_follow_creature` immediately to prevent falling back to closer steps. *(`monster_ai.rs`, `monster_distance_step.rs`, June 2026)*

38. **Chase-path P2 (772) — roam branch + force_update hygiene**: Reference `IdleStimulus` logs `roam` when an active non-idle monster has no `followCreature`; Rust mirrors via `monster_idle_prepare_and_enqueue_go` → `log_branch("roam")` + `todo_go` `via:"roam"` before enqueueing `Go` (direction still chosen in `getRandomStep` at execute). Blocked **non-chase** steps must not set `force_update_follow_path` (roam/dance with no follow). When `force_update` is set but the monster is already at the follow band (`monster_at_follow_goal`), clear the flag and skip idle repath — avoids inflated `branch/go_exec` and extra diagonal shortways from failed adjacent dance steps. *(`idle_stimulus.rs`, `creature_todo.rs`, `walk/mod.rs`, June 2026)*

39. **772 TShortway diagonals — use linked-list expand, not BinaryHeap**: Live snake replay showed ~5% diagonal `go_exec` (0% reference) while `scripts/compare_chase_pathfinding.py` reported 5/5 exact on synthetic grids. Root cause: terrain-weighted reverse chase used `BinaryHeap` open set; reference `TShortway` uses heuristic-sorted linked list (`cract.cc`, `compare_chase_pathfinding.py`). Equal-cost ties + expand order favored diagonals on live mixed-terrain tiles. Fix: `path_matching_tshortway` + `TShortwaySearch` linked-list port; cardinal wins on equal `waylength` when replacing a diagonal parent (`tshortway_should_relax`). Rebuild `path_compare` before compare script. Synthetic harness: 5/5 exact dirs + diagonal counts after fix. *(`pathfinding.rs`, June 2026)*

40. **Monster AI A0 (772) — path failure is NOWAY→roam, not TFS greedy step**: Reference `TShortway` fail in `ToDoGo` throws `NOWAY` (`cract.cc:1068`); idle catch clears `Target` and falls through to roam (`crnonpl.cc:2813`). Rust must not call `monster_try_any_closer_step` on 772. Use `monster_idle_chase_repath` from idle only; on non-flee fail return `Noway`, `clear_targets()`, enqueue roam Go same tick. Keep-distance at-goal and `full_path_search` use cheb vs per-type `target_distance` from monsters.xml — not TFS `canUseAttack` (which falsely rejects hunters at dist 4 with no spell in range). `go_to_follow_creature` stays 1098-only. *(`monster_ai.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, June 2026)*

41. **Monster AI A1 (772) — idle walk dispatch is a priority branch tree, not a single repath hub**: Reference `TMonster::IdleStimulus` walking (`crnonpl.cc:2676`) orders flee (`SearchFlightField`) → master follow → melee/dist arms → roam; `TShortway` runs only from chase arms. Rust mirrors via `MonsterIdleWalkBranch` classifier + per-arm executor in `idle_stimulus.rs`. Flee/dist-flee use `monster_idle_flee_step` (one tile, no shortway batch). Melee/dist chase call `monster_idle_chase_repath` only when `monster_idle_chase_needs_repath`. Melee vs ranged split proxies `!DistanceFighting || !ThrowPossible` as `target_distance <= 1 || !canUseAttack`. Branch debug logs belong on the executor, not inside `monster_idle_chase_repath`. *(`idle_stimulus.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, June 2026)*

42. **Monster AI A2 (772) — melee-adjacent chase step budget is branch+dist keyed, not global `CHASE_PATH_MAX_STEPS`**: At cheb==2 on the melee chase arm, reference replay shows mostly one-hop shortway batches (not 3-step). Rust uses `monster_idle_chase_step_budget(is_melee_chase, cheb)` → `(1, true)` for `MeleeChase` at cheb==2; farther melee / dist / master keep `(3, false)` until A3/A5. Thread `max_steps`/`must_reach` through `monster_idle_chase_repath` → `truncate_tshortway_go_queue`. On 772, gate off `monster_should_keep_dance_walk_alive` (X5) — idle drain owns dance pacing, not TFS `getNextStep` poll. *(`monster_ai.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `walk/mod.rs`, June 2026)*
43. **Monster AI A3 (772) — dist_chase batch size is `cheb - target_distance`, not hardcoded 4 or global 3**: Reference `ToDoGo(..., max: Distance - keepDist)` uses literal 4 only because classic distance fighters keep cheb 4; Rust reads per-type `target_distance` from `monsters.xml` via `monster_effective_target_distance`. `monster_idle_chase_step_budget(false, true, cheb, band)` → `(cheb - band, false)` (can be **0** at exact band). `truncate_tshortway_go_queue` stops only at `cheb≤1` unless `MustReach` — dist keep-band is **MaxSteps only**, not a trim stop. *(`monster_ai.rs`, `pathfinding.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, June 2026; TW-1 fix July 2026)*

44. **Monster AI A4/A5 (772) — `getNextStep` must not inline flee/dance on beat loop; master follow has Manhattan wait band**: Deleting the inline 772 flee/dance block in `monster_next_walk_step` without an early `return None` would fall through to 1098 `get_dance_step` (new leak). Replace with `if beat_driven_loop { return None; }` — idle drain re-arms via `request_idle_stimulus`. Master follow: Manhattan 2–3 hold (`monster_master_follow_in_wait_band`); `ToDoWait(1000)` deferred to B2; beyond band `ToDoGo(max:3)` via `monster_idle_chase_repath`. *(`monster_ai.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, June 2026)*

45. **Monster AI Phase B (772) — idle pacing belongs in `CreatureAction::Wait`, not `last_step` poll**: Reference enqueues `ToDoWait(1000)` after roam, dist_dance, dist_flee fail, and master Manhattan 2–3 (`crnonpl.cc`). Rust mirrors via `idle_enqueue_paced_go` / `idle_enqueue_wait_and_start`; `finish_creature_todo_execute` chains Go→Wait in one drain tick. Roam step selection moves to `monster_idle_roam_step` in idle — delete 772 `get_random_step` + `last_step` gate from `monster_next_walk_step` (X6). `CreatureAction::Attack` stub enqueues from idle combat tail when walk queue empty; ~1 Hz think `creature_on_attacking` gated off monsters on `beat_driven_loop`. *(`creature_todo.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, `creature_think.rs`, June 2026)*

46. **772 PANIC posture ≠ `IsFleeing()`**: E5 initially gated `is_fleeing()` on `MonsterState::Panic`, producing flee arms in the panic sim. Reference `TMonster::IsFleeing()` (`crnonpl.cc:3136`) is **HP ≤ FleeThreshold only**; PANIC at cheb=1 still **melee_dances**, then promotes **PANIC → ATTACKING** after a successful sidestep (`crnonpl.cc:2830`). Do not conflate damage-stimulus posture with HP flee. *(`creature/monster.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, §20 sim, June 2026)*

47. **Chase harness appear `ToDoYield` vs multi-monster**: Calling `creature_todo_yield` inside per-monster `kite_monster_appear` silences JSONL for 4-monster cyclops (0 events). Keep appear as target acquire + `add_creature_think_check`; batch appear yield needs separate investigation. Dist-chase `request_idle_stimulus` on follow-target move is safe. *(`sim_harness.rs`, `chase_kite_sim.rs`, June 2026)*

48. **772 dance `%5` dir table — N/S indices swapped**: C++ melee/dist dance (`crnonpl.cc:2814-2819`) maps case 2→North (`DestY-=1`), case 3→South (`DestY+=1`). Rust `DANCE_DIR_ORDER` had indices 2/3 as S/N — mirror-image dest on stand/panic. Fix: `[West, East, North, South, None]`. Dist dance uses same `%5` table. *(`sim_glibc_rand.rs`, §21 sim, June 2026)*

49. **Kill E6 — log stimulus damage before HP clamp**: C++ `DamageStimulus` logs post-armor computed damage (24) before HP subtraction; Rust was logging actual HP dealt (20). Fix: armor subtract in `sim_player_damage_monster` + emit stimulus in `combat_execute_with_stimulus` before apply. *(`idle_stimulus.rs`, `sim_harness.rs`, June 2026)*

50. **E6 sleeping harness — preserve posture until damage**: Explicit `monster_state sleeping` in scenario must not wake on appear/found/follow (`harness_preserve_sleep`); only first damage promotes `sleeping→under_attack`. Broad `MonsterState::Sleeping` skip broke stand chase wake — scope to harness flag only. *(`monster.rs`, `monster_events.rs`, `monster_targets.rs`, June 2026)*

51. **Chase apply — do not reverse after truncate**: `truncate_tshortway_go_queue` returns steps in execution order; an extra `steps.reverse()` after truncate inverted multi-step paths. *(`monster_ai.rs`, §21 sim, June 2026)*

52. **Batch appear defer — tick=0 dance regression**: `kite_monsters_appear_batch` + deferred idle caused cyclops `melee_dance`×2 at tick=0 and 0 `shortway` events. Revert to per-monster appear; batch infrastructure kept for future safe yield pass. *(`chase_kite_sim.rs`, `game_world.rs`, June 2026)*

53. **Phase 1 appear defer + hero `Defend`**: Stand/panic tick shift was inline idle during appear target acquire — fix with `kite_monsters_appear_batch` + `harness_defer_appear_idle` + `HARNESS_APPEAR_IDLE_DEFER_MS=2000` before first idle. Melee damage parity required `sim_melee_defense=5` from `human.mon` (not 2) and `skills.fist` in defense probe (`GetDefendValue` / `WEAPON_NONE` → `SKILL_FIST`). PANIC→ATTACKING on first melee hit closes `combat_state[1]` vs ref. *(`sim_harness.rs`, `monster_combat.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, §22 sim, June 2026)*

54. **Phase 2 cyclops defer — clear on deferred idle, not Wait drain**: Extending P1 defer through five kite teleports @ms=0 requires `request_idle_stimulus` to no-op while `harness_defer_appear_idle` is set; clear flag at start of deferred `monster_idle_stimulus`, not when appear-step `Wait(0)` drains. *(`idle_stimulus.rs`, `sim_harness.rs`, §23, June 2026)*

55. **TShortway chase path — no goal-band trim; walk_queue is LIFO**: `trim_path_to_goal_band` before truncate inverted paths when blocked creature tiles existed on the map (walkable ground, fill `Waypoints=-1`). C++ `Calculate` walks predecessor chain only. `listWalkDir` pops from the **back** — chase steps must be `push_back` in reverse execution order or `go_exec` inverts (shortway logged E, walk ran S). *(`pathfinding.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, `path_compare.rs`, §23, June 2026)*

56. **TShortway expand — relax `Waypoints==-1` cells**: Rust incorrectly skipped all relaxation when `neighbor_wp <= 0`; C++ still sets `Waylength`/`Predecessor` on `-1` tiles but does not enqueue them. *(`pathfinding.rs`, `cract.cc:158-202`, June 2026)*

57. **TShortway heuristic must be signed `int`**: Rust used `u32` + `waylength.max(0)` for expand-list sort; C++ `TShortwayPoint::Heuristic` is signed and may go negative when `Waylength` is negative during dest-seed expand (`cract.cc:181-184`). Clamp corrupts expand order. *(`pathfinding.rs`, June 2026)*

58. **NPC Phase 2 — `Npc->Data` threading exposes `Player:addItem` sub_type misuse**: When wiring `Npc->Data` through `Create`/`Delete`/`Count(...)`, `lua_script_player_add_item_full` was setting `new_item.count = sub_type` for every non-stackable item. That is correct only for charge-bearing / single-value items; for **fluid containers** the value is `fluid_type`, and for **keys** it is the key number (`action_id`). Fix: branch on `ItemType::is_fluid_container` / `is_splash` / `is_key` and call `set_fluid_type` / `set_action_id`; for charge-bearing types use `set_charges`; otherwise fall back to `count`. Crucially, the **client-visible wire count byte for fluids and runes is `Item::count`**, not the `fluid_type`/`charges` attribute, so `count` must be updated together with the attribute (TFS `Item::setFluidType`/`setCharges` both assign `count`). Buying a mana fluid with `Data=10` now produces `count=10` on the wire instead of a water-like `count=1`. `NpcActionHost::create_item`/`delete_item` now take `data`, but the host only passes it as a real subtype for items that have one; everything else uses `-1` so `removeItemOfType`/`addItem` do not accidentally filter stackable coins by `sub_type == 0`. C++ `CountObjects(Object, Type, Value)` checks `Value` only for `LIQUIDCONTAINER` and `KEY` and does **not** propagate `Value` into nested containers (passes `0`), so the NPC `Count(...)` closure mirrors that quirk rather than using the generic TFS count. *(`npc/host.rs`, `player/inventory/util.rs`, `game_world_inventory.rs`, `npc/focus.rs`, July 2026)*

58. **Synthetic arena ground = grass TypeID 102, not wp literal 150**: `lay_synthetic_arena` passed `default_wp` as OTB item id (gravel 150) instead of C++ `SyntheticGroundType` grass 102 (`chase_kite_scenario.cc:113-121`). *(`sim_harness.rs`, June 2026)*

59. **Cyclops NW/far-N live-ref paths vs `cract.cc` TShortway**: Faithful Python port (`compare_chase_pathfinding.py`) with uniform wp=150 + sibling `MovePossible` blocks yields NW **south** `(32359,32290)…` and far-N **east** `(32360,32288)…` — matching Rust. Live C++ JSONL wants NW diagonal + far-N north — **not** reachable under faithful reverse expand with quad sibling blocks alone. Brute-force static blocks need ≥4 extra tiles for NW diagonal. *(`tasks/todo.md` P2.5, `docs/TFS-RUST_772_Sim_Divergence_Report.md` §24, June 2026)*

60. **Cyclops quad brute-force FillMap blocks (June 2026)**: Over live sibling occupancy (far-N/E/S + player dest), NW diagonal ref `[(32358,32290),(32358,32291),(32359,32291)]` needs **four** extra static blocks minimum: `(32358,32289),(32359,32290),(32360,32289)` plus one east-corridor tile — not explained by unpushable cyclops on map at tick=2000. far-N north ref `[(32359,32287),(32359,32286),(32358,32286)]` has **no** solution under creature-base blocks + targeted east/west blocks; blocking `(32360,32288)` yields west path instead. East+South monsters lockstep 2/2. **Fresh oracle (June 2026):** `run_kite_scenario.py --synthetic` after freeing 7172 reproduces **identical** C++ paths — log was not stale; Rust must match live `MovePossible`/`FillMap`, not dismiss ref. *(`log/chase_path_cip_cyclops.log`, `cyclops_*_shortway_matches_python_tshortway_port`)*

61. **P2.5 FillMap root cause — OTBM items under synthetic grass**: C++ `LaySyntheticArena` replaces BANK only; live `(32359,32290)` keeps OTBM **3682** (`Unpass` fir tree) under grass 102. Rust `lay_synthetic_arena` wiped items → fill too permissive on priority cardinals. Fix: `overlay_synthetic_ground_in_arena` + `beat_driven_world_for_kite_synthetic`. Priority tiles match; **shortway 4/4** lockstep. *(`sim_harness.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, June 2026)*

62. **P2.5e — first `Go` uses `ToDoStart` min-1ms, not `get_event_step_ticks`**: Pre-first-step Rust `todo_start_go_delay` scheduled full upcoming diagonal duration (~2000ms+) → `next_wakeup@4001`, missing `go_exec` @4000. C++ `CalculateDelay(TDGo)` is 0 when `EarliestWalkTime=0`; `ToDoStart` floors delay to 1 ms (`NextWakeup=2001`). Fix: `earliest_walk_server_ms` set in `NotifyGo` (`on_walk`); 772 beat path arms `max(1, earliest - server_ms)` only. Appear defer must schedule **remaining** ms to 2000, not always +2000 (late `Wait` drain was pushing idle to 4000). *(`walk/mod.rs`, `creature/base.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, §25.4, June 2026)*

63. **FillMap + MovePossible stack parity (June 2026)**: 772 `GetFirstObject` order = `ground` → `down_items` bottom→top (index 0 = top) → `top_items` → creatures (tail approximation). `TShortway::FillMap` reads **stack-head** BANK `WAYPOINTS` from OTB `ITEM_ATTR_SPEED` only (`0` → invalid); `MinWaypoints` seeds at **1000**. `MovePossible(Execute=false)` walks the same chain: `UNPASS` blocks unless `!UNMOVE && can_push_items` (`CanKickBoxes`); `AVOID` magic fields respect `Panic` + `immunity_poison`. Runtime flags from patched OTB/XML only — no `objects.srv` on hot path. *(`tile.rs`, `items.rs`, `pathfinding.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, `docs/772_OTB_OBJECTS_SRV_FLAG_MAPPING.md`)*

64. **P2.5g — equal-key `ToDoQueue` tie is path-dependent, not one global spawn order**: Reusing `u16::MAX - harness_spawn_order` for every wakeup fixed appear-idle LIFO @2000 but inverted east/far-N @4000 (`go_exec` 2/4). C++ `priority_queue` compares Key only; harness needs explicit secondary ties: `HarnessAppearIdle` (LIFO appear) vs `HarnessGoStep` (NW,S,far-N,E oracle). *(`todo_queue.rs`, `walk/mod.rs`, `creature_todo.rs`, §26, June 2026)*

65. **Phase 3 kite — `CreatureMoveStimulus` requires `LockToDo`**: Rust gated **out** when `todo.locked`; C++ gates **in** (`crmain.cc:928-931`). Harness `player_pos` follow-move was firing close-chase on every kite teleport. Pair with drain-before-teleport on `advance_ms`+`player_pos` steps so idle commits at the pre-kite player tile. *(`monster_events.rs`, `chase_kite_sim.rs`, §27, June 2026)*

66. **X1 dist_chase cadence — never execute freshly queued `Go` in the same idle drain when a future wakeup is armed**: In 772 `process_creature_todo`, after `idle_stimulus` enqueues `Go`, running `run_monster_todo_execute` unconditionally can fire `go_exec` at the current tick (hunter @2000) instead of waiting for `ToDoStart` (`next_wakeup`). If front action is `Go` and `next_wakeup > server_ms`, return and let `DrainTodoQueue` execute on the scheduled wakeup. This restores one-beat defer behavior (`cract.cc` `IdleStimulus`→`ToDoStart` contract). *(`walk/mod.rs`, §28 X1, June 2026)*
67. **X2 `todo_go` parity lives in the trace contract, not the queued path itself**: In `hunter_dist_flee`, Rust already had the correct two-step shortway path (`shortway.max=2`, two queued positions), but `idle_enqueue_paced_go` always logged follow-target `todo_go.max` as global `CHASE_PATH_MAX_STEPS` and also mislabeled flee as a generic follow chase. For 772 parity, `todo_go` must derive its contract from the actual idle arm: dist-chase logs `monster_idle_chase_step_budget(cheb, target_distance)`, while `idle_flee` mirrors `SearchFlightField` → `ToDoGo(must=true, max=INT_MAX)` on the one-step destination. *(`creature_todo.rs`, June 2026)*


31. **772 push/kick is `MovePossible(Execute)` side-effects, split across the move boundary**: CipSoft folds creature/box kicking *into* `TMonster::MovePossible(x,y,z,Execute=true)` (`crnonpl.cc:2225-2287`) — the same call that validates the tile. The Rust port splits this: `monster_push_before_step` (`monster_push.rs`) runs the kick side-effects, then `internal_move_creature_step` re-validates and moves (kick-and-retry-same-tile is implicit — the kick clears the tile, the move then succeeds). The C++ `throw EXHAUSTED` (stepping onto a **player** tile → `Target=0`; or a `KickCreature` that has to **kill** because no adjacent tile is free) cannot be a Rust panic/Result across that boundary, so `monster_push_before_step` returns `MonsterKickOutcome::{Proceed,Exhausted}`. On `Exhausted` the caller skips the step and runs `monster_exhausted_wait_772` = the `IdleStimulus` catch (`crnonpl.cc:2890-2898`): `clear_targets() + ToDoClear + Wait(1000) + ToDoStart`. This is the audit's Finding 7 "stop for a second" — it must fire **only** on genuine EXHAUSTED, not on every blocked step (a normal blocked step still clears-queue+replans on the same beat). `CanKickBoxes()` inherits from a monster master (`crnonpl.cc:2984-2992`) — walk the master chain (bounded) not just the race flag. `KickBoxes`/`KickCreature` use a **fixed** N,S,W,E offset order (`{0,0,-1,1}/{-1,1,0,0}`) with **no RNG** — replacing the wrong-era `rand::thread_rng().shuffle` (audit Findings 6/11). **Kick-kill is full parity** (`crnonpl.cc:3074-3080`): a boxed-in pushable monster is killed via `combat::execute(Some(kicker), …, -fullHP)` → records the kicker in the victim's `damage_map` → `apply_creature_death` (corpse + loot + experience to the kicker) — **not** a bare `remove_creature` (which would skip kill credit / loot / exp). `Kill()` does not re-run `DamageStimulus`, so call `combat::execute` + `apply_creature_death` directly rather than `combat_execute_with_stimulus`. Box-move parity needs real item data (synthetic test world has an empty `items_db` so `is_unpass_772` is always false) — unit-test the gate logic + `CanKickBoxes` + the boxed-in kill, validate actual box moves via the real-map battery / `fillmap_movepossible_blocks_unpass_under_grass`. *(`monster_push.rs`, `walk/mod.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, audit Phase 1, June 2026)*


68. **772 sight is `ThrowPossible` (major-axis interp + `UNTHROW`), not Bresenham `isSightClear`**: CipSoft 772 LOS (`info.cc:1154`) walks the line by **major-axis linear interpolation** — `Cur = (Orig*(MaxT-T) + Dest*T)/MaxT` for `T in StartT..=MaxT`, `MaxT = max(|dx|,|dy|)` — a different tile set than 1098 Bresenham on diagonals, and it tests **only** the `UNTHROW` (projectile-block) flag, never `UNPASS`/`BLOCKSOLID`. So a solid-but-throwable tile (e.g. a parcel) blocks 1098 sight but **not** 772 sight (audit Finding 16b). Ported as `Map::throw_possible(orig,dest,power)` with a new `tile::flags::UNTHROW` bit aggregated from `block_projectile()` (the existing `BLOCK_PROJECTILE` alias was wrongly aliased to `BLOCKPATH`/`block_path_find` — left alone for 1098, not reused for 772). All monster/combat callers use `power = 0` (`crnonpl.cc:2798`), so `MinZ` resolves to the origin floor; multi-floor `MinZ` stepping and the `HOOKEAST`/`HOOKSOUTH` `StartT=0` origin case (new hook flags from OTB `is_hangable()` + `is_horizontal/vertical()`) are ported for completeness. Route via `GameWorld::monster_sight_clear` (772 → `throw_possible`, 1098 → `is_sight_clear`) — a single dispatcher so call sites stay era-agnostic; this gates the distance-vs-melee branch (`monster_idle_uses_dist_branch`) and ranged/spell opportunity, so getting it wrong flips archers between kiting and closing. Empty/void tiles do **not** block throw (C++ `CoordinateFlag(UNTHROW)` on no-object = false) — unlike `blocks_sight` which treats no-tile as blocked. *(`map/los.rs`, `map/mod.rs`, `tile.rs`, `monster_targets.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `creature_think.rs`, audit Phase 2, June 2026)*

69. **772 chase leash is exempt while ATTACKING/PANIC; roam uses the per-home `Radius`, not the despawn radius**: CipSoft `TMonster::MovePossible` (`crnonpl.cc:2148-2167`) applies the home-radius bound **only when `State ∉ {ATTACKING, PANIC}`** — a chasing/panicking monster follows its target out of range and is despawned later by the IdleStimulus out-of-range check (`crnonpl.cc:2407`), not pinned at an invisible boundary (audit Finding 17). The roam (non-attacking) bound is the monster's own `TMonsterhome::Radius` (`MonsterhomeInRange`, axis-box `|dx|<=R && |dy|<=R`, `|z-homez|<=2`), which equals `chebyshev <= R` — **not** the large global `deSpawnRadius` (50). Added a `home_radius` field on `Monster` (set from the spawn zone `radius` in `spawn_monster`); `monster_roam_leash_radius` returns it for 772 when `>0`, else falls back to `deSpawnRadius` (so synthetic/test monsters and 1098 are unchanged — `home_radius` defaults 0). Applied in both leash gates: `monster_can_occupy_chase_tile` (roam/dance/dist steps) and `monster_tshortway_fill_walkable` (chase pathfinding). Despawn still flows through the existing `monster_handle_out_of_spawn_range` (deSpawnRadius). *(`monster_ai.rs`, `creature/monster.rs`, `spawn_lifecycle.rs`, audit Phase 3, June 2026)*

70. **Idle/dance unit tests are non-deterministic without `TFS_SIM_SEED`**: `GameWorld::ai_rng` defaults to `StdRng::from_entropy()` and is only reseeded by `init_sim_rng_from_env` when `TFS_SIM_SEED` is in the env. Plain `cargo test` (no seed) means `sim_glibc_rng_enabled() == false`, so `sim_dance_choice`, roam, flee shuffle, and spell-damage variation fall back to the entropy-seeded `ai_rng` → tests like `test_772_dist_dance_enqueues_go_and_wait`, `test_e4_spell_delay_gate`, `test_e4_cobra_poison_at_range`, `test_772_dist_target_flee_inline_chase_after_goal_wait` **pass/fail at random per run** (verified flaky both ways in isolation). This is the live-side of audit Findings 8/15 and is fixed by Phase 5 (RNG unification / per-world glibc generator). When verifying movement/AI changes, rely on deterministic tests and the seeded sim harness battery — do **not** treat these entropy-seeded tests as a regression signal. *(observed during Phase 2/3, June 2026)*


71. **772 casting has no `break` — every gated spell casts, and every spell rolls its delay**: CipSoft CASTING (`crnonpl.cc:2521-2667`) loops all spells with no early exit; a multi-spell monster fires several impacts per idle and draws a `rand()%Delay` gate for *every* spell every idle. The Rust `monster_idle_try_casting` `if cast_any { break; }` capped it at one cast/idle **and** skipped the remaining offensive spells' `parity_rand_mod(delay)` draws, desyncing the glibc stream for that creature's life. Fix: remove the break. Single-spell monsters (most fixtures) are unaffected; multi-spell monsters re-baseline their RNG trace. *(`idle_stimulus.rs`, audit Phase 4 Finding 2, June 2026)*

72. **Phase 4 Finding 1 (distance band) is a non-issue on uniform-4 data — do NOT pin `Fixed(4)`**: With every shipped distance monster at `targetdistance="4"` and every melee at `"1"` (29× / 128×), `DistanceKeep::PerType` already resolves to the C++ hardcoded-4 band (flee `<4`, chase `cheb-4`, dance `==4`). CipSoft 772 has no per-type keep distance at all (the `.mon` files have no such field), so per-type is a 1098-ism that coincides at 4. Crucially, `target_distance` doubles as the melee-vs-distance discriminator (`monster_idle_uses_dist_branch` = `target_distance > 1 && throw_possible`), so forcing `Fixed(4)` via `monster_effective_target_distance` would flip a `targetdistance="1"` melee monster that carries a ranged spell into a kiter. Keep `PerType`; the only exposure is content drift (a distance monster authored with `!= 4`), optionally covered by a warn-only load guard. *(audit Phase 4 Finding 1 reassessment, June 2026)*

73. **P5 RNG unification: route per-draw, defer the `ai_rng` retirement (needs oracle re-baseline)**: The concrete sim-parity leaks are fixable independently by routing each flagged draw through the existing `parity_*` dispatchers (glibc in seeded sim, `thread_rng` live): `RandomShuffle` ported as `parity_random_shuffle` (forward Fisher-Yates, one `parity_random(min, size-1)` per index — the rand crate's `SliceRandom::shuffle` is *backward* with a different draw count, so it cannot match C++ even on a glibc-backed rng), used for both `SearchFlightField` sub-slices (9); roam → `parity_rand_mod(4)` (10); monster spell damage/heal/speed variation → `parity_random` on `beat_driven_loop` (14, matching the Condition arm); spawn tie-break → `parity_random(0,99)` (19). These let the affected functions drop their `&mut rng` params/borrows. **But** moving draws onto the glibc stream changes the seeded harness draw sequence, so the `run_sim_battery.py` oracle `.jsonl` logs must be re-captured from the CipSoft harness before the `TFS_SIM_MELEE_REALIGN` hack and `ai_rng` can be retired (Finding 8/15) — that re-baseline needs the C++ oracle and is out of band for a Rust-only change. The hack is gated on `sim_glibc_rng_enabled()` so it's inert in live play and plain `cargo test`; leave it until the re-baseline. *(`sim_glibc_rand.rs`, `monster_distance_step.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `spawn_placement.rs`, audit Phase 5, June 2026)*
74. **772 `IdleStimulus` ordering: summon lifecycle runs BEFORE the sleeping/idle check**: C++ `TMonster::IdleStimulus` (`crnonpl.cc:2345`) checks `LifeEndRound` then immediately runs the `Master != 0` summon despawn/re-bind block (`:2359–2405`) — only *after* that does it reach the `State == SLEEPING` / idle logic. A sleeping summon still gets despawned if its master is gone or too far. The Rust port must place `monster_idle_summon_lifecycle_772` before the `sleeping_772` early-return, not inside the `if self.beat_driven_loop` block after it. *(audit Phase 6 Finding 20, `idle_stimulus.rs`, June 2026)*

75. **772 `sendCreatureSay` omits the `level` field — era-aware codec needed**: 1098 `ProtocolGame::sendCreatureSay` (`src/protocolgame.cpp` ~2427) writes `u32 statementId + name + u16 level + u8 speakType + pos + text`. 772 (`gameserver/src/protocolgame.cpp` ~1422) writes `u32 statementId + name + u8 speakType + pos + text` — **no `level`**. The `CreatureSayWire` struct + `ProtocolCodec::encode_creature_say` dispatch handles both eras; `broadcast_creature_say_viewport` was refactored from the hardcoded 1098 `send_creature_say` helper to the codec path. Monster talk speak types: `TALKTYPE_MONSTER_YELL=0x10`, `TALKTYPE_MONSTER_SAY=0x11` (TVP `const.h`). *(audit Phase 6 Finding 3, `game_world_spectators.rs`, `codec/v772.rs`, June 2026)*

76. **`remove_creature` proactively despawns all summons — C++ defers to next `IdleStimulus`**: Rust `GameWorld::remove_creature` (`game_world_lifecycle.rs:47–55`) recursively removes all creatures whose `master == id` before removing the master itself. C++ does NOT do this — the master disappears, the summon stays until its next `IdleStimulus` discovers `GetCreature(Master) == NULL` and despawns. The Rust proactive cleanup is a superset (summons never orphan), but tests that exercise the C++ `Master == NULL` path must bypass `remove_creature` and directly `SlotMap::remove` the master to avoid the summon chain. The `SummonedCreatures == 0` relog edge (C++ `:2370`) is covered by the proactive cleanup for the common logout path. *(audit Phase 6 Finding 20, `game_world_lifecycle.rs`, June 2026)*

77. **Pass 8 Phase 1 re-audit: 10 push/collision divergences from C++ `MovePossible`** (`crnonpl.cc:2141–2293`): The Rust kick path and planning gates diverged from C++ 772 `MovePossible` in 10 ways (3 HIGH, 5 MED, 2 LOW). Key findings and fixes:

    - **AI#23 kick-and-retry loop** (`crnonpl.cc:2185` `for Attempt 0..100`): C++ re-checks the destination after each kick and kicks again — a monster can step through a multi-deep creature wall on the same beat. Rust kicked once and returned `Exhausted` (1-second stall). Fixed: added `for _attempt in 0..100` loop with tile re-check.
    - **P1-A1 `!is_summon` gate** (`crnonpl.cc:2202`): C++ `MovePossible` has no summon check — a summon with `KickCreatures` CAN kick blocking monsters. Rust gated on `!is_summon` in three places. Fixed: dropped the gate.
    - **P1-A2 player tile hard block** (`crnonpl.cc:2229–2233`): C++ `MovePossible(Execute=false)` lets a non-summon kicker plan through a player tile (EXHAUSTED fires at Execute). Rust treated player tiles as hard blocks in path search. Fixed: player tiles are plannable-through for non-summon non-IGNORED kickers.
    - **P1-A3 single-step gates used 1098 model**: Dance/roam/flee step gates went through `tile_query_add_monster` (1098), not 772 `MovePossible`. Fixed: extracted `monster_move_possible_planning_772` (772 creature/item gate without TShortway terrain checks) and routed `monster_can_occupy_chase_tile` through it on 772.
    - **P1-B1 hazard immunity poison-only** (`crnonpl.cc:2264–2267`): C++ checks per-damage-type (`NoPoison`/`NoBurning`/`NoEnergy`). Rust used `immunity_poison` for all fields. Fixed: added `immunity_fire`/`immunity_energy` fields + `avoid_damage_type_772` on items DB.
    - **P1-B2 `IGNORED_BY_MONSTERS` player** (`crnonpl.cc:2230`): C++ treats as hard block. Rust returned `Exhausted`. Fixed: added `IGNORED_BY_MONSTERS` check.
    - **P1-B3 invisibility** (`crnonpl.cc:2221–2223`): C++ blocks invisible creatures when mover lacks `SeeInvisible`. Rust had no check. Fixed: added `see_invisible` field + `is_invisible()` on creature base + invisibility gate in planning and kick paths.
    - **P1-B4 `KickCreature` dest validation** (`crnonpl.cc:3066`): C++ calls blocker's own `MovePossible(Execute=true)`. Rust used 1098 `tile_query_add_monster`. Fixed: replaced `try_creature_walk_step` with `monster_move_possible_planning_772` + forced `move_creature_on_map`.
    - **P1-B5 `IsHouse` check** (`crnonpl.cc:2168`): C++ blocks house tiles in `MovePossible`. Rust only checked PZ. Fixed: added house tile check.
    - **Data model**: `MonsterDefenses` + `MonsterCombatSnapshot` + `MonsterAiConfig` + `Monster` gained `immunity_fire`, `immunity_energy`, `see_invisible` (parsed from `<immunity fire="1"/>`, `<immunity energy="1"/>`, `<immunity invisible="1"/>`). `ItemsDb` gained `avoid_damage_type_772` returning `FieldDamageType` (resolved from items.xml `<attribute key="field" value="fire|poison|energy"/>`). `CreatureBase` gained `is_invisible()` checking `active_conditions` for `ConditionType::Invisible`.
    - **Architecture**: `monster_tshortway_fill_walkable` was refactored to delegate to `monster_move_possible_planning_772` for the `MovePossible` creature/item gate, then add TShortway-specific terrain checks (`BANK` ground, waypoint chain). This separates `MovePossible` (used by single-step gates) from `FillMap` (used by A*/TShortway pathfinder). `move_creature_on_map` was made `pub(crate)` for the forced-relocate kick path. *(audit Pass 8, `monster_push.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, `creature/monster.rs`, `creature/monster_combat.rs`, `creature/base.rs`, `walk/mod.rs`, `items.rs`, `monsters.rs`, June 2026)*

78. **Phase 8: `CreatureAction::Rotate` + atomic `Execute` drain — the 400 ms→200 ms melee turn-and-hit fix** (GL#7, AI#22, Structural): Every melee turn-and-hit took 400 ms in Rust vs 200 ms in C++ because (a) the `CreatureAction` enum lacked a `Rotate` variant (C++ `TDRotate` exists at `cract.cc:818`), so the rotate-toward-attack-target path called `monster_update_look_direction` directly — which is gated by `walk_timer_idle` and SKIPS the rotate when a walk is armed; and (b) `run_monster_todo_execute` drained one action per call, not atomically. C++ `crnonpl.cc:2872-2873` calls `Rotate(Target)` unconditionally in ATTACKING/PANIC, then enqueues `ToDoAttack()`, and `Execute` (`cract.cc:783-898`) is a `while(true)` that drains all zero-delay entries in one call. Fix: added `CreatureAction::Rotate { target_id }` (closing the structural enum gap), `enqueue_creature_rotate`, `monster_execute_rotate_toward` (NO `walk_timer_idle` gate — matches C++ unconditional `Rotate(Target)`), and changed `monster_idle_rotate_toward_attack_target` to enqueue the action instead of calling the gated `monster_update_look_direction`. The atomic drain is realized via the existing tail-recursion in `finish_creature_todo_execute` → `run_monster_todo_execute` (route `Rotate` through the `Go|Attack` dispatch arm), which chains zero-delay actions in one beat — semantically equivalent to C++'s `while(true)` `Execute` and bounded by the `+1` re-insertion clamp. **Key insight:** the existing recursion already provided atomic chaining for Go→Attack; adding Rotate to the same dispatch arm extends it to Rotate→Attack without a risky rewrite of the hot step path. `monster_update_look_direction` retains its `walk_timer_idle` gate for the casting turn (`monster_idle_try_casting` line 768) and the 1098 `onThink` path (line 1132) — only the ATTACKING/PANIC rotate-toward-attack-target path bypasses it. Tests: `test_phase8_rotate_then_attack_fires_in_one_beat` (direction change + HP drop + no `server_ms` advance in one call), `test_phase8_rotate_enqueued_when_walk_armed` (Rotate enqueued despite armed walk timer), `test_phase8_idle_tail_enqueues_rotate_before_attack` (enqueue order). *(audit Phase 8, `creature_todo.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, June 2026)*

79. **Phase 9: Z-level target clear + move-stimulus fixes (AI#24, AI#25, AI#26, GL#24)**: Four small parity fixes closing the "ramp drop" target-loss defect and the `[Attack, Go]` combat re-arm over-gate. **AI#24** — `monster_events.rs` Z-change target clear was not era-gated; C++ `CreatureMoveStimulus` (`crmain.cc:920`) does NOT clear targets on Z-change (only re-arms close-chase). Gated the Z-change clear on `!beat_driven_loop` (1098 only); 772 monsters now keep tracking across ramp drops and re-acquire via the idle lose-target range check (`crnonpl.cc:2422`) instead of losing the target instantly. The `!target_visible` clear stays for both eras (redundant with the idle range check, not flagged). **Key testing insight:** cross-floor `creature_can_see` returns false for z≤7→z>7 transitions, so the AI#24 test must use z=8→z=9 (underground ±2) to keep `target_visible=true` and isolate the Z-change gate from the visibility clear. **AI#25** — `monster_idle_772_should_lose_target` was missing `IsHouse` (`crnonpl.cc:2427`) and `IsInvisible && !SeeInvisible` (`crnonpl.cc:2429`) checks. Added both after the existing Protection zone check, matching C++ order (Protection → House → Invisibility → LoseTarget roll). House tiles are `Tile::House(_)` (not a `ZoneType` variant) — checked via `matches!` like `monster_ai.rs:2632`. **Key testing insight:** the acquire path (`monster_idle_772_acquire_target`) does NOT check `IsHouse` or `IsInvisible` (same C++ gap at `crnonpl.cc:2514-2516`, not in Phase 9 scope), so tests use a non-summon monster as the target — the acquire path filters non-summon monsters (`crnonpl.cc:2500`) and skips re-acquisition after the lose-target clear. **AI#26** — `monster_combat_creature_move_stimulus` used `!has_attack || has_go` (whole-queue scan) instead of C++'s head-only check `ToDoList.at(ActToDo)->Code == TDAttack` (`crmain.cc:931-932`). Replaced with `todo.queue.front() == Some(&CreatureAction::Attack)` — empty queue → `None` != `Some(Attack)` → early return (matches `ActToDo >= NrToDo`). A `[Attack, Go]` queue (head=Attack) now correctly fires the combat re-arm instead of being blocked by the tail `Go`. **GL#24** — `monsters_witnessing_move` sorts by SlotMap key (creation order); C++ `TFindCreatures::getNext` (`crmain.cc:101-144`) walks 16×16 blocks in row-major order. Rust uses 64×64 chunks (`CHUNK_SIZE=64`, `grid.rs:13`) — 4× coarser granularity makes exact parity impossible without re-bucketing. Documented as `NOTE(parity)` with creation-order sort kept as deterministic fallback (audit's explicit fallback path). Tests: 7 new (2 AI#24, 3 AI#25, 2 AI#26); 446 pass. *(audit Phase 9, `monster_events.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, June 2026)*

80. **Turn-on-spot defect: `Rotate` must be a direct call, not an enqueued todo action** (audit: turn-on-spot, `crnonpl.cc:2872-2873`, `cract.cc:447-473,1541-1557`): Monsters turning on the spot (visible pause + turn, then resume walking) when changing direction during pathing. Root cause: Phase 8 modeled C++ `TDRotate` (`cract.cc:818`) as a `CreatureAction::Rotate { target_id }` enum variant enqueued after `Go`. But C++ `crnonpl.cc:2872-2873` calls `Rotate(Target)` **directly** in `IdleStimulus` — it is never enqueued as a todo action during the ATTACKING/PANIC combat tail. The enqueued `Rotate` only fired when the `walk_queue` emptied (path segment complete), so the 0x6B turn broadcast landed in a **separate beat** from any `SV_CMD_MOVE_CREATURE` move packet — the client rendered a visible "turn on the spot," followed by a pause (Attack defer + idle stimulus), then the next path segment. In C++ the `Rotate(Target)` broadcast (`SV_CMD_CHANGE_FIELD` via `AnnounceChangedObject`) and the first `TDGo`'s move packet (`SendMoveCreature` via `AnnounceMovingCreature`) are sent in the **same tick** — the client processes them together and the turn is imperceptible. **Key insight:** C++ has two direction-setting paths during moves: (1) `NotifyTurn` (`cract.cc:1541-1557`) sets `this->Direction` **silently** inside `::Move` (no broadcast — the move packet itself carries the new facing); (2) `Rotate(Target)` (`cract.cc:447-473`) sets direction **and** broadcasts `OBJECT_CHANGED` via `AnnounceChangedObject` — used only in the idle combat tail and before spell casts. The enqueued Rust `Rotate` used path (2)'s broadcast but fired it at the wrong time (after path segment completion, not during idle stimulus). Fix: changed `monster_idle_rotate_toward_attack_target` to call `monster_execute_rotate_toward` **directly** (no enqueue), removed `CreatureAction::Rotate` enum variant, `enqueue_creature_rotate`, `has_rotate`, the `Rotate` branch in `execute_creature_todo_action`, and `TodoExecuteKind::Rotate`. The direct call lands the 0x6B in the same beat as the first `Go`/`Attack`, matching C++ outcome. `monster_update_look_direction` retains its `walk_timer_idle` gate for the casting turn and 1098 `onThink` path. Tests: 3 rewritten (`test_rotate_direct_call_turns_monster_immediately`, `test_rotate_direct_call_fires_when_walk_armed`, `test_idle_tail_rotate_direct_then_attack_enqueued`); 415 pass. *(audit turn-on-spot, `creature_todo.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, June 2026)*

81. **Cross-floor attack + spell defect: `ObjectDistance` returns `INT_MAX` for different Z, and every C++ spell shape checks Z** (`info.cc:302-318`, `crcombat.cc:574-578`, `magic.cc:423,522`): Monsters could deal melee damage AND cast spells at players on a different Z-level because Rust's `chebyshev` (x/y only) reported in-range distances for adjacent positions across floors. **Melee/ranged:** C++ `ObjectDistance` (`info.cc:313`) returns `INT_MAX` when `ObjZ1 != ObjZ2`, so `TCombat::Attack` (`crcombat.cc:574-578`) gets `Distance > 8` → `StopAttack(0)` + `throw TARGETLOST`. The Rust idle lose-target check already checks `tp.z != pos.z`, but a queued `Attack` could fire in `monster_do_attacking` BEFORE the next `IdleStimulus` cleared the target (race after AI#24's Z-change target retention). Fix: Z-level check at the top of `monster_do_attacking` — when `monster_pos.z != target_pos.z`, delay 200 ms and return. **Spells:** C++ `VictimShapeSpell` (`magic.cc:423`) checks `Actor->posz != Victim->posz` → return, and `CircleShapeSpell` (`magic.cc:522`, used by `DestinationShapeSpell`) checks `Actor->posz != DestZ` → return. `OriginShapeSpell`/`AngleShapeSpell` use `Actor->posz` for all tiles (implicitly same-Z). The Rust spell casting path (`monster_idle_try_casting`) used `chebyshev` for range (x/y only) and `monster_sight_clear` for terrain — but NO Z-check for `Victim`/`Destination` shapes. `Origin`/`Angle` tile generation already uses `caster_pos.z` (`monster_idle_spell_tiles` lines 684, 701), so those were safe.
