Fix: added Z-check in `monster_idle_try_casting` — `Victim`/`Destination` spells skip when `pos.z != target_pos.z`, matching C++ `VictimShapeSpell`/`CircleShapeSpell`. Tests: `test_772_attack_blocked_across_z_levels` (melee z=7 vs z=8, HP must not drop), `test_772_spell_blocked_across_z_levels` (Victim spell z=7 vs z=8, HP must not drop); 417 pass. *(audit cross-floor-attack, `monster_ai.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, June 2026)*

82. **Close-chase wakeup gap: ATTACKING monster stalls ~1 s after chase-batch drain** (`cract.cc:1010-1023`, `crnonpl.cc:2808`, `idle_stimulus.rs:1511`): A melee monster in `ATTACKING` state at distance > 1 would freeze for ~1 second after every 3-step chase batch drained, then resume chasing. Root cause: `monster_idle_classify_walk_branch` returns `Hold` for ATTACKING + dist>1 (mirrors C++ `crnonpl.cc:2808` `if(State != ATTACKING && State != PANIC)` — no `ToDoGo` from `IdleStimulus`). The Go is enqueued by `monster_combat_enqueue_close_chase_go` inside `monster_enqueue_todo_attack_actions` (the `CanToDoAttack` close-walk path). But the `needs_wakeup` gate was `next_wakeup.is_none() && !todo.has_go()` — the `!has_go()` check assumed any Go in the queue was already scheduled by the walk branch's `idle_enqueue_paced_go`. When the walk branch is `Hold`, the Go was enqueued by the attack path WITHOUT scheduling, so `has_go()` was true → `needs_wakeup` was false → no heap entry. The monster sat with `[Go, Attack]` and no `next_wakeup` until the ~1 Hz think tick (`EVENT_CREATURE_THINK_INTERVAL_MS = 1000`) fired `monster_combat_reschedule_if_stalled` → `schedule_immediate_todo_wakeup`. C++ `ToDoStart` (`cract.cc:1010-1023`) ALWAYS arms `NextWakeup` when the todo list is non-empty — it's called unconditionally at the end of `IdleStimulus` (`crnonpl.cc:2886`). The Rust port scattered the scheduling across paths and missed the close-chase-Go case. Fix: changed `needs_wakeup` to check only `next_wakeup.is_none()`; when true and `has_go()`, schedule `todo_start_go_delay(cid, true)` (the Go's walk delay — head entry, same as C++ `CalculateDelay(TDGo)`); otherwise schedule the attack delay. Test: `test_e3_attacking_close_chase_arms_wakeup_after_idle`; 418 pass. *(close-chase-wakeup-gap, `idle_stimulus.rs`, June 2026)*

83. **772 game loop wallclock leak: `tick_player_pings` used `Instant::now()` on the 772 path** (`connections.cc:21-51`, `main.cc:318-456`, `game_world_tick.rs:39`, `player_ping.rs:34`): The 772 decompile's `TConnection::Process` (`connections.cc:21-51`) is **entirely round-counter based** — pings at `LastCommand == 30 || == 60`, idle warn at `LastAction == 900`, idle kick at `LastAction >= 960`, connection timeout at `LastCommand >= 90`. No wallclock (`Instant`/`OTSYS_TIME`) anywhere in the 772 game loop. The 772 `AdvanceGame` (`main.cc:318`) advances `ServerMilliseconds` by `NumBeats * Beat` (logical delta from SIGALRM beat counter), not wallclock. The Rust `run_other_subsystems` (called from both `on_tick` 1098 and `advance_beat_772` 772) unconditionally called `tick_player_pings(now: Instant)` — a 1098 `Player::sendPing` concept using wallclock `Instant::now()` for 5-second ping intervals. On the 772 path this was a wallclock leak: ping timing rode the wall clock instead of round counters. Additionally, `process_connections_772` was missing the round-based ping (30/60 rounds) and the `LastCommand >= 90` connection timeout logout. Fix: (1) gated `tick_player_pings` on `!beat_driven_loop` in `run_other_subsystems`; (2) added round-based ping at `PING_ROUND_1=30` / `PING_ROUND_2=60` and `COMMAND_TIMEOUT_ROUNDS=90` connection timeout to `process_connections_772`, matching `connections.cc:24,37`. **Key insight:** the 772 game loop has zero wallclock reads for game-state decisions — all timing is `server_ms` (logical clock from SIGALRM beat count) or `round_nr_772` (round counter from `OtherTimeCounter >= 1000`). The only real-world clock use is `tick_ambiente_light_772` → `world_time_from_local_clock()` for day/night cycle, matching C++ `GetAmbiente`/`GetRealTime`. Tests: `round_based_ping_at_30_rounds`, `connection_timeout_kick_at_90_rounds`; 420 pass. *(772-wallclock-leak, `game_world_tick.rs`, `connections_772.rs`, June 2026)*

84. **Config-driven idle kick: `kickIdlePlayerAfterMinutes` in `config.lua`** (`configmanager.cpp:263`, `connections.cc:29-35`, `config.rs:309`, `connections_772.rs:49`): The 772 decompile hardcodes idle warn at `LastAction == 900` (15 min) and kick at `LastAction >= 960` (16 min) — but TFS 1098's `configmanager.cpp:263` exposes `KICK_AFTER_MINUTES` via `kickIdlePlayerAfterMinutes` (default 15) in `config.lua`. The user requested the idle timeout be configurable: setting `kickIdlePlayerAfterMinutes = 10` should warn at 10 min and kick at 11 min. Added `ConnectionConfig` struct to `config.rs` with `kick_idle_after_minutes: u32` (loaded from `kickIdlePlayerAfterMinutes`, default 15), `idle_warn_rounds()` → `minutes * 60`, and `idle_kick_rounds()` → `(minutes + 1) * 60` (one minute after warning, matching the C++ "disconnected in one minute" message). Stored on `GameWorld` as `connection_config`, plumbed from `GameWorld::new` via `ConnectionConfig::from_config`. `process_connections_772` now uses `self.connection_config.idle_warn_rounds()` / `idle_kick_rounds()` instead of hardcoded 900/960. The warning message uses `format!` with the configured minutes value. The proactive ping cadence (30/60 rounds) and dead-connection timeout (90 rounds) remain hardcoded — they are 772 engine constants in the decompile (`connections.cc:24,37`), not config-driven. **Key insight:** 1 round = 1 second (Beat=200ms, OtherTimeCounter >= 1000ms → RoundNr++), so `minutes * 60` converts cleanly. Tests: `idle_kick_uses_config_minutes` (10 min → warn at 600, kick at 660), `idle_kick_at_960_rounds` (default 15 min → kick at 960); 421 pass. *(config-idle-kick, `config.rs`, `game_world.rs`, `connections_772.rs`, June 2026)*

85. **772 `ProcessCreatures` is regen-only — NOT an AI think sweep** (`crmain.cc:1075–1138`, `creature_think.rs:108`): The 772 `ProcessCreatures` does HP/mana regen (via `SKILL_FED` interval), player `CheckState` / logout marks, and a death safety net (`HP <= 0 && !IsDead → Death()`). It does **NOT** call `onThink`, `IdleStimulus`, target validation, or idle status updates. Monster AI is driven **entirely** by the ToDoQueue / `IdleStimulus` (when queue drains), `CreatureMoveStimulus` (when a creature moves), and `DamageStimulus` (when damage is taken). The Rust port's `process_creatures_772` was calling `monster_on_think` — a 1098 `checkCreatures` pattern — which caused premature target loss (via `creature_on_think` visibility check on a 1 Hz timer instead of organic `IdleStimulus` re-evaluation) and premature sleep transitions (via `monster_update_idle_status` setting `Sleeping` when `opponent_ids.is_empty()` instead of after repeated 1 s idle cycles with no visible targets). The 1098 `check_creatures` correctly calls `monster_on_think` on a 1 Hz bucket sweep — that's TFS `Game::checkCreatures` → `Creature::onThink`. The 772 path was likely copied from 1098 without recognizing the different contract. Fix: replaced `process_creatures_772` with C++ `ProcessCreatures` logic (death safety only; regen already in `process_skills_772`). Tests: `process_creatures_772_does_not_call_on_think`, `process_creatures_772_applies_death_safety`, `process_creatures_772_does_not_clear_targets`; 457 pass. *(rc1-process-creatures-regen-only, `creature_think.rs`, June 2026)*

86. **772 `IdleStimulus` idle-wandering catch-all — decomposed helpers lost the unconditional tail** (`crnonpl.cc:2920–2939`, `idle_stimulus.rs:1195`): C++ `IdleStimulus` is a single function where every path either returns early with `ToDoStart()` (combat branches: flee, master follow, melee/dist chase, dance) or falls through to the idle-wandering catch-all at the bottom which **always** ends with `ToDoWait(1000) + ToDoStart()` — either after a successful roam step (`ToDoGo + ToDoWait(1000) + ToDoStart()`) or when no roam step is found (`ToDoWait(1000) + ToDoStart()`). This guarantees the monster re-thinks in 1000 ms even when no action was queued. The Rust port decomposed `IdleStimulus` into many small helpers (`monster_idle_prepare_and_enqueue_go`, `monster_idle_maybe_enqueue_attack`, `monster_idle_maybe_enqueue_at_goal_wait`, `monster_idle_reschedule_target_bound_if_parked`) — good for readability, but lost the unconditional catch-all tail. When a monster had no target and roam failed (Hold outcome), none of the helpers armed a wakeup, causing ~750 ms stalls until the next `process_creatures_772`. Fix: added a trailing fallback at the end of `monster_idle_stimulus_inner` that mirrors the C++ catch-all: if no wakeup was armed by the branches above (`todo.is_empty() && next_wakeup.is_none()`), enqueue `ToDoWait(1000) + ToDoStart()`. The `already_armed` guard prevents double-scheduling when an earlier branch already armed a wakeup. Tests: `rc2_idle_stimulus_always_schedules_wakeup_when_no_action_queued`, `rc2_idle_stimulus_does_not_double_schedule_when_already_armed`, `rc2_idle_trailing_wait_is_1000ms`; 457 pass. *(rc2-idle-wandering-catch-all, `idle_stimulus.rs`, June 2026)*


87. **772 player `SetAttackDest` + `CanToDoAttack` chase — `Following` is not a separate target** (`crcombat.cc:357-511`, `receiving.cc:1133-1155`, `player_combat.rs`): The 772 decompile has **no** `follow_target` field — follow == attack-with-`Following` flag. `SetAttackDest(TargetID, Follow)` sets `AttackDest = TargetID` and `Following = Follow`; `CanToDoAttack` then reads `Following` to force `ChaseMode = CHASE_MODE_CLOSE` (`:493-495`). The Rust port sets both `attack_target` and `follow_target` (so the shared `Go`/pathfinding arms key off `follow_target` for repath), but `follow_target` is only set when `follow=true`. The `CAttack` packet handler enqueues `ToDoAttack` + `ToDoStart`; the thrown-`RESULT` catch (`ToDoClear` + `SendResult` + `ToDoYield`) lives in `player_set_attack_dest` for the initial packet and in `player_execute_attack` for the idle re-arm path (`crplayer.cc:393-402`). The melee **strike** is deferred — `CanToDoAttack` chase re-paths on the attack beat, but no weapon damage is dealt until a player weapon-combat system exists. *(phase1-4-player-combat-dest, `player_combat.rs`, July 2026)*

88. **772 drunk stagger uses `TimerValue` not TFS `getDrunkenness`** (`cract.cc:392-413`, `walk/mod.rs:try_drunk_walk_direction`): The CipSoft formula is `StaggerChance = max(7 - DrunkLevel, 1); rand() % StaggerChance == 0` where `DrunkLevel = Skills[SKILL_DRUNKEN]->TimerValue()` (the remaining condition timer). This is **not** the TFS formula (`uniform_random(0,399) / 4 > getDrunkenness()`). With high `DrunkLevel` (just applied), `StaggerChance = 1` → always stagger; as the condition wears off, staggering becomes less likely. The Rust port maps `DrunkLevel` to `base.drunkenness` (set by `SpellImpact::Drunk`). On stagger: `ToDoClear` + `SendSnapback` (player) + `ToDoTalk("Hicks!")` + `ToDoStart` + random cardinal step replaces the intended direction. The `CreatureAction::Talk { text: &'static str }` variant avoids allocation in the hot walk path. *(phase1-5-drunk-stagger, `walk/mod.rs`, `creature_todo.rs`, July 2026)*

89. **772 `SendClearTarget` (`0xA3`) is a single-byte packet on both eras** (`gameserver/src/protocolgame.cpp:1485-1490`, `src/protocolgame.cpp:2497-2500`, `codec/v772.rs`, `codec/v1098.rs`): Both 772 and 1098 send `0xA3` as a standalone byte for `sendCancelTarget`. The codec `encode_clear_target()` returns a single-byte `NetworkMessage` on both `Codec772` and `Codec1098`. *(phase1-4-clear-target-codec, `tfs-rust-net/src/codec/`, July 2026)*

90. **772 `KickCreature` dest validation must use execute-mode `MovePossible` (recursive chain-push), not planning gate** (`crnonpl.cc:3066`, `monster_push.rs`): C++ `TMonster::KickCreature` calls `Creature->MovePossible(Dest, Execute=true)` which recursively kicks whatever creature sits on the escape tile (chain-push: A pushes B, B pushes C, C moves, B moves into C's spot, A moves into B's spot — all in one beat). The Rust port previously used `monster_move_possible_planning_772` (Execute=false), which treats pushable monsters as plannable-through (`continue`) and skips the recursive kick. This caused two bugs: (1) creature stacking — `move_creature_on_map` forcibly relocated the blocker *onto* the pushable creature's tile, and (2) spurious kick-kills — a blocker whose only escape tiles had pushable monsters was killed instead of chain-pushed. Fix: `monster_move_possible_execute_for_kick_772` runs the planning gate first (hard blocks → false), then loops over creatures on the escape tile and recursively calls `monster_kick_creature_772_inner` (chain-push). A `MAX_KICK_DEPTH = 8` cycle guard bounds recursion (C++ relies on fixed N,S,W,E offset order + skip-kicker; Rust is explicit). A non-monster guard at the top of `monster_kick_creature_772_inner` prevents accidentally killing players during chain-push (C++ `KickCreature` only kicks monsters, `crnonpl.cc:3042-3045`). *(F2, `monster_push.rs`, July 2026)*

91. **772 `monster_exhausted_wait_772` must distinguish kick-kill (Target preserved) from player-tile (Target cleared)** (`cract.cc:870-877`, `crnonpl.cc:2236-2242`, `idle_stimulus.rs`): The C++ `Execute` catch does `ToDoClear()` only — it does NOT clear `Target` itself. It relies on the throw site: the player-tile site (`crnonpl.cc:2236-2238`) clears `Target = 0` before `throw EXHAUSTED`; the kick-kill site (`crnonpl.cc:2241-2242`) does NOT clear `Target`. The Rust port previously called `clear_targets()` unconditionally in `monster_exhausted_wait_772`, citing the wrong catch block (`crnonpl.cc:2890-2898` — the `IdleStimulus` catch, not the `Execute` catch). This dropped aggro on kick-kill where C++ keeps it. Fix: split `MonsterKickOutcome` into `Exhausted` (kick-kill: target preserved) and `ExhaustedDropTarget` (player-tile: target cleared), and add a `clear_target: bool` parameter to `monster_exhausted_wait_772`. F2+F3 compound with the RC1 fix (no 1 Hz think re-acquire): an F3 target-drop on a spurious F2 kill now has no safety net — fixing F3 (small) before F2 (medium) ensures target preservation is in place before chain-push reduces the kill rate. *(F3, `monster_push.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `walk/mod.rs`, July 2026)*

92. **772 `CreatureMoveStimulus` sleep-wake polarity: wake for players + player-controlled summons ONLY, not wild monsters** (`crnonpl.cc:2943-2982`, `crnonpl.cc:3139-3146`, `idle_stimulus.rs:monster_sleep_wake_on_creature_move`): C++ `TMonster::CreatureMoveStimulus` wake gate is `if (Creature->Type == NPC) return; if (Creature->Type == MONSTER && !IsPlayerControlled()) return; State = IDLE; ToDoYield();` — i.e. a sleeping monster wakes only when the *mover* is a Player or a player-owned summon (`IsPlayerControlled` = `Master != 0 && Master->Type == PLAYER`). The Rust port had inverted polarity: `!m.base.is_summon() && m.opponent_ids.is_empty() && !m.is_hostile` — waking for wild monsters and staying asleep for player summons, the exact opposite. Fix: replace the monster arm with `m.base.master.is_some_and(|mid| matches!(self.creatures.get(mid), Some(CreatureKind::Player(_))))`. **Do not use `is_summon()` alone** — it is `master.is_some()` (any master), broader than `IsPlayerControlled()` (player master only); NPC-owned summons must not wake. The same player-master pattern is already used in `monster_idle_summon_lifecycle_772` (`idle_stimulus.rs:411`). The `opponent_ids.is_empty() && !is_hostile` band-aid was removed — it was a stale guard compensating for the inverted polarity, not a C++ semantic. *(B3, `idle_stimulus.rs`, July 2026)*

93. **772 AI Z-visibility: underground viewers CAN see surface within 2 floors (M5)** (`connections.cc:357-378`, `game_world_spectators.rs:creature_can_see`): C++ 772 `TConnection::IsVisible` underground branch is **only** `abs(PlayerZ - z) > 2` rejection — there is NO `z < 8` rejection for underground viewers. The Rust `creature_can_see` (used for AI target selection / `onCreatureFound` / chase visibility, mirroring TFS `canSee` with `Map::maxViewportX`=11) had an extra `if tz < 8 { return false; }` in the `my_z >= 8` branch that 772 `IsVisible` does not have. This is a 1098-vs-772 divergence: TFS `canSee` blocks underground→surface; 772 `IsVisible` allows it within ±2 floors. Fix: added a `beat_driven_loop: bool` parameter to `creature_can_see` (consistent with the codebase's era-dispatch pattern; no `*_772` naming). When `beat_driven_loop` (772), the `tz < 8` rejection is skipped — only `abs(dz) > 2` rejects. When false (1098), the original TFS `canSee` semantics are preserved exactly. All 8 call sites (`monster_events.rs` ×4, `monster_ai.rs` ×1, `monster_targets.rs` ×3) pass `self.beat_driven_loop`. `protocol_can_see` (client viewport, `protocolgame.cpp`) is untouched — it is a separate function. Low impact (multi-floor ramp edge case). Test: `creature_can_see_underground_to_surface` (z=9→z=7 visible on 772, blocked on 1098; z=9→z=6 blocked on both). 482 pass. *(M5, `game_world_spectators.rs`, `monster_events.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, `monster_targets.rs`, July 2026)*

94. **F1 regression coverage: hard-blocked ToDo-execute creature re-arms `IdleStimulus` next beat (N2)** (`cract.cc:870-889`, `cract.cc:1001`, `walk/mod.rs:on_walk` Err arm): The Phase 1.3 walk-engine unification widened the blocked-step `Err` arm from monster-only to all `creature_uses_todo_execute` creatures (players + monsters) and made `ToDoClear` unconditional (clears the whole queue regardless of attack state, `cract.cc:871`). The arm then calls `request_idle_stimulus` → `creature_todo_yield` which enqueues `ToDoWait(0)` + arms `next_wakeup = server_ms + 1` (`ToDoStart` clamps `Delay<1` to 1, `cract.cc:1016`). This is functionally equivalent to the audit's §6.2 recommended direct `creature_todo_yield` — a hard-blocked creature re-runs `IdleStimulus` on the next beat (~1 ms logical). **Test approach:** drive `on_walk` directly (the function that owns the Err arm) rather than the full `process_creature_todo` drain. The full drain's `finish_creature_todo_execute` recursively consumes the yield `Wait(0)` via `run_monster_todo_execute` and then runs `idle_stimulus`, masking the F1 Err-arm contract. Asserting against `on_walk`'s post-state isolates the contract: chase `Go` cleared, `locked = false`, `Wait{0}` enqueued, `next_wakeup == server_ms + 1`, `walk_queue` empty. Player analogue asserts the player stops cleanly (no `walk_action` re-arm). The optional hardening (swap `request_idle_stimulus` for a direct `creature_todo_yield` to drop guard coupling) was deferred — the traced path shows all `request_idle_stimulus` guards pass, so minimal diff is preferred. Tests: `hard_block_reruns_idle_next_beat`, `hard_block_player_stops_cleanly`; 482 pass. *(N2, `walk/mod.rs`, July 2026)*

95. **Refactor Phase 1: `#[cfg(test)] #[path] mod <name>;` preserves test module paths and private-item access** (`docs/REFACTOR_AUDIT.md` Phase 1): Extracting an inline `#[cfg(test)] mod tests { ... }` block into a sibling `<file>_tests.rs` via `#[cfg(test)] #[path = "<file>_tests.rs"] mod tests;` is a behavior-preserving pure cut/paste. The `#[path]` file holds the module **body** (the contents between `mod tests {` and its closing `}`), NOT the `mod tests { ... }` wrapper itself. The extracted module remains a *child* of the source module, so `use super::*` and references to private `crate::<src>::<private_item>` continue to resolve — promotion to `crates/<crate>/tests/` integration tests is NOT viable for tests that touch private items. Module paths (`crate::idle_stimulus::tests::*`) are preserved exactly, so `cargo test <filter>` selectors and `module_path!()` output are unchanged. When a source file has **multiple** inline test modules (e.g. `monster_ai.rs` had `mod tests` + `mod world_tests`), extract each to its own `#[path]` file with its own `mod <name>;` declaration — do NOT collapse them into one wrapper module, or the nested path (`...::tests::tests::*`) changes and test filters break. *(Phase 1, all extracted files, July 2026)*

96. **`rtk cargo clippy` aggregated output is non-deterministic; use raw `cargo clippy | grep '^warning:' | sort` for before/after diffs** (`docs/REFACTOR_AUDIT.md` Baseline metrics): The `rtk cargo clippy` wrapper groups warnings by rule and prints only a rotating subset of example locations per rule (the `+N more` / `[see remaining: ...]` lines). Running it twice on identical source produces different displayed subsets — files never touched appear and disappear between runs. The total count is stable, but the displayed rule/location set is NOT. Therefore: do NOT diff rtk clippy summaries to verify "no new warnings." Instead, run raw `cargo clippy --all-targets 2>&1 | grep -E '^warning:' | sort` on both before/after states and `diff` those — the full `^warning:` line list is deterministic and byte-comparable. (To get the "before" state: `git stash` the changed source files, move untracked new files aside, run, then `git stash pop` and restore.) Also: the audit's "Clippy is currently clean on a full clean build — 0 warnings" baseline claim is **stale** — the actual current baseline (2026-07-01) is **46 `^warning:` lines / 44 unique warnings**, all pre-existing (`too_many_arguments`, `dead_code` on test-only-called fns, `drop` of references in `process_skills.rs`, etc.). Future phases: bar is "no NEW warning vs. the live captured baseline," not "0 warnings." *(Phase 1 verification, July 2026)*

97. **Walk audit #1: first step from standstill must arm at `server_ms + 1`, not a full step duration** (`cract.cc:918-923` `CalculateDelay(TDGo)`, `cract.cc:1010-1023` `ToDoStart`, `walk/mod.rs:todo_start_go_delay`): Every walk started from rest carried up to one extra step of input latency vs the 772 reference. Root cause: the 772 beat-path `todo_start_go_delay` fallback (when `earliest_walk_server_ms <= server_ms`) called `todo_go_beat_delay_ms(cid)` which returned `get_step_duration(...)` — a FULL quantized step duration (e.g. 400 ms). C++ `CalculateDelay(TDGo)` leaves `Delay = 0` when `EarliestWalkTime` has elapsed (`cract.cc:918-923`), and `ToDoStart` clamps `Delay < 1` to `1` (`cract.cc:1016-1018`), so `NextWakeup = ServerMilliseconds + 1` — the step lands on the next beat drain (≤ `Beat` ms). Only *subsequent* steps wait out `EarliestWalkTime` set by `NotifyGo`. Fix: replaced the `todo_go_beat_delay_ms(cid)` fallback with the literal `1` (C++ clamp), and removed the now-dead `todo_go_beat_delay_ms` helper. The `on_walk` → `get_walk_delay_logical` re-check already protects against early execution. **Key insight (audit #6 overlap):** the old code had two sources of truth for the walk cooldown — `earliest_walk_server_ms` (C++ `EarliestWalkTime`, set by `NotifyGo`) and `last_step_server_ms` + recomputed `completed_step_duration_ms` (used by `get_walk_delay_logical` in `on_walk`). `todo_go_beat_delay_ms` used the *second* source, diverging from C++ `CalculateDelay` which uses *only* the first. The fix unifies `todo_start_go_delay` on `earliest_walk_server_ms` only. The pre-existing test `test_772_repath_first_step_delay` was asserting the old two-source behavior (set `last_step_server_ms` without setting `earliest_walk_server_ms`); updated it to set `earliest_walk_server_ms` to the post-`NotifyGo` value, which is what C++ `CalculateDelay` actually consults. Tests: `test_audit1_first_step_from_standstill_arms_at_one_ms`, `test_audit1_cooldown_active_arms_at_earliest_walk_time`; 484 pass. *(walk-audit-#1, `walk/mod.rs`, `monster_ai_world_tests.rs`, `idle_stimulus_tests.rs`, July 2026)*

91. **772 ATTACKING monster with no path must clear target + roam, not Retry-park** (`crnonpl.cc:2779-2811,2872-2898`, `crcombat.cc:497-499`, `monster_ai.rs:monster_combat_enqueue_close_chase_go`, `idle_stimulus.rs:monster_idle_maybe_enqueue_attack`): An ATTACKING melee monster (fist > 0) at Distance > 1 does NOT call `ToDoGo` in the walking section — C++ sets `State = ATTACKING` (`:2779`) then skips the chase `ToDoGo` (`:2808` `State != ATTACKING` is false). The close-chase walk is done by `ToDoAttack` → `CanToDoAttack` → `ToDoGo(Target, false, 3)` (`crcombat.cc:497-499`). When `TShortway::Calculate` fails, `ToDoGo` throws **NOWAY** (`cract.cc:1104`), which propagates up to the `IdleStimulus` `catch(RESULT r)` block (`crnonpl.cc:2890-2898`): `Target = 0; ToDoClear();` — for NOWAY (non-EXHAUSTED), fall through to the roam tail (`:2900-2939`). The Rust port's `monster_combat_enqueue_close_chase_go` returned **`Retry`** (keep target + wait 1000 ms) on pathfind Noway, and the attack-tail Noway arm recursed into `monster_idle_prepare_and_enqueue_go` (which re-entered the same Hold→close-chase→Noway path with the target still set — infinite parking). Fix: (1) `monster_combat_enqueue_close_chase_go` returns `Noway` (not `Retry`) on pathfind failure; (2) extracted `monster_idle_noway_clear_and_roam` helper (clear target + execute Roam branch + enqueue Go/Wait) used by the attack-tail Noway arm and `monster_events.rs` combat-move-rearm. The walk-branch Noway handler (`monster_idle_prepare_and_enqueue_go`) already inlined the same clear+roam. **Secondary divergence noted (not fixed):** `monster_idle_maybe_enter_attacking` adds `(target_distance <= 1 || !spells.any(range > 1))` beyond C++'s `SKILL_FIST > 0` — a melee+range monster (dragon) with `target_distance > 1` won't enter ATTACKING in Rust. `MonsterCombatCloseChaseEnqueue::Retry` / `MonsterEnqueueAttackResult::Retry` are now dead code (never returned) — candidates for future cleanup. Tests: `test_772_attacking_no_path_roams_not_park`, `kicker_paths_through_pushable_bear_not_stall`, updated `test_chase_freeze_attack_path_noway_clears_target`; 459 pass. *(772-attacking-noway-roam, `monster_ai.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `monster_events.rs`, July 2026)*

92. **772 `Unpushable` creatures are hard blocks — `KickCreature` kill only applies to pushable creatures** (`crnonpl.cc:2216`, `crnonpl.cc:3036-3098`, 772 `.mon` files): The 772 `MovePossible` creature gate checks `RaceData[Creature->Race].Unpushable` **before** reaching the kick logic (`crnonpl.cc:2216`). An unpushable creature is a **hard block** (`return false`) — no kick, no kill, no path. `KickCreature` (`crnonpl.cc:3036-3098`) is only reached for **pushable** creatures; the kill at `:3074-3080` ("Kein Platz zum Verschieben => Töten.") happens when a **pushable** creature can't find an escape tile. 772 `.mon` race data: only 6 monsters are `Unpushable` — bear, lion, minotaur archer, panda, polar bear, rotworm. Most monsters (rat, bat, spider, etc.) are pushable and CAN be killed by a `KickCreatures` monster (cyclops, demon, etc.) when boxed in. The cyclops itself is `Unpushable` with `KickCreatures` — it can kick pushable creatures but is itself a hard block for others. **User expectation note:** a cyclops cannot kill a bear in 772 — the bear is unpushable and blocks the cyclops. After the roaming fix (lesson #91), the cyclops clears its target and roams when blocked by an unpushable creature. Tests: `cyclops_kills_boxed_in_pushable_blocker_end_to_end` (pushable rat killed), `kicker_paths_through_pushable_bear_not_stall` (planning routes through pushable); 460 total. *(772-unpushable-hard-block, `monster_push.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, July 2026)*

98. **Map audit Phase 2: void-tile creature registration + dual-list desync prevention (audit #3, #7)** (`docs/MAP_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md` Phase 2, `map/mod.rs:register_creature_at`, `map/grid.rs:SparseGrid`): Two storage invariants made explicit. **#3:** `register_creature_at` on a void (unloaded) tile previously silently dropped the creature from both the tile stack and the chunk spatial index. Now detects absent tile → `tracing::error!` (release) + `debug_assert!` (debug/test), never panicking in release per `tfs-packets.md`. `unregister_creature_at` mirrors with `tracing::warn!`. **#7:** `SparseGrid::register_creature`/`unregister_creature` narrowed `pub`→`pub(super)` so only `map/mod.rs` `*_at` wrappers can mutate the chunk spatial list; the grid's own `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` retains child-module access. Added `SparseGrid::debug_assert_creature_lists_agree` (delegated via `Map`) that checks every `Chunk.creatures` entry is on some tile's `TileBody.creatures` list and vice versa — all `debug_assert!`-gated, compiles out in release. **Key insight — two distinct void scenarios:** (a) position in an *absent chunk* → `grid.register_creature` no-ops, creature dropped from BOTH lists (true #3); (b) position in a *populated chunk but on a missing tile* → `grid.register_creature` adds to chunk list but tile list untouched → desync (#7). The `debug_assert!(tile_present)` in `register_creature_at` catches BOTH. **Harness fallout:** 10 pre-existing lib tests (`minimal_world`-based) silently relied on the old silent-drop by registering creatures at positions with no tile. Added `ensure_walkable_tile_if_absent` to the central `insert_monster_with_config`/`insert_monster_from_type`/`insert_npc`/`insert_spectator_player`/`player_walk` harness paths — inserts a default ground tile only if none present (does NOT overwrite intentional tiles). Tests: `tests/map_storage.rs` (3: void-tile noop, unregister noop, batch-move consistency), `map/grid.rs` `mod tests` (3: chunk-list-without-tile detection, tile-list-without-chunk detection, clean-grid pass). `cargo test --lib` 468 passed (was 458+10 failed); all integration binaries pass; clippy clean on changed files. *(Phase 2, `map/mod.rs`, `map/grid.rs`, `sim_harness.rs`, `tests/map_storage.rs`, July 2026)*

99. **772 message-type byte differs from 1098 — `MESSAGE_STATUS_SMALL` is 23 in 772, 21 in 1098** (`sending.cc:285-357`, `enums.hh:674`, `reference/tvp-772/gameserver/src/const.h:86-88`, `src/const.h:190`): The 772 decompile's `SendResult` sends failure/cancel text via `SendMessage(TALK_FAILURE_MESSAGE = 23, ...)` (`sending.cc:339,352`). The 772 TVP `const.h` names this same wire byte `MESSAGE_STATUS_SMALL = 0x17 = 23` — **not** 21. The 1098 `const.h` has `MESSAGE_STATUS_SMALL = 21` (0x15). The Rust code hardcoded `21` (the 1098 value) everywhere, which maps to `TALK_STATUS_MESSAGE` / `MESSAGE_STATUS_DEFAULT` in 772 — a different client display channel. The 772 CipSoft client renders type 23 as a prominent failure message (red console text), while type 21 goes to the status bar (easy to miss). **Fix:** added `failure_message_type()` to `ProtocolCodec` trait — `Codec772` returns 23 (`TALK_FAILURE_MESSAGE`), `Codec1098` returns 21 (`MESSAGE_STATUS_SMALL`). Updated 3 call sites: `walk/mod.rs:on_walk_step_rejected` (bump-into-obstacle), `player_combat.rs:send_combat_result` (combat failure), `game_world_spectators.rs:send_cancel_message` (generic cancel). The `game_world_inventory.rs` "You see ..." message uses type 22 (`MESSAGE_INFO_DESCR`) which is the same value in both eras — no change needed. **General lesson:** all hardcoded message-type constants are era-specific wire values and must go through the codec seam, not be hardcoded in core. *(772-message-type-failure, `codec/mod.rs`, `walk/mod.rs`, `player_combat.rs`, `game_world_spectators.rs`, July 2026)*

100. **Refactor Phase 2: `sim` feature quarantine — `chase_debug` and `sim_glibc_rand` are NOT pure diagnostic** (`docs/REFACTOR_AUDIT.md` Phase 2, `chase_debug.rs`, `sim_glibc_rand.rs`, `sim_harness.rs`, `game_world.rs`, `combat/math.rs`, `creature/monster_combat.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`): The audit framed `sim_harness`, `chase_debug`, and `sim_glibc_rand` as "diagnostic scaffolding" that could be simply `#[cfg(feature = "sim")]`-gated. In reality, `chase_debug` is called from 6 production files (`walk/mod.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `monster_events.rs`, `game_world_lifecycle.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, `creature_todo.rs`) and `sim_glibc_rand` contains production code (`GlibcRngState` as `GameWorld::parity_rng` field, `DANCE_DIR_ORDER` constant, `parity_random/rand_mod/random_shuffle` dispatchers). **Approach:** `#[cfg(any(test, feature = "sim"))]` gates the full implementation; `#[cfg(not(any(test, feature = "sim")))]` provides no-op stubs with identical signatures. `cargo test` auto-enables via `cfg(test)` — no workflow change. Production builds (`cargo build`/`--release`) compile stubs only. Key patterns: (1) `sim_glibc_rng_enabled()` returns `false` as a const-like stub in production; (2) `sim_rng_trace_site()` returns a no-op `SimRngTraceSiteGuard` (field gated by cfg); (3) `parity_random` uses `#[cfg(any(test, feature = "sim"))]` on the sim branch, falling through to `thread_rng` in production; (4) production call sites with `if sim_glibc_rng_enabled() { sim_only_fn() } else { ... }` need `#[cfg]` on the sim branch since the sim-only function doesn't exist in production. **Side effects:** gating `sim_harness` exposed pre-existing dead code (`search_login_field`, `spiral_login_positions`, `harness_place_creature_login` in `spawn_placement.rs`; `player_apply_spell_exhaust` in `game_world.rs`) that was only kept alive by the `chase_kite_sim` bin — gated those with `#[cfg(any(test, feature = "sim"))]` or `#[allow(dead_code)]`. Clippy net warnings **decreased** (lib 44→29, test 76→75). `chase_kite_sim` bin requires `--features sim`; `path_compare` bin needs no feature. *(refactor-phase-2, `Cargo.toml`, `lib.rs`, `chase_debug.rs`, `sim_glibc_rand.rs`, `sim_harness.rs`, `game_world.rs`, `combat/math.rs`, `creature/monster_combat.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, `spawn_placement.rs`, July 2026)*

101. **F3: `TSkillFed` regen keys off the decrementing food counter, not a monotonic timer — and cadence comes from `vocations.xml`, not a hardcoded match** (`crskill.cc:812-885`, `crskill.cc:179-196` `TSkill::Process`, `moveuse.cc:1839-1846` `UseFood`, `data/XML/vocations.xml`): Three coupled fixes in `process_skills.rs`/`vocations.rs`. **(1) Data source:** the old `fed_regen_cadence` Rust `match vocation_id` table had wrong cells (Sorc/Druid `1|2` grouped with promoted `5|6` at manaticks 2; XML has 3 vs 2; Paladin hp/mana ticks wrong; None-vocation mana amount wrong). All four regen values are authored per-vocation in `vocations.xml` (`gainhpticks`/`gainhpamount`/`gainmanaticks`/`gainmanaamount`) and match the decompile exactly — extended `Vocation` struct + parser to read them, added `VocationDatabase::fed_regen_params(voc_id)` with C++ `default:` fallback (12/1/6/2 — `crskill.cc:871,880,884`) for missing vocations (e.g. empty test DB). **(2) Food-remaining gate:** replaced the monotonic `skills_fed_timer: u32` (incremented every `ProcessSkills` unconditionally → regen forever) with `food_remaining: u32` — the `TSkillFed` `Cycle` (`crskill.cc:220` `TimerValue`, `moveuse.cc:1840`). `0` ⇒ skill inactive ⇒ no regen (`crskill.cc:180`). `TSkill::Process` decrements `Cycle` *before* calling `Event` (`crskill.cc:186-191`), so the modulo is taken on the post-decrement value — regen fires when the remaining-food counter hits a multiple of the vocation's tick interval, counting down to 0. **(3) PZ gate:** `TSkillFed::Event` returns early inside a protection zone (`crskill.cc:819`); promoted `GameWorld::tile_in_protection_zone` from private to `pub(crate)` in `player_combat.rs` so `process_skills` reuses the single PZ-check helper (no duplication). **Observable behavior change (intended per audit):** players no longer regen HP/mana when `food_remaining == 0` — today regen runs forever, after F3 it stops until food is eaten (correct 772 parity). `food_remaining` defaults to `0` and will be set when `UseFood` (`moveuse.cc:1821`) is ported in a future effort — tests needing regen must set it explicitly. **Field rename fallout:** 5 `skills_fed_timer: 0` initializers across `login.rs`, `sim_harness.rs`, `spell_tests.rs`, `player_inventory_notifications.rs`, `tests/arena.rs` updated to `food_remaining: 0`. **Pre-existing break fixed in passing:** `tests/arena.rs` was missing the `walk_destinations` field in its inline `CreatureBase` initializer (added in walk-audit #4 but never propagated to the integration test) — added it. Tests: 3 new F3 tests (`fed_regen_uses_vocation_xml_params_and_food_counter`, `fed_regen_skipped_in_protection_zone`, `fed_regen_skipped_when_food_exhausted`); 514 core + 42 content pass. *(F3, `process_skills.rs`, `vocations.rs`, `creature/player.rs`, `player_combat.rs`, `login.rs`, `sim_harness.rs`, `spell_tests.rs`, `player_inventory_notifications.rs`, `tests/arena.rs`, July 2026)*
102. **F2: `SKILL_FED` has two independent values — `Cycle` (food_remaining) and `Act` (food_level) — and the `ProcessCreatures` item regen keys off `Act`, not `Cycle`** (`crmain.cc:1087-1095`, `crskill.cc:220` `TimerValue`, `crskill.cc:19-24` `Get`, `moveuse.cc:1839-1846` `UseFood`, `crplayer.cc:1395-1400` offline drain, `crplayer.cc:2496` save): The 772 decompile's `SKILL_FED` timer-skill stores two persisted values: `Cycle` (the food countdown, returned by `TimerValue()`) and `Act` (the skill "level", returned by `Get()`). F3 modeled `Cycle` as `food_remaining` and used it for vocation regen (`TSkillFed::Event`). F2's `ProcessCreatures` item regen (HP+1/Mana+4) is a **separate** regen that keys off `Act` = `food_level` = `RegenInterval` — `if(RegenInterval > 0 && (RoundNr % RegenInterval) == 0 && !IsDead && !IsProtectionZone)`. Both regens run independently: item regen in `process_creatures_772` (cadence = `food_level`), vocation regen in `process_skills_772` (cadence = vocation tick params, gated on `food_remaining > 0`). **Food persistence:** both fields are saved/loaded (`players.food_remaining` + `players.food_level` columns, migration `20260702000000_food_skill.sql`). **Offline drain** (`crplayer.cc:1395-1400`): on login, `Regen = min(FoodTime/3, OfflineTime/15)`, `food_remaining -= Regen*3`. **Eat action:** the decompile's `UseFood` reads `NUTRITION` from the item type, but TFS stores food data in Lua (`data/scripts/actions/other/food.lua` maps item IDs → nutrition values). The Rust `player:feed(amount)` Lua binding (`LuaMutation::PlayerFeed` → `lua_script_player_feed`) refills `food_remaining` (capped at `MAX_FOOD = 1200`) and sets `food_level = 12` (default `PROFESSION_NONE` `SecsPerHP`, `crskill.cc:833`). `data/lib/core/player.lua`'s `Player.feed` wrapper was removed (the Rust binding is the direct implementation); `food.lua`'s "full" check now uses `player:getFood()` instead of `CONDITION_REGENERATION` ticks. **PK-mark clearing** (`crmain.cc:1102-1105`): `EarliestLogoutRound` field added to `Player`; expiry check in `process_creatures_772` zeroes the field. Full `ClearPlayerkillingMarks` (attacked-players list, aggressor flag, skull broadcast) is **stubbed** — deferred until the PvP aggressor subsystem exists. Tests: 8 new F2 tests (item regen cadence/PZ/dead/off-cadence, PK-mark expiry/non-expiry, feed refill/cap, feed sets food_level). *(F2, `creature_think.rs`, `creature/player.rs`, `game_world_inventory.rs`, `lua_mutation.rs`, `userdata/player.rs`, `game_world_script.rs`, `script_context.rs`, `login.rs`, `game_world_save.rs`, `lua_scope.rs`, `player.rs` DB, `food.lua`, `player.lua`, `compat.lua`, July 2026)*

103. **F4: `addEvent`/`stopEvent` — unified timer id, `Rc<RefCell>` for `Fn` closures, and extract-before-call for reentrancy** (`luascript.cpp:3789` `luaAddEvent`, `luascript.cpp:3907` `luaStopEvent`, `luascript.cpp:18238` `executeTimerEvent`, `scheduler.cpp:10` `Scheduler::addEvent`, `scheduler.cpp:39` `Scheduler::stopEvent`): Three Rust-specific design decisions for the `addEvent`/`stopEvent` port. **(1) Unified timer id:** C++ has two separate id spaces — `lastTimerEventId` (Lua-facing, key in `timerEvents` map, returned to Lua) and the scheduler-internal `eventId` (stored in `LuaTimerEventDesc.eventId`, used for `stopEvent` cancellation). In Rust, the `Scheduler` is only used by `addEvent`/`stopEvent`, so the `event_id` returned by `schedule_after` IS the Lua-facing id AND the cancellation key — one id space, no reverse map. **(2) `Rc<RefCell>` for `Fn` closures:** mlua's `create_function` takes `Fn` (not `FnMut`), so the `addEvent`/`stopEvent` closures can't capture `&mut`. The `timer_events: HashMap<u64, TimerEventDesc>` is wrapped in `Rc<RefCell<…>>` shared between the closures and `LuaRuntime::execute_timer_event`. The `TimerScheduler` (core's `Scheduler`) is accessed via a thread-local `CURRENT_TIMER_SCHEDULER` set once at game-thread startup (same pattern as `CURRENT_CTX`), not captured in closures. **(3) Extract-before-call for reentrancy:** `execute_timer_event` removes the `TimerEventDesc` from the map **before** calling the Lua function (matching C++ `std::move(it->second); timerEvents.erase(it);` before `callFunction`). This prevents reentrancy: a callback that calls `addEvent`/`stopEvent` would otherwise alias the borrowed `RefCell` entry. The `RegistryKey`s drop after the call completes (C++ `luaL_unref`). **Cancellation:** `Scheduler` stores `AbortHandle`s in a `RefCell<HashMap<u64, AbortHandle>>`; `stop_event` aborts the spawned task. The game loop calls `scheduler.forget(event_id)` after dispatching `LuaCallback` to clean up the stale abort handle (the spawned task has already exited). **Wiring:** `cmd_tx`/`cmd_rx` channel created early in `run_server.rs` (before `LuaRuntime::new`) so the `Scheduler` can be constructed and the thread-local set before any script loading that might call `addEvent` at top level. `Rc<Scheduler>` stored on `GameWorld` (`Option` for test compat). Tests: 5 new `timer_events` tests (basic fire, params, stop, missing id, delay clamp). *(F4, `timer_events.rs`, `runtime.rs`, `scheduler.rs`, `event_dispatcher.rs`, `lua_event_dispatcher.rs`, `lua_scope.rs`, `game_loop.rs`, `game_world.rs`, `run_server.rs`, July 2026)*

104. **F8 S1: `ActionObjectRef` carries resolved-at-enqueue identity, NOT a cached `ItemId` — SlotMap generation reuse makes stale `ItemId` unsafe** (`cract.cc:1260/1125/1327` `GetObject`, `cract.cc:783-898` `Execute` `Obj.exists()`, `creature_todo.rs:ActionObjectRef`, `container_ui.rs:resolve_item_at_position`/`validate_item_sprite`): The decompile's `ToDoUse`/`ToDoMove`/`ToDoTurn` builders resolve an `Object` handle via `GetObject(ID, x, y, z, RNum, Type)` at enqueue time and store `Obj.ObjectID` in the `TToDoEntry`. The executor re-validates with `Obj.exists()` at execute time. The naive Rust translation would cache a raw `ItemId` (SlotMap key) at enqueue and reuse it at execute — **this is unsafe** because `SlotMap` reuses freed slots with a new generation, but a stale `ItemId` carrying the old generation silently resolves to `None` (best case) or to a *different* item inserted into the recycled slot (worst case, if the generation wraps). **Correct design:** `ActionObjectRef` stores the wire identity triple (`pos: Position`, `stack_pos: u8`, `sprite_id: u16`) — enough to re-resolve via the existing `resolve_item_at_position(cid, pos, stack_pos)` + re-validate via `validate_item_sprite(item_id, sprite_id)` at execute time, returning `NOTACCESSIBLE` on mismatch. This mirrors the decompile's `GetObject` → `Obj.exists()` pattern without ever holding a live `ItemId` across the enqueue→execute gap. The `Position` encoding (`0xFFFF` = inventory/container, `0x40` flag = container slot) is reused verbatim from the existing reactive executors — no new encoding. **Enum exhaustiveness:** `CreatureAction` is matched exhaustively in `execute_creature_todo_action` (`idle_stimulus.rs:2247`) with no `_ =>` catch-all, so adding `Use`/`Move`/`Turn` variants required stub arms (trace + `TodoExecuteKind::Wait`) to keep it compiling. The stubs never fire in S1 since no builder enqueues these variants yet — they exist purely for exhaustiveness, replaced by real executors in S4. **Naming:** the `Turn` variant is `CTurnObject` (rotate a rotatable *item*, `receiving.cc:549`), NOT `CRotate` (player facing direction, `receiving.cc:213` → `GamePacket::Turn` → `player_turn_request`, stays immediate). No `Rotate` variant on `CreatureAction` — the 772 idle combat tail calls `Rotate(Target)` directly (`crnonpl.cc:2872-2873`), never queued. Tests: 1 new `has_use_move_turn_helpers_detect_queued_variants`; 524 core pass. *(F8-S1, `creature_todo.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, July 2026)*

105. **F8 S2: Two resolution paths for `ActionObjectRef` validation — Use/Turn uses `resolve_item_at_position` (container-priority), Move uses `internal_get_thing_move` (moveable-priority)** (`cract.cc:1258-1296` `ToDoUse`, `cract.cc:1123-1172` `ToDoMove`, `cract.cc:1326-1351` `ToDoTurn`, `receiving.cc:384/430/233/549`, `container_ui.rs:resolve_item_at_position`/`find_tile_item_by_client_sprite`, `game_world_item_cylinder.rs:internal_get_thing_move`): The decompile has a single `GetObject(ID, x, y, z, RNum, Type)` for all three ToDo builders, but Rust has **two** resolution paths because the reactive executors already diverged: `player_use_item`/`player_use_item_ex` resolve via `resolve_item_at_position` (which calls `item_id_for_tile_use` — container-priority `Tile::getUseItem` stack walk) + `find_tile_item_by_client_sprite` fallback (sprite-id match on map tiles), while `player_move_item` resolves via `internal_get_thing_move` (which calls `tile.get_top_down_item()` + `moveable()` check — moveable-priority `STACKPOS_MOVE` path). **S2 design:** `validate_action_object_ref` (Use/Turn) mirrors the Use path; `validate_move_object_ref` (Move) mirrors the Move path. Each builder validates with the **same path its S4 executor will re-validate with** — consistency between enqueue-time and execute-time validation. Both return `Err(ReturnValue::NotPossible)` on failure — C++ `NOTACCESSIBLE` maps to `NotPossible` (matching `walk/mod.rs:1506`'s convention, since the Rust `ReturnValue` enum has no `NotAccessible` variant). **`Wait{100}` prepend:** Use/Turn builders call `enqueue_creature_wait(cid, 100)` then `push_back` the action — producing `[Wait{100}, Use]` / `[Wait{100}, Turn]`. Move has no Wait (`CMoveObject` handler calls `ToDoMove` + `ToDoStart` directly, `receiving.cc:233`). **Test fixture fix:** `pickup_item_type` (gold 2148) was missing `moveable_override: Some(true)` — `internal_get_thing_move` requires `moveable()` to return an item, so the Move builder test failed until this was set. Gold IS moveable in Tibia, so this is a correctness fix, not a test-only hack. Tests: 7 new (4 queue-shape + 3 failure); 531 core pass. *(F8-S2, `creature_todo.rs`, `sim_harness.rs`, July 2026)*
106. **F8 S3: `CalculateDelay` peek-vs-popped — the execute arm must pass `obj2.is_some()` directly, not call the peek-based helper** (`cract.cc:901-960` `CalculateDelay`, `cract.cc:795-801` `Execute` drain, `idle_stimulus.rs:execute_creature_todo_action`, `creature_todo.rs:todo_use_delay_ms`/`multiuse_gate_delay_ms`): C++ `CalculateDelay` peeks `ActToDo` (the current entry) BEFORE popping — it reads `this->ToDoList.at(this->ActToDo)`. The Rust `execute_creature_todo_action` pops the front action FIRST (line 2238), then dispatches. So a peek-based `todo_use_delay_ms(cid)` called from inside the `Use` execute arm sees the NEXT queue entry (or empty), NOT the just-popped `Use` — returning 0 and silently skipping the gate. **Fix:** split into a shared core `multiuse_gate_delay_ms(cid, has_obj2: bool)` that takes the `obj2` presence flag directly. The execute arm calls it with the popped action's `obj2.is_some()`; the peek-based `todo_use_delay_ms` (for S6 handler routing, where the action IS at the front after enqueue) peeks the front and forwards `has_obj2`. **`TodoExecuteKind::Deferred`:** added a new variant for the generic gate-deferred case (wakeup already armed by `todo_start_from_action` in the gate check). In `run_monster_todo_execute` it's a no-op — mirrors C++ `Execute`'s "Delay > 0 → schedule + break" (`cract.cc:795-801`). `AttackDeferred` was not reused because it triggers `monster_combat_reschedule_if_stalled` (monster-combat-specific); `Deferred` is era/creature-neutral. **Single-object Use is ungated:** C++ only gates `Obj2 != 0` (`cract.cc:926`); the `Wait{100}` floor is the only single-use delay. `Move`/`Turn` are delay 0 (C++ `default` case, `cract.cc:946-948`). Tests: 3 unit (`todo_use_delay_ms` two-object within/past gate + single-object ungated) + 2 integration (two-object Use defers with wakeup at `earliest_multiuse_server_ms`; single-object Use does not defer, queue drains); 536 pass. *(F8-S3, `creature_todo.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `idle_stimulus_tests.rs`, July 2026)*

107. **F8 S4: `Result<(), ReturnValue>` refactor + `apply_todo_result_catch` — the C++ `RESULT` catch needs the error value, so executors must return it** (`cract.cc:870-889` `Execute` catch, `cract.cc:1026-1031` `ToDoYield`, `cract.cc:953-989` `ToDoClear`, `enums.hh:390-453` RESULT codes, `container_ui.rs:player_use_item`/`player_use_item_ex`, `game_world_player_throw.rs:player_move_thing`/`player_move_item`, `creature_todo.rs:apply_todo_result_catch`, `idle_stimulus.rs:execute_player_use`/`execute_player_move`): The C++ `Execute` drain wraps each executor in `try { ... } catch(RESULT r) { ... }` — the catch needs the `RESULT` value to decide `EXHAUSTED`→`ToDoWait(1000)` vs else→`ToDoYield`, and to send `SendResult`/`SendSnapback`. The existing Rust executors returned `()` and called `send_cancel_message` inline — losing the error value. **Refactor:** change `player_use_item`/`player_use_item_ex`/`player_move_thing`/`player_move_item` to return `Result<(), ReturnValue>`. Replace `self.send_cancel_message(conn_id, rv); return;` with `return Err(rv);`. Walk-to-reach deferral paths (`try_walk_to_and_action`) return `Ok(())` (deferred = no error to catch — transitional, S5 folds into `Go`-prepend). Reactive callers (`game_loop.rs`, `walk_action.rs`) wrap `Err(rv)` → `send_cancel_message` to preserve existing behavior until S7. **`apply_todo_result_catch`:** `player_todo_clear` (returns `had_pending_go`) → `EXHAUSTED`→`enqueue_creature_wait(1000)`+`todo_start_from_action(1000)`, else→`enqueue_creature_wait(0)`+`todo_start_from_action(0)` (= `ToDoYield`). Player-only: `send_cancel_message` (= `SendResult`) + conditional `encode_cancel_walk` (= `SendSnapback`) when `had_pending_go && !snapback_exempt`. **Snapback exemption:** `NOTINVITED`(50)/`ENTERPROTECTIONZONE`(48)/`MOVENOTPOSSIBLE`(52) skip snapback (`cract.cc:882-884`). 772 RESULT→Rust `ReturnValue` mapping is approximate — `MOVENOTPOSSIBLE`/`NOTTURNABLE`/`DESTROYED` have no exact variants; `NotPossible`/`ThereIsNoWay` are the existing convention (`walk/mod.rs:1506`). **New `player_rotate_item` executor** (`game_world_player_rotate.rs`): C++ `operate.cc:2562-2583` `Turn` + `cract.cc:771-777` `TCreature::Turn`. Re-validates object → `Err(NotPossible)` (DESTROYED/NOTACCESSIBLE); checks `rotatable()` → `Err(NotPossible)` (NOTTURNABLE); checks `rotate_to != 0`; transforms `item.item_type = rotate_to` (= `Change(Obj, RotateTarget, 0)`); broadcasts `0x6B` for map tiles. **`FLAG_ROTATABLE` is private** (`otb.rs:427`) — tests set the bit directly (`1u32 << 15`) since there's no public setter. **Queue state after catch:** `ToDoClear` wipes the queue, THEN `ToDoWait(N)` enqueues a single `Wait{N}` — so the post-catch queue has `[Wait{N}]`, NOT empty. Tests: 10 new (5 Turn executor + 3 RESULT catch + 2 Use/Move execute arms); 546 pass. *(F8-S4, `container_ui.rs`, `game_world_player_throw.rs`, `game_loop.rs`, `walk_action.rs`, `game_world_player_rotate.rs`, `creature_todo.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, July 2026)*

108. **F8 S5: Go-prepend walk-to-reach replaces bespoke `walk_action_due` for the ToDo flow — extract core executors so the ToDo arm skips the reactive ready-check + `try_walk_to_and_action`** (`cract.cc:600-760` `Use` executor walk-to-reach, `walk_action.rs:setup_player_walk_to_target`, `container_ui.rs:player_use_item_core`/`player_use_item_ex_core`, `idle_stimulus.rs:execute_creature_todo_action` Use/Move arms): The C++ `Use` executor checks adjacency at execute time; if not reachable, it prepends `ToDoGo(dest)` + re-enqueues `ToDoUse` + `ToDoStart` and returns — walk-to-reach is a `Go` entry in front of the action, not a special path. The Rust bespoke path (`walk_action`/`walk_action_due`/`try_walk_to_and_action`/`try_run_player_walk_action_from_todo`) was the pre-ToDo way of deferring walk-to-use. S5 replaces it for the ToDo flow with `Go`-prepend in the execute arm itself. **Three-part design:** **(1) `setup_player_walk_to_target`** (`walk_action.rs`) — path-finds via `get_creature_path_to(cid, target, 0, 1)` and sets up `walk_queue`/`walk_destinations` **without** `player_todo_clear_with_snapback` (unlike `player_auto_walk_path` which clears the ToDo queue). The execute arm calls this, then `push_front(Use)` + `push_front(Go)` → `[Go, Use, ...]`, then `todo_start_go_delay` + `schedule_immediate_todo_wakeup`. On no-path → `Err(ThereIsNoWay)` → `apply_todo_result_catch`. **(2) Core executor extraction** (`container_ui.rs`) — `player_use_item`/`player_use_item_ex` had the ready check (`player_use_item_ready`/`player_use_item_ex_ready`) + walk-to-reach (`try_walk_to_and_action`) baked in. After a `Go`-prepend walk, the walk timer (`earliest_walk_server_ms`) is set to the next step time — so `player_use_item_ready` would return false and defer via `defer_player_walk_action`, re-triggering the bespoke path (breaking S5's goal). Extracted `player_use_item_core`/`player_use_item_ex_core` (the logic after ready-check + walk-to-reach: teleport-floor-use + `try_open_container_for_item` + multiuse exhaust). `execute_player_use` calls the core helpers directly — the ToDo arm handles adjacency via `Go`-prepend and timing via `Wait{100}` + `CalculateDelay`, so the reactive-path gates are redundant. **(3) Execute arm adjacency check** — Use checks `look_distance_tfs(player_pos, obj1.pos) > 1` for map tiles; Move checks `dx > 1 || dy > 1`. Inventory/container sources (`pos.x == 0xFFFF`) are always "adjacent" (no walk needed). **Bespoke path retirement scope:** the ToDo execute arm no longer sets `walk_action`/`walk_action_due`, so `try_run_player_walk_action_from_todo` is a no-op for ToDo-routed actions. The reactive path (`container_ui.rs`/`game_world_player_throw.rs` calling `try_walk_to_and_action`/`defer_player_walk_action`) stays until S6 reroutes handlers to builders + S7 deletes dead code. `try_run_player_walk_action_from_todo` tests still pass (function intact, just no longer triggered by the real ToDo flow). **Move's `throw_dest_reachable_after_walk_to_item` pre-check** stays in `player_move_item` for the reactive path; on the ToDo path it's a no-op (player is adjacent after the walk, so `dx <= 1 && dy <= 1` skips the pre-check). Tests: 6 new (Use not-adjacent → `[Go, Use]`; Use adjacent → no Go; Use inventory → no Go; Move not-adjacent → `[Go, Move]`; Use no-path → RESULT catch; `setup_player_walk_to_target` doesn't clear ToDo); 552 pass. *(F8-S5, `walk_action.rs`, `container_ui.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `creature_todo.rs`, July 2026)*
109. **F8 S6: Handler routing — `Wait{100}` prefix means the S3 `todo_use_delay_ms` peek-based helper was built on a wrong assumption; the handler uses `todo_start_from_action(cid, 1)` and the execute arm applies the multiuse gate** (`receiving.cc:384/430/233/549` `CUseObject`/`CUseTwoObjects`/`CMoveObject`/`CTurnObject`, `cract.cc:901-960` `CalculateDelay`, `cract.cc:955-1024` `ToDoStart`, `cract.cc:993-1000` `ToDoAdd` preamble, `game_loop.rs:handle_game_packet`, `creature_todo.rs:todo_use_delay_ms`/`multiuse_gate_delay_ms`): S3 built `todo_use_delay_ms` (peek-based `CalculateDelay(TDUse)`) "for S6 handler routing," assuming the `Use` action sits at the queue front after enqueue. **This is wrong** because `enqueue_player_use` prepends `Wait{100}` — so after enqueue the queue is `[Wait{100}, Use{...}]` and the front is `Wait`, not `Use`. `todo_use_delay_ms` peeks the front, sees `Wait` (not `Use` with `obj2`), and returns 0 regardless of the multiuse gate. **Correct S6 routing:** the handler calls `todo_start_from_action(cid, 1)` (next beat — clamped to 1 for forward progress, `cract.cc:1016`). The `Wait{100}` entry, when popped by the execute drain, schedules the 100ms wakeup via its own `todo_start_from_action(cid, 100)`. The multiuse gate for two-object `Use` is applied by `multiuse_gate_delay_ms(cid, obj2.is_some())` in the execute arm when the `Use` action reaches the front (after `Wait` drains) — mirroring C++ `Execute` re-running `CalculateDelay` per entry (`cract.cc:795-801`). **Removed** `todo_use_delay_ms` + its 3 unit tests as dead code (the `multiuse_gate_delay_ms` core + 2 S3 integration tests still cover the gate). **`ToDoAdd` preamble:** every `ToDo*` builder runs `if(LockToDo) { ToDoClear(); if(pending Go) SendSnapback; }` before appending (`cract.cc:993-1000`). The S2 Rust builders (`enqueue_player_use`/`enqueue_player_move`/`enqueue_player_turn`) do NOT do this clear — the handler must, mirroring `player_set_attack_dest`'s `player_todo_clear_with_snapback` call. Each S6 rerouted arm calls it before the builder. **Gate removal:** `Throw`/`RotateItem` removed from the `game_packet_requires_timed_action` gated set (added to the exclusion list) — the ToDo engine's `Wait{100}` + `CalculateDelay` gate subsumes the receipt-time `player_packet_action_ready` gate, which would otherwise drop packets the ToDo engine would correctly queue. The now-dead `Throw` arm in `player_packet_action_ready` was removed. **1098 keeps the reactive path** (`!beat_driven_loop` branch in each arm) until Phase 2/3 unification — `RotateItem` has no 1098 executor either, so it traces (no-op) until then. Tests: 8 new (1 gate-exclusion + 7 handler-routing: UseItem/UseItemEx/Throw/RotateItem queue shape + wakeup armed; RotateItem 1098 no-op; builder failure → cancel + no enqueue; LookAt stays reactive); 557 pass. *(F8-S6, `game_loop.rs`, `game_world.rs`, `creature_todo.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, July 2026)*

110. **F8 S7: `walk_action` deferral is fully dead for 772 after S6 — `try_run_player_walk_action_from_todo` + the `had_walk_action` branch in `process_creature_todo` + the 772 `on_player_walk_complete` scheduling path are all dead code** (`cract.cc:783-898` `Execute`, `walk/mod.rs:process_creature_todo`, `walk_action.rs:try_run_player_walk_action_from_todo`/`on_player_walk_complete`, `container_ui.rs:player_use_item`/`player_use_item_ex`, `game_world_player_throw.rs:player_move_thing`): After S6 rerouted all 772 player actions (Use/Move/Turn) through ToDo builders at packet receipt, the `walk_action` field is **never set** for 772 players in production. The only setters are `set_next_walk_action_task` (called from `try_walk_to_and_action`) and `defer_player_walk_action` (called from the reactive executors' not-ready branches) — both are only reachable from `player_use_item`/`player_use_item_ex`/`player_move_thing`, which the 772 handler no longer calls. **Three dead pieces removed:** **(1)** The `had_walk_action` branch in `process_creature_todo` (walk/mod.rs:390-400) — checked `p.walk_action.is_some()` and dispatched to `try_run_player_walk_action_from_todo`; for 772 this was always false, so the branch never fired. **(2)** `try_run_player_walk_action_from_todo` (walk_action.rs:86-103) — the 772 ToDo drain hook; its only caller was the dead branch. **(3)** The 772 `schedule_creature_wakeup` path in `on_player_walk_complete` (walk_action.rs:50-54) — even if `walk_action` were set (tests only), scheduling a wakeup that fires `process_creature_todo` with no `walk_action` handler would leave the action stuck forever. Simplified `on_player_walk_complete` to early-return when `beat_driven_loop` (1098 path unchanged). **What stays for 1098:** `walk_action`/`walk_action_due` fields, `process_walk_action_tasks`, `on_player_walk_complete` (1098 branch), `defer_player_walk_action`, `try_walk_to_and_action`, `run_player_walk_action`, `set_next_walk_action_task`, `clear_player_walk_action` — all are still used by the 1098 reactive path (`handle_game_packet` `else` branches + `process_walk_action_tasks` drain). **Comment cleanup:** the "transitional — S5 folds" comments on `player_use_item`/`player_use_item_ex`/`player_move_thing` (`Ok(())` = walk-to-reach deferral) were updated to "1098 reactive path" — the deferral is permanent 1098 behavior, not transitional. The 772 ToDo path uses `Go`-prepend via `execute_player_use`/`execute_player_move` (calling `player_use_item_core`/`player_use_item_ex_core` directly, skipping the walk-to-reach). **grep verification:** the 772 (`if beat_driven_loop`) branches in `handle_game_packet` for `UseItem`/`UseItemEx`/`Throw`/`RotateItem` call only builders + `todo_start_from_action` — no inline executor calls. Tests: 2 new (`on_player_walk_complete` no-op for 772 even with planted `walk_action`; `process_creature_todo` ignores planted `walk_action` for 772 — doesn't consume it); 559 pass. *(F8-S7, `walk/mod.rs`, `walk_action.rs`, `container_ui.rs`, `game_world_player_throw.rs`, `idle_stimulus_tests.rs`, July 2026)*

111. **F8 D1 (decompile parity audit): `ToDoMove` builder ALWAYS prepends `Wait{100}` — the F5 doc comment conflated the *handler* (`CMoveObject`, no `ToDoWait`) with the *builder* (`ToDoMove`, always `this->ToDoWait(Delay)`) and dropped the 100 ms throw floor** (`cract.cc:1123-1172` `ToDoMove`, `cract.cc:1155` `int Delay = 100;`, `cract.cc:1165` `this->ToDoWait(Delay);`, `receiving.cc:233` `CMoveObject`, `creature_todo.rs:enqueue_player_move`, `game_loop.rs:handle_game_packet` `Throw` arm): The 772 `CMoveObject` *handler* calls `ToDoMove(...)` + `ToDoStart()` with no leading `ToDoWait` — true. But `ToDoMove` *itself* unconditionally calls `this->ToDoWait(Delay)` with `Delay = 100` for the non-creature-container path (`cract.cc:1155,1165`) before `ToDoAdd(TDMove)`. The F8 S2 Rust builder `enqueue_player_move` enqueued `Move` with **no** `Wait` prefix, citing the handler's lack of `ToDoWait` — conflating handler with builder. **Observable divergence:** a throw/move executed on the next beat (~1 ms via `todo_start_from_action(cid, 1)` clamp) instead of ~100 ms out, so items moved faster than the reference and rapid move packets had no pacing. **Fix:** `enqueue_player_move` now calls `enqueue_creature_wait(cid, 100)` before pushing `Move`, producing `[Wait{100}, Move]` — the same shape as `enqueue_player_use`/`enqueue_player_turn` (which were already correct). The `game_loop.rs` `CMoveObject` arm still calls `todo_start_from_action(cid, 1)` after enqueue (consistent with `Use`/`Turn`); the `Wait{100}` entry schedules the real 100 ms wakeup when drained. **Creature-container branch (D9) deliberately deferred:** `isCreatureContainer()` (pushing a body) uses `Delay = 1000` + a `BANK` destination check + `Combat.DelayAttack(2000)` — creature push isn't ported, so only the `Delay = 100` path fires today; the D9 path must be added when creature push lands. **Test fallout:** `enqueue_player_move_enqueues_single_move_no_wait` renamed to `enqueue_player_move_prepends_wait_then_move` (asserts `[Wait{100}, Move]`); `s5_move_not_adjacent_prepends_go_and_re_enqueues_move` now drains the `Wait{100}` first (one `execute_creature_todo_action` returning `Wait`) before the `Move` arm runs the `Go`-prepend — the queue after enqueue is `[Wait{100}, Move]`, after the Wait drains it's `[Move]`, then the not-adjacent arm produces `[Go, Move]`. **Lesson:** when a C++ *handler* calls a *builder*, the builder's own `ToDoWait`/`ToDoGo`/`ToDoClear` preamble is part of the observable queue shape — audit the builder, not just the handler. *(F8-D1, `creature_todo.rs`, `game_loop.rs`, `tasks/f8-decompile-parity-audit.md`, July 2026)*

112. **F8 D3 (decompile parity audit): `ToDoTurn` builder's `ObjectInRange(1)` → `ToDoGo(...)` walk-to-reach was missing from the Rust `Turn` execute arm — distant rotates failed with a cancel instead of walking the player adjacent first** (`cract.cc:1326-1351` `ToDoTurn`, `cract.cc:1340-1341` `if(!ObjectInRange(this->ID, Obj, 1)) this->ToDoGo(ObjX, ObjY, ObjZ, false, INT_MAX);`, `idle_stimulus.rs` `CreatureAction::Turn` execute arm, `game_world_player_rotate.rs:player_rotate_item`, `creature_todo.rs:enqueue_player_turn`): The 772 `ToDoTurn` builder resolves the object, then for map-tile sources (`ObjX != 0xFFFF`) checks `ObjectInRange(ID, Obj, 1)` and prepends `ToDoGo(...)` when the player isn't adjacent, *then* `ToDoWait(100)` + `ToDoAdd(TDTurn)`. The F8 S4 Rust `Turn` execute arm called `player_rotate_item` directly with no walk-to-reach, and `player_rotate_item`'s own range check (`look_distance_tfs > 1`) returned `Err(NotPossible)` for any object > 1 tile away — so rotating a rotatable object (wall torch/rope) across the room **failed with a cancel** in Rust, whereas the reference walks the player adjacent and then rotates. This was the clearest behavioral regression of the three F8 builders (Use/Move got the S5 `Go`-prepend; Turn didn't). **Fix:** the `Turn` execute arm now mirrors the S5 `Move` arm shape — `needs_walk = obj.pos.x != 0xFFFF && (dx>1 || dy>1)` (same-z Chebyshev, matching `ObjectInRange(1)` for same-z; the Δz case is D2/D6, separate); on `needs_walk` it calls `setup_player_walk_to_target(cid, obj.pos, now)`, on `Ok` pushes `[Turn, Go]` to the front (`push_front(Turn)` then `push_front(Go)` → `[Go, Turn, ...]`) + `todo_start_go_delay(cid, true)` + `schedule_immediate_todo_wakeup`, on `Err(rv)` applies `apply_todo_result_catch` (C++ `RESULT` catch, `cract.cc:870-889`). The adjacent/inventory path still dispatches to `player_rotate_item` directly. **Predicate choice:** the `Move` arm's `dx>1 || dy>1` was used (not the `Use` arm's `look_distance_tfs > 1`) because the audit's D3 remediation explicitly specifies it and it matches `ObjectInRange(1)` for same-z without the `+15` Δz fold that D6 flags. **Builder doc comment** updated to note the walk-to-reach is deferred to the execute arm (same shape as `Use`/`Move`) and the Δz `UPSTAIRS`/`DOWNSTAIRS` throw is D2/D6 (not yet ported). **Tests:** 3 new mirroring the S5 Move tests — `s5_turn_not_adjacent_prepends_go_and_re_enqueues_turn` (drains `Wait{100}` first, then asserts `[Go, Turn]` + populated `walk_queue`), `s5_turn_adjacent_does_not_go_prepend` (asserts direct rotate → item transforms to `rotate_to` + empty queue/walk_queue), `s5_turn_no_path_to_target_applies_result_catch` (isolated tile → `Err(ThereIsNoWay)` → RESULT catch → `[Wait{0}]`). 533 pass. **Lesson:** when porting a family of builders that share a C++ preamble (`GetObject` → z-check → `ObjectInRange` → `ToDoGo` → `ToDoWait` → `ToDoAdd`), port the *whole* preamble to every arm — the S5 `Go`-prepend was added to `Use`/`Move` but skipped for `Turn` because the `Turn` executor (`player_rotate_item`) was new code with its own range check, masking the missing walk-to-reach. The executor's range check is a *fallback*, not a replacement for the builder's walk-to-reach. *(F8-D3, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `creature_todo.rs`, `tasks/f8-decompile-parity-audit.md`, July 2026)*
113. **772 floor-change desync Phase 0: golden tests lock the spurious self-creature packet as the sole wire divergence — the map body (floor descriptions + edge rows) is byte-identical between the Rust 1098 path and 772 `NotifyGo`'s `SendFloors`/`SendRow`** (`docs/772_FLOOR_CHANGE_DESYNC.md` §14 Phase 0, `cract.cc:1400-1460` `NotifyGo`, `sending.cc:517-573` `SendFloors`, `sending.cc:463-510` `SendRow`, `map_description.rs:671-889` `send_move_creature_player`, `map_description.rs:474-666` `append_move_up/down_creature`, `protocol_compat.rs` `v1098_floor_change`/`v772_floor_change` modules): Phase 0 golden tests were added to `protocol_compat.rs` as two modules. **`v1098_floor_change` (5 tests, passing — regression guard):** `hole_down_in_place` (z=7→8, `0x6C`+`0xBF`+`0x66`+`0x67`, len=20), `ladder_up_in_place` (z=8→7, `0x6D`+`0xBE`+`0x68`+`0x65`, len=31), `stairs_down_diag` (z=7→8+dy=1, `0x6C`+`0xBF`+`0x66`+2×`0x67`, len=23), `same_z_east` (z=7→7+dx=1, `0x6D`+`0x66`, len=15), `teleport_map_description` (`0x64`+pos+empty body, len=22). **`v772_floor_change` (5 tests, 4 failing until Phase 1 — the bug):** the 4 move tests use `assert_772_matches_1098_minus_self_packet` which calls `send_move_creature_player` with both codecs, strips the leading self-packet from the 1098 output, and asserts the 772 output equals the stripped result — proving the map body is identical and only the self-packet diverges. The 5th test (teleport map-description parity) passes because `send_map_description_packet` (`0x64`) is era-independent for an empty map. **Key implementation detail:** all tests use an empty map (`get_tile` → `None`) for deterministic skip-compression bytes — the skip counter increments per empty tile, flushes `0xFF,0xFF` at every 256-tile boundary, and writes a final `skip,0xFF` at the end. The self-packet length depends on the move type: `0x6C` remove (surface→underground) = 7 bytes (opcode+pos+stack), `0x6D` move (other z-change or same-z) = 12 bytes (opcode+old_pos+stack+new_pos). **Lesson:** the `write_map_description_body` z-loop iterates ALL visible floors (8 for surface z≤7, 5 for underground z>7), not just the target floor — so the edge rows (`0x66`/`0x67`/`0x68`/`0x65`) carry all visible floors' tiles, making the row body larger than a single-floor width×height. The `append_move_down_creature`'s `0xBF` floor body iterates 3 floors (8,9,10) with a shared skip counter (756 tiles → 2 flushes + final), while `append_move_up_creature`'s `0xBE` iterates 6 floors (5→0) with a shared skip (1512 tiles → 5 flushes + final). *(772-Phase0, `protocol_compat.rs`, `map_description.rs`, `docs/772_FLOOR_CHANGE_DESYNC.md`, July 2026)*
114. **772 floor-change desync Phases 1–4: `move_creature_self_packet` capability gate + `Option<NetworkMessage>` spectator fallback + Phase 3 unreachability proof via `are_in_range_1_1_0`** (`docs/772_FLOOR_CHANGE_DESYNC.md` §14 Phases 1–4, `cract.cc:1400-1460` `NotifyGo`, `sending.cc:421-460` `SendFullScreen`, `sending.cc:658-700` `SendMoveCreature`, `protocol_version.rs:47` `ProtocolCaps`, `map_description.rs:668-940` `send_move_creature_player`/`send_move_creature_spectator`, `walk/mod.rs:993-1042` `emit_teleport_move_packet`, `walk/mod.rs:1089-1110` `broadcast_spectator_move`): **Phase 1** added `move_creature_self_packet: bool` to `ProtocolCaps` (false for 772, true for 1098) and gated the leading self-packet (`0x6D`/`0x6C`) in `send_move_creature_player` on it — both the z-change branch (0x6C remove for surface→underground, 0x6D move for other z-changes) and the same-z branch (0x6D). The floor/row emission (`append_move_down/up_creature` + outer `0x65-0x68` rows) is unchanged — it already matches 772 `NotifyGo`'s `SendFloors`/`SendRow` byte-for-byte (§16.1). All 5 `v772_floor_change` golden tests now pass (were 4 failing). **Phase 2** skipped the `sendRemoveTileCreature` (`0x6C`) step in `emit_teleport_move_packet` when `!move_creature_self_packet` — 772 `SendFullScreen` emits `0x64` alone with no remove. The map description (`0x64`) is era-independent. **Phase 3** verified the both-axes diagonal+z case (e.g. `(100,100,7)→(101,101,8)`) is **unreachable** via the walk path: `are_in_range_1_1_0` requires `dz == 0`, so ANY z-change (height climbing, queryDestination chain) makes the segment a teleport → `emit_teleport_move_packet`, never `send_move_creature_player`. The row-ordering divergence (772: x-steps then y-steps; Rust: append's fixed col+row then outer N/S-before-E/W) only exists in `send_move_creature_player`'s z-change branch, which is dead code for the walk path. A companion test (`are_in_range_1_1_0_rejects_z_change_confirming_both_axes_z_unreachable`) locks this invariant. **Phase 4** made `send_move_creature_spectator` era-aware by taking `&Codec` and returning `Option<NetworkMessage>`: 772 returns `None` when `stack >= 10` or invalid (772 `SendMoveCreature`'s `WasVisible=false` → `SendAddField` appear fallback, no `0xFFFF+id` form); 1098 keeps the `0xFFFF+creature_id` fallback. The call site in `broadcast_spectator_move` handles `None` by falling back to `send_creature_appear_to_conn`. **Lesson:** the walk path's `are_in_range_1_1_0` (dz==0) teleport detection means `send_move_creature_player`'s z-change branch is never exercised in production — it's only tested by golden unit tests. The 772 `NotifyGo` adjacent path (DistanceZ ≤ 1) uses `SendFloors`+`SendRow` for z-changes, but the Rust walk path routes all z-changes to the teleport path (`0x64` full screen). This is a heavier payload than 772's incremental floor update but is correct (no desync) — the doc accepts this tradeoff. *(772-Phase1-4, `protocol_version.rs`, `map_description.rs`, `walk/mod.rs`, `protocol_compat.rs`, `map_description.rs` tests, July 2026)*
115. **772 floor-change desync same-z regression: the 772 client PROCESSES `0x6D` for self same-z moves — the doc's §10 "likely ignored" assumption was wrong** (`docs/772_FLOOR_CHANGE_DESYNC.md` §10, `cract.cc:1400-1460` `NotifyGo`, `map_description.rs:814-826` `send_move_creature_player` same-z branch): Phase 1 originally gated the self-packet (`0x6D`) for ALL 772 moves — both z-changes and same-z. Live client testing (2026-07-04) revealed an immediate regression: every same-z step desynced, the player couldn't move at all. The doc's §10 assumption that "the extra `0x6D` is likely ignored by the 772 client (creature ID == self → skip)" was **wrong**. The 772 client **processes** `0x6D` for self same-z moves — it uses the move packet to update the player's internal position tracking. Without it, `SendRow` (`0x66`) shifts the viewport but the player sprite desyncs from the camera on every step. **Fix:** the same-z branch in `send_move_creature_player` now always emits `0x6D` for both eras (no `emit_self_packet` gate). The z-change branch keeps the gate (772 suppresses `0x6D`/`0x6C` for z-changes only). The double-shift (§1) self-corrects for same-z moves, so keeping `0x6D` is safe. **Why 772 `NotifyGo` doesn't send `0x6D` for same-z:** the 772 server's `NotifyGo` uses `SendRow` which apparently includes an implicit self-position update that the Rust `0x66` row emission doesn't replicate (the bytes match but the client's row handler may behave differently when no preceding `0x6D` sets the position). Rather than reverse-engineer the exact client row-handler semantics, keeping the `0x6D` is the pragmatic fix — it works, the double-shift self-corrects, and it matches 1098 behavior. **Test update:** `same_z_east_no_self_packet` → `same_z_east_keeps_self_packet` (asserts 772 == 1098 for same-z, both emit `0x6D` + `0x66`). *(772-Phase1-same-z-fix, `map_description.rs`, `protocol_compat.rs`, `docs/772_FLOOR_CHANGE_DESYNC.md`, July 2026)*
116. **772 floor-change desync teleport detection: 772 `NotifyGo` uses `DistanceZ <= 1` for the adjacent path (SendFloors/SendRow), but Rust used 1098's `areInRange<1,1,0>` (dz==0) for BOTH eras — routing ALL z-changes to the full-screen 0x64 teleport path instead of the incremental 0xBE/0xBF + 0x65-0x68 floor update** (`cract.cc:1421` `NotifyGo` adjacent condition, `map.cpp:262` 1098 `areInRange<1,1,0>`, `walk/mod.rs:77-110` `are_in_range_1_1_0`/`are_in_range_1_1_1`/`is_adjacent_move`, `walk/mod.rs:1845` `initial_teleport`, `walk/mod.rs:1888` `chain_teleport`): The 772 `NotifyGo` has TWO paths: **(1) adjacent** (`DistanceX <= 1 && DistanceY <= 1 && DistanceZ <= 1`) → `SendFloors` (0xBE/0xBF) + `SendRow` (0x65-0x68) — an incremental floor update that shifts the viewport one floor and adds edge rows; **(2) teleport** (`DistanceZ > 1` or dx/dy > 1) → `SendFullScreen` (0x64) — a full viewport reset. The 1098 TFS `Map::moveCreature` uses `areInRange<1,1,0>` (dz==0) — ALL z-changes are teleports (full screen 0x64). The Rust code used 1098's `are_in_range_1_1_0` for BOTH eras, routing every 772 adjacent z-change (stairs, ladders, holes — the most common floor changes) through `emit_teleport_move_packet` (0x64 full screen). The 772 client expects `SendFloors`/`SendRow` for adjacent z-changes — getting a 0x64 full screen reset instead desyncs the viewport because the client's floor-change rendering pipeline handles 0xBE/0xBF (incremental shift) differently from 0x64 (complete reset). **Fix:** added `are_in_range_1_1_1` (dz ≤ 1) and `is_adjacent_move(codec, a, b)` which dispatches on `move_creature_self_packet`: 772 → `are_in_range_1_1_1` (adjacent z-changes are NOT teleports), 1098 → `are_in_range_1_1_0` (z-changes ARE teleports). Both `initial_teleport` and `chain_teleport` detection now use `is_adjacent_move`. This routes 772 adjacent z-changes through `send_move_creature_player` (which has the 0xBE/0xBF + rows branch, already verified byte-identical to 772 `NotifyGo` in §16.1). **Phase 3 re-evaluation:** the both-axes+z diagonal case (dx=1, dy=1, dz=1) IS now reachable for 772 — it routes through `send_move_creature_player`'s z-change branch, which has a row-ordering divergence from `NotifyGo` (§16.3). This is accepted: the row content is byte-identical, only the sequence differs, and the desync is minor and self-correcting. *(772-teleport-detection-fix, `walk/mod.rs`, `protocol_compat.rs`, `docs/772_FLOOR_CHANGE_DESYNC.md`, July 2026)*
117. **772 TVP vs decompile: TVP sends `0x6D` for self-moves (1098 pattern) but the real 772 client does NOT track the player's own creature in its tile map — `0x6D` at the player's position triggers "MoveCreature has been received for a coordinate where there is no creature anymore [bug000017]"** (`cract.cc:1400-1460` `NotifyGo`, `tvp-772/gameserver/src/protocolgame.cpp:1796` `sendMoveCreature`, `map_description.rs:814-826` `send_move_creature_player` same-z branch): TVP's `sendMoveCreature` for `creature == player` sends `0x6D` (line 1796) — this is the 1098 TFS pattern. The decompile's `NotifyGo` never sends `0x6D` for self-moves — it uses `SendFloors` (0xBE/0xBF) + `SendRow` (0x65-0x68) exclusively. Live client debugging (2026-07-04) confirmed the decompile is authoritative: the real 772 client logs **"MoveCreature has been received for a coordinate where there is no creature anymore [bug000017]"** when it receives `0x6D` for a self-move. The source position (player's own tile) has no creature entry because the client doesn't store the player as a creature in its tile map. The `SendRow` packets (0x65-0x68) are the client's implicit self-move signal — the client shifts its viewport and updates its internal position from the row direction. **TVP was designed for OTClient** (which handles self `0x6D` differently — it tracks all creatures including self), not the real 772 client. **Earlier misdiagnosis:** when we first suppressed `0x6D` for all self-moves, the user reported "can't move at all" on same-z steps, so we reverted to keeping `0x6D` for same-z. This was wrong — the real issue was the teleport detection (z-changes routing to 0x64 full-screen instead of SendFloors/SendRow), which was fixed separately with era-aware `is_adjacent_move`. With both fixes in place, same-z moves emit only `SendRow` (matching the decompile), and z-changes route through `send_move_creature_player`'s 0xBE/0xBF + rows branch (also matching the decompile). **Lesson:** when a "can't move" report follows a multi-part fix, isolate which part caused the regression before reverting — the self-packet suppression was correct but was masked by the teleport detection bug. Also: TVP is the primary wire reference for 772 opcodes and field formats, but NOT for self-move packet structure — the decompile owns that because TVP inherits TFS's 1098-era `sendMoveCreature` architecture. *(772-tvp-vs-decompile-self-move, `map_description.rs`, `protocol_compat.rs`, `docs/772_FLOOR_CHANGE_DESYNC.md`, July 2026)*
