118. **772 floor-change desync root cause was teleport detection, NOT the self-packet — TVP sends `0x6D`/`0x6C` for self-moves on both eras and works fine on the real 772 client; the self-packet is REQUIRED to update the client's central position before floor change parsing** (`tvp-772/gameserver/src/protocolgame.cpp:1791-1828` `sendMoveCreature` self path, `cract.cc:1400-1460` `NotifyGo`, `walk/mod.rs:1845` `is_adjacent_move`, `map_description.rs:687-713` z-change branch, `walk/mod.rs:1015-1075` `emit_teleport_move_packet`): The original Phase 1 hypothesis was that the `0x6D`/`0x6C` self-packet caused the 772 floor-change desync (the "double-shift" theory). This was **wrong**. TVP sends `0x6D` for self same-z moves (line 1796) and `0x6D`/`0x6C` for self z-changes (lines 1793-1805) on both eras, and TVP works fine on the real 772 client. The `bug000017` log ("MoveCreature has been received for a coordinate where there is no creature anymore") is a **debug warning**, not a desync cause. The REAL root cause was the **teleport detection**: the Rust walk path used 1098's `areInRange<1,1,0>` (dz==0) for BOTH eras, routing ALL 772 z-changes through `emit_teleport_move_packet` (0x64 full screen) instead of `send_move_creature_player` (0xBE/0xBF + SendRow). The 772 client expects `SendFloors`/`SendRow` for adjacent z-changes — getting a 0x64 full screen reset desyncs because the client's floor-change rendering handles 0xBE/0xBF (incremental shift) differently from 0x64 (complete reset). **The self-packet is REQUIRED**: it updates the client's central position BEFORE the floor change (0xBE/0xBF) is processed. Without it, the client uses the OLD position when parsing the floor description, placing tiles at wrong coordinates → "no thing at pos" errors on subsequent tile updates (confirmed by OTClient debugging 2026-07-04). **Fix:** (1) era-aware `is_adjacent_move` — 772 uses `areInRange<1,1,1>` (dz ≤ 1), 1098 uses `areInRange<1,1,0>` (dz==0); (2) always emit self-packet for both eras (matching TVP); (3) teleport remove packet matches TVP — skip only when `newPos.z == 8 && oldPos.z == 7` (surface→underground), not for all 772 teleports; (4) spectator `send_move_creature_spectator` always returns `Some` with `0xFFFF + creatureID` fallback for stack >= 10 (TVP line 1844-1845), not `None`. **The `move_creature_self_packet` cap is now unused for packet gating** — it remains in `ProtocolCaps` but no longer controls any wire behavior. **Lesson:** when a multi-part fix causes a regression, isolate which part caused it before reverting — the self-packet suppression was wrongly reverted (causing the z-change desync to persist) when the real issue was the teleport detection. Also: TVP is the primary wire reference for 772 — the decompile's `NotifyGo` omits the self-packet but both paths work; TVP's approach is simpler and proven on the real client. *(772-tvp-alignment, `map_description.rs`, `walk/mod.rs`, `protocol_compat.rs`, `map_description.rs` tests, `docs/772_FLOOR_CHANGE_DESYNC.md`, July 2026)*

119. **772 floor-change OTClient vs real client dispatch: OTClient-on-772 must use TVP teleport path (remove + 0x64), real 772 client uses decompile NotifyGo incremental path (0x6D + SendFloors/SendRow) — the era-only dispatch was wrong, per-connection fork required** (`docs/772_FLOOR_CHANGE_CLIENT_TARGETS.md` §6, `tvp-772/gameserver/src/protocolgame.cpp:1766-1829` `sendMoveCreature` self teleport path, `cract.cc:1400-1465` `NotifyGo`, `walk/mod.rs:97-122` `is_adjacent_move`, `walk/mod.rs:1739-1790` walk dispatch, `walk/mod.rs:1945-1958` OTClient flag threading, `creature/player.rs:182-191` `is_otclient`, `protocol_compat.rs:1170-1190` `west_vs_north_onto_south_stairs_differ`): The era-only dispatch (`is_772 = !codec.caps().move_creature_self_packet`) routed ALL 772 connections through `emit_notify_go` (decompile incremental `SendFloors`/`SendRow`). This works for the real 7.72 client but **breaks OTClient**: OTClient tracks the local player as a tile creature, and the leading `0x6D` self-packet in `emit_notify_go` pre-jumps the self to the final tile, then the incremental `SendFloors`/`SendRow` stream (designed for the decompile's step-by-step `posx/posy/posz` update) can't reconcile the already-jumped position. The perpendicular-approach stair case (walking west onto south-facing stairs: overall `(101,100,8)→(100,101,7)`, dx=-1, dy=+1, dz=-1) leaves a leftover delta on **both** axes after the diagonal z-shift, producing a row sequence OTClient can't map → concrete error. The parallel approach (north onto south stairs: `(100,101,8)→(100,101,7)`, dx=0, dy=0, dz=-1) shifts along the stair's own axis, so the row sequence stays consistent enough that OTClient doesn't throw — explaining the direction-dependent repro. **Fix:** (1) added `Player::is_otclient()` (mirrors `item_with_description` seam: `otclient_v8 != 0 || operating_system >= CLIENTOS_OTCLIENT_LINUX`); (2) `is_adjacent_move` now takes `otclient: bool` — OTClient-on-772 uses `areInRange<1,1,0>` (dz==0, z-changes are teleports, matching TVP `protocolgame.cpp:1766-1829`), real 772 client uses `areInRange<1,1,1>` (dz≤1, adjacent z-changes are incremental, matching decompile `NotifyGo`); (3) the walk dispatch at `mod.rs:~1750` branches on `use_notify_go = is_772 && !is_otclient` — OTClient-on-772 routes through the per-segment TVP teleport path (`emit_teleport_move_packet`: remove + `0x64` full screen), real 772 client keeps `emit_notify_go`; (4) `internal_move_creature_step` threads the OTClient flag into both `initial_teleport` and `chain_teleport` detection. The §6 experiment in `772_FLOOR_CHANGE_CLIENT_TARGETS.md` that concluded "no per-connection fork is needed" was wrong — it only tested suppressing the self-packet while keeping the incremental body, never testing the TVP teleport path (remove + `0x64`) which is what OTClient actually needs. **Tests:** `otclient_772_west_onto_south_stairs_uses_teleport_path` (asserts 0x6C + 0x64, no 0xBE/0xBF), `real_772_client_west_onto_south_stairs_uses_notify_go` (asserts 0xBE/0xBF embedded in NotifyGo packet, no 0x64), updated `era_aware_teleport_detection_routes_z_changes_correctly` for new `is_adjacent_move(codec, otclient, a, b)` signature. 573 pass. *(772-otclient-floor-change-dispatch, `walk/mod.rs`, `creature/player.rs`, `idle_stimulus_tests.rs`, `docs/772_FLOOR_CHANGE_CLIENT_TARGETS.md`, July 2026)*

120. **772 floor-change wire order: 0x6B chain turn emitted BEFORE move packets — must be deferred to AFTER move packets to match C++ order** (`map.cpp:316` `sendMoveCreature`, `game.cpp:888` `internalCreatureTurn`, `walk/mod.rs:2200-2211` step 3 turn, `walk/mod.rs:1767-1791` move packet emission, `walk/mod.rs:320-377` `internal_creature_turn_with_broadcast`): The post-queryDestination chain turn (`0x6B`) was emitted inside `internal_move_creature_step` at step 3, which runs BEFORE the move packets are emitted in `on_walk`. C++ order is the opposite: `Map::moveCreature` sends `sendMoveCreature` (remove + `0x64` or `0x6D`) during the move loop (`map.cpp:316`), THEN `Game::internalMoveCreature` calls `internalCreatureTurn` → `sendCreatureTurn` (`0x6B`) after the loop (`game.cpp:888`). The Rust ordering caused the client to receive `0x6B` for a position it hasn't seen the creature move to yet — the creature is still at the old position in the client's tile map, so `getMappedThing` fails → "no thing at pos" error. **Fix:** split `internal_creature_turn_with_broadcast` into two functions: (1) `internal_creature_turn_with_broadcast` — sets direction AND broadcasts (used by `try_creature_walk_step` for push callers that emit moves immediately); (2) `internal_creature_turn_broadcast_only` — broadcasts `0x6B` without mutating direction (used by `on_walk` after move packets). `internal_move_creature_step` now sets the direction immediately (matching C++ state mutation order) and returns `Option<PendingChainTurn>`; `on_walk` emits the `0x6B` via `internal_creature_turn_broadcast_only` AFTER `emit_teleport_move_packet`/`emit_notify_go`/`broadcast_spectator_move`. `try_creature_walk_step` flushes the pending turn immediately after `internal_move_creature_step` returns (push callers don't have a separate move-packet phase). **Test:** `otclient_772_chain_turn_emitted_after_move_packets` asserts `0x6B` packet index > `0x64` packet index. 575 pass. *(772-wire-order-chain-turn, `walk/mod.rs`, `idle_stimulus_tests.rs`, July 2026)*

121. **772 splash/pool layer mismatch — OTB `FLAG_ALWAYSONTOP` on group-11 splashes is wrong for our tile model; runtime override routes splashes to `down_items` matching 772 `objects.srv` `BOTTOM`** (`docs/772_SPLASH_LAYER_MISMATCH.md`, `reference/cipsoft-772/runtime/dat/objects.srv` TypeID 2886/2889 `Flags={Bottom,LiquidPool,...}`, `enums.hh:206` `BOTTOM=2`/`TOP=3`, `tibia-game-master/src/operate.cc:2585-2619` `CreatePool`, `crates/tfs-rust-content/src/otb.rs:265-280` `parse_node`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/creature/monster_inventory.rs:481-575` `create_liquid_splash_772`): Our OTB flags splashes (group 11) with `FLAG_ALWAYSONTOP` + `alwaysOnTopOrder=2`, the same vector/order as ladders/signs/borders → both route to `top_items`. 772 `objects.srv` flags pools `BOTTOM` (below creatures) — a different layer from ladders (`TOP`). The mismatch forced `create_liquid_splash_772` to use a TFS-style "no splash in ladders" guard (skip placing if any top item shares `splash_order`) instead of the decompile's `CreatePool` `BOTTOM`-scan algorithm. Observable consequence: 772 allows blood splashes on ladder tiles; we blocked them (opposite behavior). **Fix (Option A — runtime override, unconditional, both eras):** (1) `otb::parse_node` clears `FLAG_ALWAYSONTOP` on `GROUP_SPLASH` items before `db.insert` — splashes route to `down_items` via the existing `always_on_top()` routing in `map/mod.rs:170` and `game_world_item_cylinder.rs:291`; (2) `create_liquid_splash_772` rewritten to mirror decompile `CreatePool`: single pass over `down_items` collecting existing splashes for replacement and detecting non-splash conflict (corpse/drop → silent `NOROOM` abort, matching `crmain.cc:222`/`:772`); deletes old pools then `internal_add_item_to_tile` places the new splash in `down_items`; (3) the `splash_order`/`alwaysOnTopOrder` ladder guard is deleted (dead code — splashes and ladders no longer share `top_items`). `top_items` (ladders/signs/borders) are NOT scanned → blood-on-ladders works. `is_splash()` (group check) is unaffected and remains the wire-encoding fluid-color signal. `get_item_stack_pos` already counts `top_items` before `down_items` and `map_description.rs` iterates top → creatures → down, so splashes now render after creatures = 772 `BOTTOM` order (correct). **Why not era-gated:** moving splashes to `down_items` is correct for both eras — matches 772 `BOTTOM` and produces the same observable result as 1098 TFS `Tile::addThing` (splashes render below creatures either way; the ladder guard becomes unnecessary because ladders are in a different vector). The override applies unconditionally at OTB load time. **Audit:** only two `always_on_top()` call sites (`map/mod.rs:170`, `game_world_item_cylinder.rs:291`) — both are routing decisions that now correctly send splashes to `down_items`; all `top_items` iterators either chain `down_items` (so splashes are still found) or are stack-pos calculations (correctly exclude splashes now). **Tests:** `test_splash_lands_in_down_items_with_ladder_present`, `test_splash_replaces_existing_pool`, `test_splash_aborts_when_corpse_present`, `test_death_pool_and_corpse_coexist_in_down_items`. 550 pass. *(772-splash-layer-override, `otb.rs`, `monster_inventory.rs`, July 2026)*

122. **Chat CH-0: 772 vs 1098 outgoing wire divergence for `sendChannel`/`sendCreatePrivateChannel` — 772 omits user/invited name lists that 1098 appends** (`reference/tvp-772/gameserver/src/protocolgame.cpp:1297` `sendChannel`, `:1273` `sendCreatePrivateChannel`; repo-root `src/protocolgame.cpp:1702` `sendChannel`, `:1675` `sendCreatePrivateChannel`; `crates/tfs-rust-net/src/codec/{wire.rs,v772.rs,v1098.rs,mod.rs}`): The 5 missing outgoing chat functions from the plan's §0.2 table are NOT all era-identical. `sendChannelsDialog` (`0xAB`), `sendClosePrivate` (`0xB3`), and `sendOpenPrivateChannel` (`0xAD`) share the same byte layout across 772 and 1098 — these stay as free functions in `outgoing_extra.rs`. But `sendChannel` (`0xAC`) and `sendCreatePrivateChannel` (`0xB2`) diverge: 1098 appends `u16 usersCount + [string userName]* + u16 invitedCount + [string invitedName]*` (and `sendCreatePrivateChannel` prepends a single `u16(1) + string ownerName` entry), while 772 stops after the channel name with no user/invited lists at all. The pre-existing `send_channel_open`/`send_create_private_channel` free functions in `outgoing_extra.rs` were 1098-shaped (wrote trailing `u16(0)+u16(0)` counts) — they would have sent extra bytes the 772 client doesn't expect. **Fix:** route the 3 divergent/structured functions (`sendChannelsDialog`, `sendChannel`, `sendCreatePrivateChannel`) through the `ProtocolCodec` seam with neutral wire structs (`ChannelsDialogWire`, `ChannelOpenWire`, `CreatePrivateChannelWire`) carrying max-width fields; `Codec772` ignores the `users`/`invited`/`owner_name` fields, `Codec1098` emits them. The era-identical functions stay as free functions using the new `protocol_opcodes::server` constants. The old 1098-shaped free functions are `#[deprecated]` (retained only for legacy test pinning, no call sites). **§4.5 resolved:** divergence is isolated to `codec::v772`/`codec::v1098` — no `if version == 772` in core, matching `TFS-wire-codec.md`. **Tests:** 6 golden-byte tests (3 per era) in `protocol_compat.rs`; 74 total pass. *(chat-ch0-wire-divergence, `codec/wire.rs`, `codec/v772.rs`, `codec/v1098.rs`, `outgoing_extra.rs`, July 2026)*

123. **PC-2 player melee strike — `combat_execute_with_stimulus` removes the target on death, so `hp_after` reads via `contains_key` not `unwrap_or(hp_before)`** (`crcombat.cc:647` `CloseAttack`, `crmain.cc:486` `Damage` → `apply_creature_death`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/combat/strike.rs:114-145`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/game_world_lifecycle.rs:167` `apply_creature_death`): The first strike implementation read `hp_after = self.creatures.get(target_id).map(|k| k.base().health).unwrap_or(hp_before)`. This is wrong because `combat_execute_with_stimulus` calls `apply_creature_death` when HP ≤ 0, which removes the creature from `world.creatures` — so `.get()` returns `None` and `unwrap_or(hp_before)` returns the pre-strike HP, hiding the death. The `damage_done` calculation then yields 0 (no HP delta), `ActivateLearning` never fires on the killing blow, and the `StopAttack` branch (gated on `hp_after <= 0`) never triggers. **Fix:** check `target_alive = self.creatures.contains_key(target_id)` after the stimulus call; if missing, the target died on this strike → `hp_after = 0`, `damage_done = hp_before`, `ActivateLearning` fires, and `StopAttack` clears `attack_target`/`follow_target`. **Test:** `strike_kills_target_stops_attack_and_activates_learning` arms a 1-HP target + huge fist attack, loops strikes until death, asserts `attack_target == None` and `learning_points == 30`. *(pc2-strike-death-detection, `player/combat/strike.rs`, July 2026)*

124. **PC-2 world-aware defense snapshot — `melee_defense_snapshot` was fist-only for player targets; `melee_defense_snapshot_for(target_id)` routes player targets through `player_get_defend_value` + `player_get_armor_strength`** (`crcombat.cc:191` `GetDefendValue`, `:286` `GetArmorStrength`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/creature/monster_combat.rs:246-285` `melee_defense_snapshot_for`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/monster_ai.rs:396,484`): The pre-PC-2 `melee_defense_snapshot(&CreatureKind)` helper reads `Monster.defense`/`Monster.armor`/`Monster.melee_skill` for monsters, but for player targets it fell back to a fist-only stub (no shield/weapon defend, no shielding skill, no armor). This meant a monster striking a player resolved defense/armor as if the player were bare-fisted — wrong outcome. **Fix:** add `GameWorld::melee_defense_snapshot_for(target_id)` that dispatches on `CreatureKind`: player targets use `player_get_defend_value` (PC-1 — shield/weapon defend + shielding skill per `GetDefendValue` priority) + `player_get_armor_strength` (full equipped armor sum); monster/NPC targets delegate to the existing `melee_defense_snapshot`. Both `monster_ai.rs` strike call sites (melee + spell-with-melee) switched to the world-aware version. **Test:** `strike_player_target_snapshot_uses_shield_defense` asserts snapshot `defense_value == 5` (fist fallback) without shield, `== 22` (shield defense) with shield equipped. *(pc2-player-target-defense-snapshot, `creature/monster_combat.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, July 2026)*

125. **PC-2 `LearningPoints` window vs `Increase(1)` skill-exp — PC-2 wires the gate, PC-5 wires the exp accumulation** (`crskill.cc:535` `ProbeValue`, `:549` `LearningPoints--` + `Increase(1)`, `crcombat.cc:526` `ActivateLearning`, `crcombat.cc:655` `if (DamageDone > 0) ActivateLearning()`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/combat/strike.rs:84-105`, `tasks/player-combat-plan.md` §0.5): The 772 skill-advancement system has two halves: (1) the `LearningPoints` window — `ActivateLearning` sets it to 30 on a damaging strike, and each attack/defense probe decrements it by 1 while > 0; (2) the `Increase(1)` side-effect — while `LearningPoints > 0`, each probe also calls `Increase(1)` on the relevant skill, which accumulates tries counters and calls `req_skill_tries` to level the skill. PC-2 wires half (1) only: `CreatureBase::learning_points: i32` + `activate_learning()` + the attack-probe decrement. Half (2) is PC-5 scope because `PlayerSkills` has no `_tries` fields yet and `req_skill_tries` is not integrated. The target-side defense probe `LearningPoints`/shield-skill `Increase` is likewise PC-5 (`roll_target_defense` has no `learning` flag yet). **Test:** `strike_learning_points_decrement_gated_on_active` sets `learning_points = 5` + zero `melee_damage` multiplier (no damage → no `ActivateLearning`), asserts `learning_points == 4` after one strike (one decrement, no reset). *(pc2-learning-points-window, `creature/base.rs`, `player/combat/strike.rs`, July 2026)*

126. **Pre-existing test-compile blocker on `main` — `EventDispatcher::as_any` trait method missing from `CountingEventDispatcher`/`CountingEventDispatcherProxy` impls** (`crates/tfs-rust-core/src/event_dispatcher.rs:111`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/test_world.rs:55`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/creature_think_tests.rs:13`): `cargo test -p tfs-rust-core` on unmodified `main` fails with `E0046: not all trait items implemented, missing: as_any` on two test-only `EventDispatcher` impls. The `as_any` method was added to the trait (with `NullEventDispatcher` providing it) but the two test stubs were not updated. Trivial 2-line fix (`fn as_any(&self) -> &dyn std::any::Any { self }` on each) unblocks test compilation. Not PC-2 scope but required to verify PC-2 tests. *(preexisting-as-any-test-blocker, `test_world.rs`, `creature_think_tests.rs`, July 2026)*

127. **PC-2 fist-only no-damage bug — `sim_melee_attack` initialized to 0 for real players in `login.rs`** (`crcombat.cc:183` `RaceData[Race].Attack`, `runtime/mon/human.mon` `Attack=7`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/login.rs:212`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/creature/player.rs:185-187`): The `sim_melee_attack` field is a sim-harness stub for the human race fist attack value (`RaceData[Race].Attack = 7` from `human.mon`). It was correctly set to `7` in `sim_harness.rs:108` (`sim_hero_player`) but initialized to `0` in `login.rs:212` (the real player construction path). With no weapon equipped, `player_get_attack_value` returns `(sim_melee_attack, SkillNr::Fist)` = `(0, Fist)` → `weapon_damage` produces 0 → `melee_damage_after_defense_and_armor(0, defense, armor)` = 0 → no damage ever. The player clicks attack, the strike fires, but the monster takes 0 damage and nothing visible happens. **Fix:** initialize `sim_melee_attack: 7` and `sim_melee_defense: 5` in `login.rs` (matching `human.mon` `Attack=7`/`Defend=5`). The `sim_*` fields are placeholders until a proper race-data system is wired; they must carry the human race defaults everywhere real players are constructed, not just in the sim harness. *(pc2-fist-zero-damage, `login.rs`, July 2026)*

128. **PC-2 combat feedback — `notify_player_combat_damage` only handled player targets; monster targets got no damage text, no health bar update** (`crmain.cc:765` `TextualEffect`, `crmain.cc:777-784` status message, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/game_world_spectators.rs:457-552`): The original `notify_player_combat_damage` had `let Some(CreatureKind::Player(p)) = self.creatures.get(target_id) else { return; }` at the top — it returned early for non-player targets. So when a player attacked a monster, the strike dealt damage (blood splatters appeared via `apply_physical_hit_blood` in `combat_execute_with_stimulus`), but no animated damage text was broadcast, no health bar fan-out happened, and the monster's health bar on the client stayed stale until the player left and re-entered the viewport (which triggered a full creature re-send). **Fix:** rewrite `notify_player_combat_damage` to handle all creature types: read `pos`/`wire_id`/`hp_pct` from any `CreatureKind` (not just `Player`); broadcast animated damage text + health bar to spectators for ALL targets; send the private "You lose X hitpoints" status message + `send_player_stats` only for player targets (monsters don't have connections). *(pc2-monster-damage-feedback, `game_world_spectators.rs`, July 2026)*

129. **PC-2 poff/spark effects — C++ `Damage` emits `EFFECT_POFF` (3) on miss and `EFFECT_BLOCK_HIT` (4) on armor-absorbs-all; both were missing from the Rust strike paths** (`crmain.cc:577-579` `Damage <= 0 → EFFECT_POFF`, `crmain.cc:624-628` physical + armor absorbs all → `EFFECT_BLOCK_HIT`, `enums.hh:178-179` `EFFECT_POFF=3, EFFECT_BLOCK_HIT=4`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/combat/strike.rs:112-122`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/monster_ai.rs:421-431,518-528`): The C++ `TCreature::Damage` function emits graphical effects for three outcomes: (1) `Damage <= 0` (defense >= attack, pre-armor) → `EFFECT_POFF` (3, the puff cloud); (2) physical damage but armor absorbs all → `EFFECT_BLOCK_HIT` (4, the gray spark); (3) damage gets through → `EFFECT_BLOOD_HIT` + `TextualEffect` + splash (already handled by `apply_physical_hit_blood` + `notify_player_combat_damage`). Our Rust code applies armor in `melee_damage_after_defense_and_armor` BEFORE calling `combat_execute_with_stimulus`, so by the time we reach the shared path, `dmg == 0` could be from either defense or armor. The poff-vs-spark distinction needs the pre-armor values (`attack_roll` vs `defense_roll`), so it must be at the strike call site, not in `combat_execute_with_stimulus`. **Fix:** add `if dmg <= 0 { broadcast_magic_effect(pos, if attack_roll <= defense_roll { 3 } else { 4 }) }` after computing `dmg` in all three strike paths (player `strike.rs`, monster melee `monster_ai.rs:518`, monster ranged `monster_ai.rs:421`). *(pc2-poff-spark-effects, `player/combat/strike.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, July 2026)*

130. **PC-2 killing-blow damage text — `notify_player_combat_damage` was called AFTER `combat_execute_with_stimulus`, which removes the target via `apply_creature_death` → `remove_creature` when HP ≤ 0** (`crmain.cc:765` `TextualEffect`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/game_world_spectators.rs:491`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/combat/strike.rs:129-162`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/monster_ai.rs:436-471,546-565`): `notify_player_combat_damage` read `pos`/`wire_id`/`hp_pct` from `self.creatures.get(target_id)`, which returns `None` after the target dies and is removed. So the killing-blow damage text (animated text `0x84`) and health bar update were never sent — the client saw the monster disappear (corpse + death effect) but no damage number floated. **Fix:** introduce `CombatNotifySnapshot` (pos, wire_id, is_player, max_hp) captured BEFORE `combat_execute_with_stimulus` via `combat_notify_snapshot(target_id)`. Pass it to `notify_player_combat_damage`, which re-reads HP only if the target is still alive (0% if dead). Updated all 3 strike call sites (player `strike.rs`, monster melee `monster_ai.rs`, monster ranged `monster_ai.rs`). *(pc2-killing-blow-damage-text, `game_world_spectators.rs`, `strike.rs`, `monster_ai.rs`, July 2026)*

131. **PC-2 target swap — `GamePacket::Attack`/`Follow` were incorrectly gated by `player_packet_action_ready` (attack cooldown), silently dropping target-swap packets mid-attack** (`receiving.cc:1133-1155` `CAttack` has NO `EarliestAttackTime` gate, `crcombat.cc:442-511` `CanToDoAttack` checks it at `TDAttack` execute time, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/game_loop.rs:117-161,250-259`): `game_packet_requires_timed_action` included `GamePacket::Attack` and `GamePacket::Follow` in the gated set. When the player was mid-attack cooldown (`earliest_attack_ms` in the future), `player_packet_action_ready` → `attack_ready_at` returned false, and the Attack packet for the new target was silently dropped with a trace log. The player kept attacking the old target until the cooldown expired. C++ `CAttack` calls `SetAttackDest` + `ToDoAttack` + `ToDoStart` unconditionally — the attack cooldown is only checked in `CanToDoAttack` when the `TDAttack` action executes, not at packet receipt. **Fix:** add `GamePacket::Attack { .. }` and `GamePacket::Follow { .. }` to the exclusion list in `game_packet_requires_timed_action`. *(pc2-target-swap-gate, `game_loop.rs`, July 2026)*

132. **PC-2 walk stops attack — `maybe_idle_stimulus_after_go_complete` called `monster_idle_stimulus` directly instead of `idle_stimulus`, so players never got their `IdleStimulus` after a walk** (`crplayer.cc:392-395` `TPlayer::IdleStimulus` re-arms `ToDoAttack` when `AttackDest != 0`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/idle_stimulus.rs:2759-2761`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/walk/mod.rs:465`): `maybe_idle_stimulus_after_go_complete` was shared by `finish_creature_todo_execute` and `process_creature_todo` to re-arm idle after a `Go` completes. It called `monster_idle_stimulus` directly, which returns early for non-monsters (`monster_idle_stimulus_inner` line 1127: `let Some(CreatureKind::Monster(m)) = ... else { return; }`). So when a player walked while attacking: (1) `player_move_request` cleared the ToDo queue (removing the queued `Attack`), (2) the `Go` executed and completed, (3) `maybe_idle_stimulus_after_go_complete` → `monster_idle_stimulus` → no-op for players, (4) the attack was never re-armed even though `attack_target` was still set. The player had to manually re-click Attack. **Fix:** change `maybe_idle_stimulus_after_go_complete` to call `self.idle_stimulus(cid)` which dispatches to `player_idle_stimulus` for players (re-arms `ToDoAttack` when `attack_target` is set) and `monster_idle_stimulus` for monsters (same behavior as before). *(pc2-walk-stops-attack, `idle_stimulus.rs`, July 2026)*

133. **PC-2 respawn retry spam — `stall_respawn` set `respawn_at = now_ms` (immediately due), causing per-second WARN log spam when placement failed** (`crnonpl.cc:1296-1323` `StartMonsterhomeTimer` resets `Timer = random(regen/2, regen)`, `crnonpl.cc:1457-1487` `ProcessMonsterhomes` calls `StartMonsterhomeTimer` after `SearchSpawnField` fails or player proximity shrinks radius below 0, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/spawn.rs:192-196`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/spawn_lifecycle.rs:324-337`): When a monster died and a player stood near its spawn point, the respawn placement failed every tick (radius shrunk below 0 due to player proximity, or all tiles blocked). `stall_respawn` set `respawn_at = now_ms`, making the slot **immediately due again** on the next `poll_spawn_respawns` check. This produced a WARN log every second indefinitely ("could not place spawned monster on map"). C++ `ProcessMonsterhomes` calls `StartMonsterhomeTimer(i)` on **both** success-with-room-for-more and failure paths, which resets the timer to a full `spawntime` cycle — it does NOT retry immediately. **Fix:** (1) `stall_respawn` now sets `respawn_at = now_ms + spawntime_ms` (full respawn cycle delay, matching `StartMonsterhomeTimer`); (2) lowered the placement-failure log from `warn!` to `debug!` — C++ silently skips when `MaxRadius < 0` or `SearchSpawnField` finds no tile, emitting no diagnostic. *(pc2-respawn-retry-spam, `spawn.rs`, `spawn_lifecycle.rs`, July 2026)*

134. **PC-2 melee from any distance — `CHASE_MODE_NONE` path in `player_can_to_do_attack_chase` returned `Adjacent` unconditionally, allowing melee strikes from across the screen** (`crcombat.cc:442-511` `CanToDoAttack` does nothing for `CHASE_MODE_NONE`, `crcombat.cc:608-616` `Attack()` calls `DelayAttack(200)` then checks `Range == 1 && Distance > 1` → `throw TARGETOUTOFRANGE`, `cract.cc:870-877` Execute catch does `ToDoYield` (re-arm), `sending.cc:348` `TARGETOUTOFRANGE` falls through to `default: break` (no message sent), `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/combat/mod.rs:293-297`): The Rust `player_can_to_do_attack_chase` `else` branch (non-`Close` chase modes) returned `PlayerChaseOutcome::Adjacent` regardless of distance. The `Adjacent` outcome in `player_execute_attack` calls `player_close_attack_strike` unconditionally — so a player with `CHASE_MODE_NONE` (stand still) could strike a monster at cheb=7. C++ `CanToDoAttack` does nothing for `CHASE_MODE_NONE` (no chase Go), then `Attack()` checks the weapon range vs actual distance: `Range == 1` (melee) + `Distance > 1` → `throw TARGETOUTOFRANGE` → `ToDoYield` (re-arm, no strike, no message). **Fix:** the `else` branch now checks `cheb > 1` → new `PlayerChaseOutcome::OutOfRange` (re-arm with `DelayAttack(200)`, no strike); `cheb ≤ 1` → `Adjacent` (strike). The `OutOfRange` outcome mirrors the C++ `TARGETOUTOFRANGE` path: `DelayAttack(200)` + re-arm, no `SendResult` (C++ `sending.cc` has no case for `TARGETOUTOFRANGE`). *(pc2-melee-from-any-distance, `player/combat/mod.rs`, July 2026)*

135. **LUA-1 constants centralization — three pre-existing wrong values uncovered while moving scattered `globals.set` lines into `constants.rs`** (`reference/tvp-772/gameserver/src/const.h:62-76,264-266`, `reference/tvp-772/gameserver/src/enums.h:266,275,146,147,297`, `crates/tfs-rust-lua/src/runtime.rs::register_event_script_bootstrap`, `crates/tfs-rust-lua/src/constants.rs`, `tasks/lua-api-plan.md` LUA-1): The scattered `globals.set` constant lines in `register_event_script_bootstrap` had drifted from the 772 reference values: (1) `TALKTYPE_CHANNEL_Y/O/R1` were `7/8/14` (TFS 1.4.2 numbering) but 772 `const.h:66,73,71` says `5/12/10` — `game_world_chat.rs` already used the correct 772 values locally, so the Lua constants were inconsistent with core; (2) `PlayerFlag_CanTalkRedChannel` was `1<<21` but `const.h:265` says `1<<22` — `1<<21` is actually `PlayerFlag_CanTalkRedPrivate` (`const.h:264`), which was not registered at all; (3) `CONDITION_SOUL` was `0` but `enums.h:266` says `1<<13`; `CONDITION_PARAM_SOULGAIN/SOULTICKS` were `0` but `enums.h:146,147` says `12/13`. The plan only called out `CONDITIONID_DEFAULT` (was `0`, should be `-1` per `enums.h:275`) as the known bug, but the others were silently wrong too. The no-op `Condition` stub masked the CONDITION_* errors (values were passed to no-op setters), and the channel scripts hadn't been invoked yet (CH-4 wiring pending), so the TALKTYPE/PlayerFlag errors were latent. **Fix:** `constants.rs` hardcodes all 772 values with `const.h:NN`/`enums.h:NN` cites. `TALKTYPE_*` are NOT sourced from `tfs_rust_common::enums::SpeakType` because that enum carries 1098 numbering (`ChannelYellow=7`, `ChannelWhite=8`, …) and is used by `idle_stimulus.rs` — adding a 772-correct `SpeakClasses` enum is deferred to avoid touching those callers. *(lua1-constants-drift, `constants.rs`, `runtime.rs`, July 2026)*

136. **LUA-1 `SpeakType` enum in `tfs_rust_common::enums` is 1098-shaped, not 772 — do not source TALKTYPE_* from it** (`crates/tfs-rust-common/src/enums.rs:147-161`, `reference/tvp-772/gameserver/src/const.h:62-76`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/idle_stimulus.rs:15,2214,2216`): The existing `SpeakType` enum (`Say=1, Whisper=2, Yell=3, PrivatePlayerToNpc=4, PrivateNpcToPlayer=5, Private=6, ChannelYellow=7, ChannelWhite=8, ChannelRed=9, ChannelOrange=10, PrivateRed=11, MonsterSay=36, MonsterYell=37`) matches TFS 1.4.2 (1098) wire numbering, NOT 772. The 772 `const.h` TALKTYPE enum is `SAY=1, WHISPER=2, YELL=3, PRIVATE=4, CHANNEL_Y=5, RVR_CHANNEL=6, RVR_ANSWER=7, RVR_CONTINUE=8, BROADCAST=9, CHANNEL_R1=10, PRIVATE_RED=11, CHANNEL_O=12, CHANNEL_R2=14, MONSTER_YELL=16, MONSTER_SAY=17`. The codebase is 772-default but this enum is 1098-shaped and is consumed by `idle_stimulus.rs` (`SpeakType::MonsterSay as u8` = 36, `SpeakType::Say as u8` = 1). Until a 772-correct `SpeakClasses` enum is added (and `idle_stimulus.rs` callers migrated), Lua TALKTYPE constants must be hardcoded in `constants.rs` with `const.h:NN` cites. The single source of truth for 772 wire talktype values is `game_world_chat.rs`'s local `const u8` block, which `constants.rs` mirrors. *(lua1-speaktype-enum-is-1098, `enums.rs`, `constants.rs`, July 2026)*

137. **LUA-2 account-type backing — `accounts.type` is a separate axis from `players.group_id`, and the DB layer wasn't loading it** (`reference/tvp-772/gameserver/src/enums.h:80-85`, `reference/tvp-772/gameserver/src/iologindata.cpp` `gameworldAuthentication`, `crates/tfs-rust-db/src/player.rs::load_player_full`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/creature/player.rs`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/login.rs::player_from_loaded`, `tasks/lua-api-plan.md` §1.3): Channel scripts gate on `player:getAccountType()` (account-level: NORMAL/TUTOR/GM/GOD, `accounts.type` 1-6) while the codebase's access checks use `group_id`→`Group.access` (per-character `groups.xml` flags). `Player` had `account_id`/`group_id` but no `account_type`; the DB layer's `load_player_full` selected `premium_ends_at` from `accounts` but dropped `type`. **Fix:** added `Player.account_type: u8` (default `ACCOUNT_TYPE_NORMAL=1`); folded `type` into the existing `SELECT premium_ends_at FROM accounts` query as `SELECT premium_ends_at, type FROM accounts WHERE id = ?` (one query, no extra round-trip); `LoadedPlayerData.account_type` carries it through; `player_from_loaded` sets it. All 5 `Player { … }` construction sites updated (login.rs, sim_harness.rs, spell_tests.rs, tests/arena.rs, notifications.rs). The `ScriptContext::get_player_account_type` read returns `Option<u8>`; the Lua binding widens to `i32` so `>=` comparisons against `ACCOUNT_TYPE_GAMEMASTER` (4) etc. work without u8/i32 mismatch errors. *(lua2-account-type-plumb, `player.rs`, `player.rs` db, `login.rs`, July 2026)*

138. **LUA-2 LuaJIT (5.1) has no bitwise shift operator — `1 << 22` in Lua test code is a SyntaxError** (`crates/tfs-rust-lua/Cargo.toml` `mlua` `features = ["luajit", "vendored"]`, `crates/tfs-rust-lua/src/userdata/player.rs::tests`): The engine is LuaJIT (Lua 5.1 dialect), where `<<` is not an operator (bitwise shift is `bit.lshift(x, n)` from the `bit` library, or the 5.3+ `//`/`<<` operators). Writing `player:hasFlag(1 << 22)` in test Lua source raises `SyntaxError: unexpected symbol near '<'`. **Fix:** pass the precomputed literal (`1 << 22 == 4194304`) directly in test Lua. Production scripts use the `PlayerFlag_CanTalkRedChannel` global constant (registered in `constants.rs` as `1i32 << 22` on the Rust side), so they never write a bare `<<` in Lua. *(lua2-luajit-no-shift-operator, `userdata/player.rs` tests, July 2026)*

139. **LUA-2 `Vocation` userdata wraps the raw id by value — no SlotMap lookup needed** (`reference/tvp-772/gameserver/src/luascript.cpp` `luaPlayerGetVocation`, `crates/tfs-rust-lua/src/userdata/vocation.rs`, `tasks/lua-api-plan.md` §1.4): Channel scripts only call `player:getVocation():getId()`. The C++ `luaPlayerGetVocation` returns a `Vocation*` userdata, but the entire payload the scripts read is `Vocation::id` (the `players.vocation` column). Rather than store a SlotMap key and re-resolve, `VocationRef(i32)` stores the raw vocation id by value — `getId()` is a field return, no context lookup. This matches §1.4 option (a) and stays extensible: `getName`/`getPromotion` can later resolve the id through `VocationRegistry` via `ScriptContext` if needed. The `getVocation` binding returns `nil` when the player is missing (default-`None` `ScriptContext`), so null-context tests degrade gracefully. *(lua2-vocation-userdata-by-value, `userdata/vocation.rs`, July 2026)*
140. **LUA-3/LUA-4 772 `ConditionType_t` uses bit flags, Rust `ConditionType` uses sequential numbering — the era-mapping seam lives in core** (`reference/tvp-772/gameserver/src/enums.h:249-269`, `crates/tfs-rust-common/src/enums.rs::ConditionType`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/game_world_chat.rs::condition_type_from_lua_772`): The 772 `ConditionType_t` enum uses `1 << N` bit flags (e.g. `CONDITION_CHANNELMUTEDTICKS = 1 << 15 = 32768`), while the Rust `ConditionType` enum follows TFS 1.4.2 sequential numbering (`ChannelMutedTicks = 17`). The Lua scripts reference the 772 bit-flag values (registered as globals in `constants.rs`), but the Rust internal `ActiveCondition.ctype` stores the sequential `ConditionType`. The `LuaMutation::PlayerAddCondition` carries the raw Lua-facing `i32` ctype; the core applier (`condition_type_from_lua_772`) maps it to `ConditionType` before creating the `ActiveCondition`. This keeps the era-mapping seam in core (where `ConditionType` and `ActiveCondition` live) and the lua crate era-agnostic. The 772 enum also has variants not in the Rust enum (`CONDITION_REGENERATION`, `CONDITION_SOUL`, `CONDITION_PACIFIED`) and vice versa (`ExhaustWeapon/Combat/Heal`, `Freezing`, `Dazzled`, `Cursed`); only the channel-script-relevant variants are mapped, unknown values log a warning and return `ConditionType::None`. *(lua3-4-conditiontype-bitflag-mapping, `game_world_chat.rs`, July 2026)*

141. **LUA-4 `Player` global is both a class table and a constructor — use `__call` metamethod, don't overwrite** (`crates/tfs-rust-lua/src/runtime.rs::register_event_script_bootstrap`, `reference/tvp-772/gameserver/src/luascript.cpp` `registerClass`): `register_event_script_bootstrap` sets `Player` as a plain Lua table for `function Player:onInventoryUpdate(...)` method definitions (read later by `register_table_method_callback`). Overwriting `Player` with a constructor function breaks method registration (can't index a function). **Fix:** set a `__call` metamethod on the existing `Player` table so `Player(name)` invokes the constructor while `Player.method = ...` still works as a table field write. `set_metatable` returns `()` in mlua (not `Result`), so no `?` operator. *(lua4-player-call-metamethod, `runtime.rs`, July 2026)*

142. **LUA-4 `LuaMutation` no longer `Copy` — `String` fields force `Clone`** (`crates/tfs-rust-lua/src/lua_mutation.rs`): Adding `PlayerSendCancelMessage { text: String }` and `SendChannelMessage { text: String }` to `LuaMutation` breaks the `#[derive(Copy)]` — `String` is not `Copy`. Changed to `#[derive(Clone)]` only. The `apply_mutation` function takes `LuaMutation` by value, which now moves the `String` fields instead of copying. Call sites that construct the mutation inline (`apply_mutation(LuaMutation::PlayerSendCancelMessage { ... })`) work unchanged; no call site relies on `Copy`. *(lua4-mutation-no-copy, `lua_mutation.rs`, July 2026)*

143. **LUA-4 advertising-channel scripts were deleted in a cascade snapshot — restore from git before uncommenting CH-5 blocks** (`data/scripts/chatchannels/advertising.lua`, `data/scripts/chatchannels/advertising-rook.lua`, commit `3a5a7d2`): The `channel_scripts_load_with_constants` test expects 8 registered channels, but only 6 non-skipped scripts existed because `advertising.lua`, `advertising-rook.lua`, `englishchat.lua`, and `worldchat.lua` were deleted in commit `3a5a7d2` (cascade snapshot). The plan references `advertising*.lua` as having CH-5 blocks to uncomment. **Fix:** restored `advertising.lua` (id=9) and `advertising-rook.lua` (id=10) from commit `9f321a8` with CH-5 blocks uncommented. `englishchat.lua` and `worldchat.lua` were not restored (not referenced by the plan; restoring them would exceed the expected 8-channel count). *(lua4-advertising-scripts-restored, `data/scripts/chatchannels/`, July 2026)*

144. **PC-3 `OUTOFAMMO` (38) has no `SendResult` message — 772 `sending.cc:348` `default: break`** (`reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/crcombat.cc:725,757`, `reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/sending.cc:285-357`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/combat/mod.rs::send_combat_result`): The 772 `OUTOFAMMO` result code (38) is thrown by `WandAttack` (insufficient mana, `crcombat.cc:725`) and `DistanceAttack` (no ammo, `crcombat.cc:757`). The `crplayer.cc:393-401` catch path calls `SendResult(Connection, r)`, but `sending.cc:291-348` has no `case OUTOFAMMO:` — it falls through to `default: break`, so `Message = NULL` and no `SendMessage` is emitted. The catch path still does `ToDoWait(1000)` + `ToDoStart`. **Fix:** added `CombatResult::OutOfAmmo` to the Rust enum; `send_combat_result` early-returns for `OutOfAmmo` (no text queued), matching `default: break`. The caller's re-arm cadence (`DelayAttack(200)`) mirrors the `ToDoYield` path. *(pc3-outofammo-no-message, `player/combat/mod.rs`, July 2026)*

145. **PC-3 ranged dispatch — `player_can_to_do_attack_chase` must check weapon range, not just `cheb ≤ 1`** (`reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/crcombat.cc:608-639`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/combat/mod.rs::player_can_to_do_attack_chase`): The `CHASE_MODE_NONE` branch previously returned `Adjacent` for `cheb ≤ 1` and `OutOfRange` for `cheb > 1`, regardless of weapon type. But C++ `Attack()` checks `GetDistance()` (the weapon's `Range`): range 1 → `CloseAttack`, range 2/3 → `DistanceAttack`/`WandAttack` with a 7×5 visible window check (`abs(dx) ≤ 7 && abs(dy) ≤ 5`, `crcombat.cc:624-627`). A player with a bow (range 6) at `cheb=3` under `CHASE_MODE_NONE` should strike, not re-arm with `TARGETOUTOFRANGE`. **Fix:** added `player_weapon_range` (returns `ItemType.shoot_range` for distance weapons, `3` for wands, `1` for melee/fist); the `CHASE_MODE_NONE` branch now checks `weapon_range ≥ 2` → 7×5 window + `cheb ≤ weapon_range` → new `PlayerChaseOutcome::RangedStrike`; else `OutOfRange`. The `Adjacent` arm in `player_execute_attack` also checks `weapon_range ≥ 2` → `player_ranged_attack_strike` (a ranged weapon at cheb=1 is still within its range). *(pc3-ranged-dispatch, `player/combat/mod.rs`, `player/combat/values.rs`, July 2026)*

146. **PC-3 wand damage roll — `WandDef.damage_min/max` maps to 772 `WANDATTACKSTRENGTH/VARIATION` via `(min+max)/2` + `(max-min)/2`** (`reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/crcombat.cc:731`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/combat/ranged.rs::player_wand_attack`): The 772 `WandAttack` rolls `AttackStrength + random(-AttackVariation, AttackVariation)` (`crcombat.cc:731`). TFS `WandDef` stores `damage_min`/`damage_max` (from `weapon:damage(min, max)` in `wands.lua`). The mapping: `AttackStrength = (min + max) / 2`, `AttackVariation = (max - min) / 2` — so `random(-V, V)` produces the `[min, max]` range. For `Wand of Vortex` (damage 8..18): `Strength=13`, `Variation=5` → rolls 8..18. The wand's `DamageType` is `WandDef.element` (e.g. `CombatType::Energy`), and `WANDRANGE=3` is hardcoded (wands don't carry range in `WandDef`). *(pc3-wand-damage-roll, `player/combat/ranged.rs`, July 2026)*

147. **PC-3 distance hit probe — `Difficulty = (Distance >= 2) ? Distance : 5`, not the weapon range** (`reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/crcombat.cc:792`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/combat/ranged.rs::player_distance_attack`): The 772 `DistanceAttack` hit probe uses `Probe(Difficulty * 15, HitChance, LearningPoints > 0)` where `Difficulty = (Distance >= 2) ? Distance : 5` — `Distance` is the Chebyshev distance to the target, NOT the weapon's range. So a target at cheb=3 gives `Difficulty=3` → probe threshold `3*15=45`; a target at cheb=1 gives `Difficulty=5` (the else branch) → probe threshold `5*15=75`. The `HitChance` is 90 for bows, 75 for throwing weapons (`crcombat.cc:766,779`). The Rust `probe_hit(rng, skill, difficulty * 15, hit_chance)` mirrors this exactly. *(pc3-distance-hit-probe-difficulty, `player/combat/ranged.rs`, July 2026)*

148. **PC-3 typed damage hit effects — wands showed the projectile and damage text but no impact animation** (`reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/crmain.cc:706-765`, `src/game.cpp:3999-4065`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/idle_stimulus.rs::combat_execute_with_stimulus`): The shared combat apply path only emitted the physical blood/splash effect (`apply_physical_hit_blood`) for `CombatType::Physical`. Wand hits use elemental damage (fire/energy/earth/life-drain), so the projectile flew, HP changed, and animated damage text appeared, but no magic effect was broadcast on the target tile. C++ `TCreature::Damage` maps `DAMAGE_FIRE → EFFECT_FIRE (16)`, `DAMAGE_ENERGY → EFFECT_ENERGY_HIT (12)`, `DAMAGE_POISON → EFFECT_POISON (9)`, and `DAMAGE_LIFEDRAIN → EFFECT_MAGIC_RED (14)`; TFS `Game::combatGetTypeInfo` uses the same wire bytes for these four types. **Fix:** added `combat_type_hit_effect` in `combat/mod.rs` and broadcast the matching magic effect in `combat_execute_with_stimulus` for non-physical damage that lands (primary + secondary). Physical blood and invisibility removal stay unchanged. Added `test_combat_execute_typed_damage_broadcasts_hit_effect`. *(pc3-typed-hit-effect, `combat/mod.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, July 2026)*

149. **Player `PlayerFlag_SetMaxSpeed` was parsed but never applied to base walk speed** (`src/const.h`, `src/player.h` `updateBaseSpeed`/`getStepSpeed`, `src/groups.cpp`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/flags.rs`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/creature/vocation.rs::base_walk_speed`): `groups.lua` already shipped the `setmaxspeed` key, and the Lua loader stored it in `Group.flags`, but `flag_name_to_bit` did not map it to a `PlayerFlag` bit. Even if it had, `player_from_loaded`, `add_experience`, and `remove_experience` computed speed with `base_walk_speed(level/vocation)` and ignored the flag, so GM/CM/God characters with `setmaxspeed = true` still walked at normal vocation/level speed. **Fix:** added `PLAYER_FLAG_SET_MAX_SPEED = 1 << 29`; resolved the flag from the player's group at login and cached `set_max_speed` on `Player`; `base_walk_speed` now short-circuits to 1500 when the flag is set; level-up/down recalculation uses the cached flag. The shipped 772 `data/defs/groups.lua` keeps `setmaxspeed = false` (TVP 772 parity); set it to `true` for GM/CM/God to match TFS 1.4 / 1098 behavior. Added tests for flag resolution and 772/1098 max-speed wire values. *(player-setmaxspeed, `player/flags.rs`, `creature/vocation.rs`, `login.rs`, `creature/player.rs`, July 2026)*

150. **Refactor Phase 3 — `_772` naming-rule violations were mostly already fixed; remaining were in content/lua/net, not just core** (`docs/REFACTOR_AUDIT.md` Phase 3, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/`, `crates/tfs-rust-content/src/`, `crates/tfs-rust-lua/src/`, `crates/tfs-rust-net/src/`): The audit (2026-07-01) listed 259 `_772` identifiers / 110 on `fn`s, but by 2026-07-10 the audit's 10 example functions (`advance_beat_772`, `process_creatures_772`, etc.) had already been renamed in prior work. The remaining production `_772` functions were spread across **four** crates, not just `tfs-rust-core`: (1) `condition_type_from_lua_772` in core, (2) six `items_db` methods (`is_terrain_bank_772`, `is_unpass_772`, `is_unmove_772`, `is_avoid_hazard_772`, `avoid_damage_type_772`, `waypoints_raw_772`) + `reference_772_objects_srv_under` in content, (3) `return_value_message_772` in lua, (4) `send_icons_772`, `liquid_color_772`, `build_login_success_772`, `premium_days_left_772` in net. All renamed to behavior-based names (`is_unpass` → `is_unpassable`, `is_unmove` → `is_immovable` for idiomatic Rust; `send_icons_772` → `send_icons_classic` for the u8 narrow-field variant; `build_login_success_772` → `build_login_success_classic` paired with the existing `_1098` → `_modern` convention). **Exceptions kept:** test fns (`test_772_*`), config (`protocol_version_reads_772`), data constants (`OTB_MAJOR_772`, `LOGIN_ERR_772`, `REF_772_DIR_NAMES` — literal values, not functions), local variables (`is_772`, `codec_772` — not public APIs). The `_1098`-suffixed production functions (`send_player_stats_1098`, `send_player_skills_1098`, `send_basic_data_1098`, `build_login_success_1098`, `enqueue_initial_login_packets_1098`) are also naming-rule violations but were out of scope for Phase 3 as written — they should be addressed in a follow-up. *(refactor-phase3-772-rename, all crates, July 2026)*

151. **PC-4 INVULNERABLE right — use TFS `PlayerFlag_CannotBeAttacked` (bit 3), not a 772 `CharacterRights` DB table** (`reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/enums.hh:517`, `reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/crmain.cc:536-538`, `src/const.h`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/flags.rs`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/idle_stimulus.rs::combat_execute_with_stimulus`): The 772 decompile uses a per-character `Rights` bitfield (INVULNERABLE = bit 61) loaded from a `CharacterRights` DB table at login. TFS 1.4.2 instead uses `groups.xml` `PlayerFlag_CannotBeAttacked` (bit 3) — same observable outcome (incoming damage zeroed + `EFFECT_POFF` broadcast), no new DB table or login plumbing. The codebase already uses the group-flag system for `IGNORED_BY_MONSTERS` and `SET_MAX_SPEED`, so PC-4 added `PLAYER_FLAG_CANNOT_BE_ATTACKED = 1 << 3` + `PLAYER_FLAG_CANNOT_USE_COMBAT = 1 << 0` (the `NO_ATTACK` right equivalent) to `player/flags.rs` and mapped them in `flag_name_to_bit` (`cannotbeattacked`, `cannotusecombat`). The M1 check lives at the top of `combat_execute_with_stimulus`, gated on `primary.1 < 0 || secondary.1 < 0` to avoid blocking heals/condition applies (C++ `Damage` is only called with negative values for damage in practice). *(pc4-invulnerable-group-flag, `player/flags.rs`, `idle_stimulus.rs`, July 2026)*

152. **PC-4 `BlockLogout(60)` — `EarliestProtectionZoneRound` only extends when `block_pz` or already pending** (`reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/crmain.cc:433-453`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/combat/fight_mode.rs::player_block_logout`): 772 `TCreature::BlockLogout(Delay, BlockProtectionZone)` sets `EarliestLogoutRound = max(., RoundNr + Delay)` unconditionally, but `EarliestProtectionZoneRound` is only extended when `BlockProtectionZone == true` **or** the player already has a pending PZ block (`EarliestProtectionZoneRound > RoundNr`). In `NON_PVP` worlds, `BlockProtectionZone` is cleared before the check (`crmain.cc:434-436`), so a PZ block requested in a NoPvp world is silently dropped. The Rust `player_block_logout` mirrors this: `let block_pz = block_pz && world_type != WorldType::NoPvp;` then the conditional extend. `BlockLogout(60)` is called from three sites: `player_set_attack_dest` `!Follow` path (attacker only, `crcombat.cc:434`), `player_execute_attack` strike (attacker + target, `crcombat.cc:601-602`), and the existing PK-mark clearing stub in `creature_think.rs` already handles the `EarliestLogoutRound <= RoundNr` expiry. *(pc4-blocklogout-pz-conditional, `player/combat/fight_mode.rs`, July 2026)*

153. **PC-4 SecureMode gate — `WorldType == Pvp` only; `PvpEnforced` / `NoPvp` skip the gate** (`reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/crcombat.cc:374-381,563-568`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/combat/fight_mode.rs::player_secure_mode_blocks_attack`): The 772 secure-mode PVP gate fires only when `WorldType == NORMAL` (which maps to `WorldType::Pvp`). In `PVP_ENFORCED` and `NON_PVP` worlds, secure mode does not gate attacks — the C++ `if (WorldType == NORMAL && SecureMode && !IsAttackJustified(target))` short-circuits. The Rust `player_secure_mode_blocks_attack` returns `false` early when `pvp_config.world_type != WorldType::Pvp`. The gate fires at two sites: `validate_player_attack_target` (the `SetAttackDest` `!Follow` path, `crcombat.cc:374-381`) and `player_execute_attack` (the `Attack` strike path, `crcombat.cc:563-568`) — both must check because the player can toggle secure mode between setting the target and the first strike. `IsAttackJustified` is stubbed to `false` (no aggressor/party tracking yet), so secure mode blocks **all** PvP in `WorldType::Pvp` until the skull subsystem lands. *(pc4-securemode-pvp-only-gate, `player/combat/fight_mode.rs`, `player/combat/mod.rs`, July 2026)*

154. **PC-4 `SetAttackMode` delay — `DelayAttack(2000)` fires only on **change**, not on every packet** (`reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/crcombat.cc:325-337`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/player/combat/fight_mode.rs::player_set_fight_modes`): The 772 `SetAttackMode` compares the new mode to the current `AttackMode` and only calls `DelayAttack(2000)` when they differ (`crcombat.cc:333-336`). The client sends `0xA7` `FIGHT_MODES` on every mode toggle, so the same-mode case happens when the player closes and reopens the dialog without changing the stance. The Rust `player_set_fight_modes` checks `attack_mode_changed` before calling `delay_attack_ms(server_ms, 2000)`; the `earliest_attack_ms` field is the gate for the next strike (`attack_ready_at`), so a 2000ms delay pushes the next attack beat out by 2 seconds. *(pc4-setattackmode-delay-on-change-only, `player/combat/fight_mode.rs`, July 2026)*

155. **Snapback audit — `SendSnapback` (0xB5) logic is split across TWO C++ functions; the Rust `send_result_player` exempt set was INVERTED — FIXED** (`reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/sending.cc:285-357` `SendResult`, `reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/cract.cc:870-889` `Execute` catch, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/creature_todo.rs:816-855` `send_result_player`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/walk/mod.rs:1284-1310` `on_walk_step_rejected`): The 772 snapback ("knock back / bounce back") decision is split across two C++ functions, and the Rust port conflated them. **C++ `SendResult`** (`sending.cc:351-356`) sends the failure message AND sends `SendSnapback` for exactly 3 results: `MOVENOTPOSSIBLE` / `NOTINVITED` / `ENTERPROTECTIONZONE`. **C++ `Execute` catch** (`cract.cc:871-886`) computes `SnapbackNecessary = ToDoClear() || Stop` (true iff remaining unexecuted `Go` entries), then sends `SendSnapback` for **non-exempt** results when `SnapbackNecessary`. The 3-result exemption in the Execute catch exists ONLY to avoid a double snapback (since `SendResult` already sent one). **Net C++:** the 3 results always get 1 snapback (from `SendResult`); other results get 1 snapback iff `SnapbackNecessary`. **Rust `send_result_player` (BEFORE fix):** sent `send_cancel_message` (message only, NO snapback — unlike C++ `SendResult`), then gated the explicit snapback on `had_pending_go && !snapback_exempt` where the exempt set included the 3 results + `ThereIsNoWay`. **Net Rust (BEFORE):** the 3 results got 0 snapback (exempt); other results got 1 snapback iff `had_pending_go`. **The inversion:** C++ sends snapback FOR the 3 (via `SendResult`); Rust exempted the 3 FROM snapback. **Fix (S2/S4/S6):** `send_result_player` now sends snapback unconditionally for the 2 exempt results that can reach this path (`PlayerIsNotInvited` = `NOTINVITED`, `ActionNotPermittedInProtectionZone` = `ENTERPROTECTIONZONE`), and gates non-exempt results on `had_pending_go`. `ThereIsNoWay` (C++ `NOWAY`) was removed from the exempt set — it is NOT in the C++ 3-result set (that's `MOVENOTPOSSIBLE`, which maps to `NotPossible` and only reaches the Go arm via `on_walk_step_rejected`, never `apply_todo_result_catch`). `MOVENOTPOSSIBLE` never reaches `send_result_player` because the Go arm uses `on_walk_step_rejected`. **S3 (accepted divergence):** `on_walk_step_rejected` always sends snapback for the Go arm. C++ gates on `SnapbackNecessary` for `NOTACCESSIBLE` but sends unconditional for `MOVENOTPOSSIBLE` (via `SendResult`). Both map to `ReturnValue::NotPossible` in Rust and can't be distinguished. Always sending is the safer divergence (extra harmless snapback for `NOTACCESSIBLE` without remaining Gos vs missing snapback for `MOVENOTPOSSIBLE` without remaining Gos). Tests: 5 new integration tests (`test_snapback_audit_s2_notinvited_sends_unconditional_snapback`, `test_snapback_audit_s2_enterprotectionzone_sends_unconditional_snapback`, `test_snapback_audit_s4_noway_with_pending_go_sends_snapback`, `test_snapback_audit_s4_noway_without_pending_go_no_snapback`, `test_snapback_audit_s2_nonexempt_with_pending_go_sends_snapback`) + 2 updated unit tests; 611 core pass. Full audit: `docs/SNAPBACK_KNOCKBACK_AUDIT.md`. *(snapback-audit-fix, `creature_todo.rs`, `walk/mod.rs`, `idle_stimulus_tests.rs`, July 2026)*

156. **Monster beat-loop audit — two `CalculateDelay` / `Execute` loop divergences FIXED** (`reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/cract.cc:905-915` `CalculateDelay(TDWait)`, `reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/cract.cc:783-807` `Execute` `while(true)`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/idle_stimulus.rs:2442-2466` `Wait` execute arm, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/idle_stimulus.rs:3163-3186` `run_monster_todo_execute` `Wait` dispatch): Two divergences in the monster movement beat loop, found while auditing whether monster move fires at the right intervals. **(1) `CalculateDelay(TDWait)` `EarliestWalkTime` floor (audit #14 adjacent):** C++ `CalculateDelay(TDWait)` computes `WaitTime = max(TD->Wait.Time, EarliestWalkTime)` then `Delay = WaitTime - ServerMilliseconds` (`cract.cc:906-910`). Rust's `Wait` execute arm used `todo_start_from_action(delay_ms)` which arms at `server_ms + delay_ms` — missing the `EarliestWalkTime` floor. For a slow monster (GoStrength 0 → speed 80, step duration 2000ms on 150wp ground), a `Wait{1000}` after a `Go` step would fire at `server_ms + 1000` instead of `EarliestWalkTime = server_ms + 2000` — 1000ms too early. Fix: the `Wait` arm now computes `wait_time = max(server_ms + delay_ms, earliest_walk_server_ms)` and schedules at `max(wait_time, server_ms + 1)` (preserving the `ToDoStart` `+1` clamp, `cract.cc:1016`). **(2) Zero-delay `Wait{0}` same-beat chaining (audit #14):** C++ `Execute` is a `while(true)` loop — consecutive entries with `CalculateDelay() == 0` run in the **same wakeup** (`cract.cc:784-807`). Rust's `run_monster_todo_execute` `Wait` dispatch always called `monster_combat_reschedule_if_stalled` when the queue was non-empty, which re-armed at `+1` (next beat) — each chained zero-delay action drifted +1 beat (up to 200ms). Fix: the `Wait` dispatch now checks `has_armed_wakeup` — if the `Wait` was zero-delay (no wakeup armed), it chains same-beat via `run_monster_todo_execute` (tail recursion, matching C++ `while(true)`); if a wakeup was armed (`Wait{N>0}`), it falls through to `monster_combat_reschedule_if_stalled`. Tests: 2 new (`test_monster_wait_after_go_floors_at_earliest_walk_time`, `test_monster_wait_zero_chains_same_beat_to_next_action`); clippy clean. *(monster-beat-loop-audit, `idle_stimulus.rs`, `idle_stimulus_tests.rs`, July 2026)*
157. **PC-3a AoE shape model — both eras share the 772 disc-ring grid; no `AreaShapeModel` selector needed** (`reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/magic.cc:4344` `InitCircles`, `src/combat.cpp:1391` `AreaCombat::setupArea`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/combat/circles.rs`): The 772 `circles.dat` (21×21 grid, rings 0–7, 101 tiles) and the 1098 TFS `setupArea` (13×13 grid, rings 1–8) produce **identical** ring-offset data — 772 ring N == 1098 ring N+1. An `AreaShapeModel` enum was initially added to `MechanicsProfile` to select between them, but since the data is the same, the selector was reverted. Both eras use `DISC_RINGS` (rings 0–7) directly. The 772 `ExecuteCircleSpell` (`magic.cc:459`) iterates `for R = 0; R <= Radius; R++` collecting every point in `Circle[R]`; `disc_offsets(radius)` mirrors this. *(pc3a-no-era-variance-aoe, `combat/circles.rs`, `formulas.rs`, July 2026)*

158. **PC-3a `__newindex` for `spell.onCastSpell` — mlua 0.10 puts meta methods inside `add_methods`, not a separate `add_meta_methods`** (`crates/tfs-rust-lua/src/userdata/spell.rs`, `mlua-0.10.5/src/userdata.rs:591-608`): The `data/scripts/spells/` pattern `function spell.onCastSpell(creature, variant)` is a field assignment on the `SpellBuilder` userdata, requiring a `__newindex` metamethod. In mlua 0.10, the `UserData` trait has only `add_fields` and `add_methods` — meta methods go inside `add_methods` via `methods.add_meta_method(MetaMethod::NewIndex, ...)`. There is no separate `add_meta_methods` function. The `SpellBuilder` stores the captured function as a `RegistryKey` in `Rc<RefCell<Option<RegistryKey>>>`, and `:register()` stores it in a parallel `_pending_spell_callbacks` Lua table at the same index as the spell in `_pending_spells`. `load_spell_scripts` then drains both tables, storing each callback's `RegistryKey` on the `LuaRuntime` via `register_spell_callback(words, key)`. *(pc3a-newindex-mlua-0.10, `userdata/spell.rs`, `combat_scripts.rs`, July 2026)*

159. **PC-3a spell exhaustion + PZ lock — 772 `CastSpell` gates before `CheckMana`, not after** (`reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/magic.cc:3387-3407` `CastSpell`, `reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/magic.cc:731-774` `CheckMana`, `reference/cipsoft-772/tibia-game-master/src/crmain.cc:433-457` `BlockLogout`, `crates/tfs-rust-core/src/game_world_chat.rs::player_say_spell`): The 772 `CastSpell` flow has a specific gate order: (1) `CheckSpellbook`/`CheckAccount`/`CheckLevel`/`CheckRing`, (2) exhaustion check `EarliestSpellTime > ServerMilliseconds → throw EXHAUSTED`, (3) aggressive spell in PZ check `IsProtectionZone → throw PROTECTIONZONE`, (4) `CheckMana` (deducts mana/soul + sets `EarliestSpellTime = max(., ServerMs + Delay)`), (5) spell-specific execution. The Rust `player_say_spell` mirrors this: vocation/level/mana gates first, then exhaustion check, then PZ check, then mana deduction + `delay_spell_ms`, then `player_block_logout(50, true)` for aggressive spells (50 rounds = 10s at 200ms/beat), then `fire_on_cast_spell` dispatch. The exhaustion delay is set **after** the gate, so the first cast always succeeds (exhaustion starts at 0). The PZ lock uses `player_block_logout` which is the same function used for melee combat (`crmain.cc:433`). *(pc3a-spell-gate-order, `game_world_chat.rs`, July 2026)*

160. **PC-3a `Combat:execute()` variant resolution — `Position` doesn't impl `FromLua` in mlua 0.10** (`crates/tfs-rust-lua/src/userdata/combat.rs`, `src/luascript.cpp:13198` `luaCombatExecute`): The TFS Lua `Combat:execute(creature, variant)` receives a variant table `{ type = N, pos = {x=.., y=.., z=..}, number = .. }`. Extracting the `pos` field as a `Position` struct directly fails because `tfs_rust_common::Position` doesn't implement `mlua::FromLua`. The fix is to extract the `pos` field as a `mlua::Table` and read `x`/`y`/`z` individually as `i64` (then cast to `u16`/`u8`). The variant type dispatch mirrors C++: type 2 = `VARIANT_POSITION`, type 3 = `VARIANT_TARGETPOSITION` (both use `pos`), type 1 = `VARIANT_NUMBER` (resolve target creature position via `ScriptContext::get_player_position`). If the variant is nil, the center falls back to the caster's position (origin spell). *(pc3a-variant-fromlua, `userdata/combat.rs`, July 2026)*

161. **Primary parity: 772 outcomes + TFS domain + idiomatic Rust** (Cursor rules, July 2026): **Outcomes** from `tibia-game-master` / `gameserver` wire. **Domain shape** stays **TFS-style** so the data pack (`data/`, Lua, XML, OTB/OTBM, cylinders, `ConditionDamage`, spell/combat userdata) keeps working — do not adopt decompile architecture. **Implementation** is best idiomatic Rust, not a TFS or decompile C++ transliteration. Era knobs in `MechanicsProfile` — no `*_772.rs` forks. When TFS 1.4.2 **numbers** disagree with the decompile, the **772 profile follows the decompile**. `config.lua` = `772`.


162. **PC-3a spell-gap audit — count from scripts, not from memory** (`tasks/pc3a-spell-gaps.md`, `data/scripts/spells/`, `data/scripts/functions.lua`): Gap docs drift when they invent area names (`AREA_WAVE3` vs actual `AREA_SQUAREWAVE5`/`AREA_WAVE4`), undercount `COMBAT_PARAM_DISPEL` (9, including healing runes) / `CREATEITEM` (11, including bombs + wild growth), or treat `poison_storm`/`envenom`/`soulfire` as TARGETCREATURE callbacks when they call `addCondition` from `onCastSpell`. `conjureItem` is datapack Lua (`Player:conjureItem` in `functions.lua`), not a missing Rust userdata method — load + bridge `Player:` methods onto `CreatureRef`. `addCondition`/`removeCondition`/`addItem` already exist on `CreatureRef`; the real gaps are combat-path condition apply, value-callback invocation, and `getMagicLevel`. Always `rg` the spell pack before updating gap counts. *(pc3a-spell-gaps-audit, July 2026)*

163. **Monster “random diagonals / pauses” — blame IdleStimulus batching, not the beat loop** — see `tasks/monster-movement-cipsoft-audit.md`. CipSoft chase is `ToDoGo(..., false, 3)` batches; open uniform TShortway stays cardinal; pauses are mostly `ToDoWait(1000)` / batch gaps / diagonal ×3 `NotifyGo`. *(July 2026)*

164. **Monster scheduler parity — dist wipe / absolute Wait / batch LockToDo** (`tasks/monster-movement-cipsoft-audit.md`, `crmain.cc:920`, `cract.cc:1033`, `cract.cc:1010`): Dist `monster_on_follow_creature_moved` must **not** clear mid-batch Go/`walk_queue` (CipSoft CLOSE + head `TDAttack` only). `CreatureAction::Wait { deadline_ms }` stores absolute `ServerMilliseconds + Delay` at enqueue. `todo.locked` is set on `ToDoStart` and held until todo + `walk_queue` drain (`creature_todo_release_lock_if_drained`), not unlocked per action. Yield + `ToDoStart` re-locks the Wait batch. *(July 2026)*

165. **Monster idle pass 4 — flee/master fallthrough + post-idle Execute break** (`crnonpl.cc:2752-2777`, `cract.cc:789-793`): HP `Flee` + failed `SearchFlightField` has no else — must re-enter the roam arm (`DistFlee` fail stays `ToDoWait` only). Master follow: Manhattan 2 = Wait-only; 3 = `ToDoWait` **then** `ToDoGo(max 3)`; ≤1 falls through to roam jiggle. After `IdleStimulus`, `Execute` must **break** for every fresh batch (not only `Go`-fronted) so `Attack`-fronted melee dance no-step does not strike same beat. Removed X3 `flee_opening_melee_dance_done` — `IsFleeing()` is checked before melee dance. *(July 2026)*

166. **Terrain-weighted TShortway — TW-1..TW-3 fixed** (`cract.cc` `TShortway`, `pathfinding.rs`): `truncate_tshortway_go_queue` stops at `cheb≤1` only (dist band via MaxSteps); empty FillMap keeps MinWaypoints=1000; dist budget allows 0; empty truncate after reachable path = success. *(July 2026)*

167. **Server never loads `objects.srv`** (`pipeline.rs`, `sim_harness::load_items_db_for`): Terrain `WAYPOINTS` come from patched OTB `ITEM_ATTR_SPEED` only (`patch-otb-waypoints` offline). Runtime overlays must not run at game startup — `objects_srv` stays for the patcher + audits. **Gap:** DistUse has no OTB bit; without the removed DistUse overlay those flags stay false until an offline DistUse content path exists. *(July 2026)*

168. **Name/HP bar flicker + caster run-turn-face** (`walk/mod.rs` `broadcast_spectator_move`, `spawn_lifecycle.rs` `send_creature_appear_to_conn`, `idle_stimulus.rs`): (1) Both-visible spectator moves with `!fully_sent` must **remove then appear** — appear-alone left a ghost and blinked bars; always `mark_creature_fully_sent` even when `known=true`. (2) `monster_idle_stimulus_after_creature_move` must `skip_casting` (C++ `CreatureMoveStimulus` is Wait+Attack only). (3) Destination/Victim cast `Rotate` must not fire when `next_wakeup` is armed. Classic rock-soil OTB speeds match srv; `items.xml` misnames unpass shallow water/mountains as "rock soil". Giant spider `runonhealth=0` — “flee” was turn-dance, not HP flee. `SpellImpact::Field` still stub. *(July 2026)*

169. **Idle Rotate 0x6B spam** (`idle_stimulus.rs` `monster_idle_rotate_toward_attack_target` / `monster_face_toward`): C++ `Rotate` then `ToDoAttack` relies on same-window Execute so the turn is masked by `Go`. P4-3 defers Execute → every chase idle broadcast a stand-still `0x6B`. Fix: enqueue Attack first, then Rotate; **suppress wire turn** when `todo.has_go` or `walk_queue` non-empty (direction still updates). Cast Destination/Victim faces without `0x6B` (idle tail owns standing broadcasts). *(July 2026)*

170. **items.xml names do not drive FillMap weights** (`fillmap_terrain_waypoints_at`): Audit vs `objects.srv` — **0** walkable-BANK speed mismatches; **0** OTB-zero / srv-walkable holes. XML↔srv name noise is widespread (rock soil↔water/pavement, lava↔floor, etc.) but path cost uses OTB `group`+`ITEM_ATTR_SPEED`+`block_solid` only. Misnamed walkable shallow water 865–869 correctly weigh **170** (slow). Residual: 249 srv walkable banks with OTB `group≠1` (ignored as ground unless stack-head ground). *(July 2026)*

171. **Rock-soil / mountain walk desync** (`items.otb` Unpass flags + `fillmap_terrain_waypoints_at`): Spider-area OTBM "rock soil" **4411–4421** is srv `a mountain` (`Bank+Unpass`, wp0). Blanket Unpass→`blockSolid` blocked **players** ("not enough room"). Fix: solidify **non-Bank** Unpass only (trees/walls); **clear** solid on Bank+Unpass+wp0 so players walk; FillMap still `-1` on speed 0; Clip-as-ground speed0 → offline **150**. Restart to reload OTB. *(July 2026)*

172. **OTB terrain cost join key is `client_id`, NOT `server_id`** (`otb_patch.rs` `srv_identity`/`build_*`, `objects_srv.rs` `resolve_server_id`, audit `audit_objects_srv_waypoints`): The `.sec`→OTBM conversion remaps 772 `TypeID → OTB server_id` via `client_id` (verified `.sec` 1026-1008-07 vs OTBM: **940/1024** tiles = `client_to_server[sec_id]`, 83 direct where server==client). So an OTB row's true 772 identity is **`objects.srv[client_id]`**. The old `patch-otb-waypoints` joined `objects.srv[server_id]` (server-first `resolve_server_id`) — wrong for the ~90% of grounds TVP renumbered. Effect: walkable rock soil (`.sec` 4402 → OTB server **4413**, client 4402) got stamped with `objects.srv[4413]="a mountain"` `wp0` → `fillmap_terrain_waypoints_at` returns `-1` → **monsters treat walkable rock soil as impassable mountain** ("creatures hate pathing here" at (32844,32269,7); **102/1024** walkable `.sec` tiles were wp0 in that sector). The old audit reported a **false 843/843 match** because it resolved via the same server-first function (self-consistent). Fix: patch/audit **iterate OTB rows** and join on `client_id`; `resolve_server_id` is now `client_id`-first. Re-patched from pristine `items.otb.bak` — only **69** speed nodes differed from TVP's shipped (already client-aligned) OTB, i.e. the old patch had *broken* 863. Audit now = **0 walkable / 0 blocked mismatches**. Supersedes the false confidence in lessons 170/171. *(otb-client-id-terrain-join, July 2026)*

173. **Monster absolute destination tracking + kick displacement detection** (`monster_ai.rs`, `walk/mod.rs` `on_walk`, `monster_push.rs`, `cract.cc:386-389` `Go` + `cract.cc:870-877` `Execute` catch, `cract.cc:1082-1106` `ToDoGo`): The decompile stores **absolute** destination coordinates in TDGo entries. When a monster is kicked mid-step, `::Move` relocates it but does NOT clear its ToDoList. On the next `Execute`, `Go(oldDestX, oldDestY, oldDestZ)` checks `Distance = max(abs(curX - oldDestX), abs(curY - oldDestY))` — the kick displacement makes `Distance > 1` → `throw NOTACCESSIBLE` → `ToDoClear + ToDoYield`. The Rust previously stored only **relative** `Direction`s in `walk_queue` for monsters (`walk_destinations` was player-only), so the displacement went undetected — the monster continued walking in the old direction from its new position. Fix: populate `walk_destinations` (absolute positions) at all monster `walk_queue` push sites (roam, flee, dance, chase path, walk-to-spawn), pop them in `on_walk`'s monster branch, and run the same adjacency check as players. The kick itself does NOT clear walk state — the displacement is detected **lazily** on the next `on_walk`, matching the decompile's `Go` → `NOTACCESSIBLE` → `ToDoClear + ToDoYield` path. Also discovered: Rust `monster_on_creature_move(self,self)` calls `monster_update_target_list` + `monster_update_idle_status` (which can clear walk_queue via `monster_set_idle(true)` when no opponents are visible), while the decompile's `CreatureMoveStimulus(self,self)` only sets `State = IDLE` + `ToDoYield()` — a separate divergence to address later. *(monster_ai.rs, walk/mod.rs, monster_push.rs, monster_events.rs, monster_targets.rs, idle_stimulus.rs, sim_harness.rs, July 2026)*

174. **Self-move stimulus must NOT re-evaluate idle/targets** (`monster_events.rs` `monster_on_creature_move` self-branch, `crnonpl.cc:2945-2952` `TMonster::CreatureMoveStimulus`): Resolves the divergence flagged in lesson 173. C++ `TMonster::CreatureMoveStimulus(self,self)` early-returns after `if (State == SLEEPING && Type != OBJECT_DELETED) { State = IDLE; ToDoYield(); }` — it does **NOT** call `updateTargetList`, `updateIdleStatus`, or forward to `TCreature::CreatureMoveStimulus` (the close-chase combat re-arm). The Rust self-move branch additionally called `monster_update_target_list` + `monster_update_idle_status`; with no visible opponents, `monster_update_idle_status` → `monster_set_idle(true)` cleared `walk_queue` / `walk_destinations` / `opponent_ids` / `friend_ids` / `damage_map` / targets and set `state = Sleeping` mid-roam — dropping in-flight steps the decompile preserves (the kick test had to pre-seed an opponent as a workaround to avoid the wipe). Fix: drop both calls from the self-move branch; `monster_sleep_wake_on_creature_move` already implements the sleep-wake + `ToDoYield` (and no-ops when not sleeping, matching the `if (State == SLEEPING ...)` gate). The `opponent_ids`-seed workaround in `monster_push_tests.rs` is now cosmetic only. Tests: `test_772_self_move_does_not_reevaluate_idle_or_clear_walk_queue`, `test_772_self_move_wakes_sleeping_monster`; 631 pass (1 pre-existing unrelated fillmap/OTB failure). *(monster_events.rs, monster_push_tests.rs, July 2026)*

175. **Pushed mid-walk: step toward absolute destination, not queued Direction** (`walk/mod.rs` `on_walk`, `cract.cc:383-446` `TCreature::Go`): The decompile's `Go(DestX, DestY, DestZ)` moves to the **exact absolute destination** stored in the TDGo entry — `Object Dest = GetMapContainer(DestX, DestY, DestZ); ::Move(this->ID, this->CrObject, Dest, -1, false, NONE)` (`cract.cc:443-446`). It does **NOT** use a direction. The Rust `on_walk` popped the queued `Direction` and stepped `current_pos.offset(dir)` — correct when the creature hasn't been displaced, but **wrong after a kick**: the queued Direction and the absolute destination diverge. Example: monster at (101,100) walking East → destination (102,100). Kicked South to (101,101). The decompile's `Go(102,100,7)` moves NE to (102,100). The old Rust path stepped East from (101,101) → (102,101) — wrong tile, wrong 0x6D `new_pos`, wrong animation direction. Fix: after the adjacency check passes, recompute `dir = direction_from_positions(cur_pos, dest)` from the current position to the stored `walk_destinations` entry (the existing helper already supports diagonal directions). For the same-position case (creature kicked to exactly its intended destination, `Distance == 0`), skip the step — the decompile's `::Move` to the same position is a map no-op. The recompute runs **before** the drunk stagger, so stagger still overrides the direction (matching `cract.cc:392-413` where stagger replaces `DestX/DestY` after the adjacency check). TVP `pushCreature` (`monster.cpp:1397`) exists but pushes **after** the mover steps (post-move `internalMoveCreature`), unlike the 772 decompile's pre-step kick (`MovePossible(Execute=true)` → `KickCreature`). Tests: `pushed_mid_walk_steps_toward_absolute_destination_not_queued_dir`, `pushed_to_exact_destination_skips_step`; 633 pass. *(walk/mod.rs, July 2026)*

176. **Monster `Angle`/`Destination` spells mis-shaped + `areaeffect` never broadcast** (`monster_combat.rs` `default_shape_for_node`/`parse_area_effect_name`, `idle_stimulus.rs` `monster_idle_spell_tiles`/`monster_idle_try_casting`): Dragons' fire wave (`<attack name="fire" length="8" spread="3">`) silently self-cast as `SpellShape::Actor` — `default_shape_for_node` ignored `length`/`spread`, so with no `range`/`radius`/`target` it fell through to `Actor`. A fire-immune dragon hitting itself = invisible + no damage = "monsters don't cast fire." Three coupled bugs: (1) shape detection — TFS data-pack `length`+`spread` must map to 772 `SHAPE_ANGLE` (`monsters.cpp:191` `setupArea`+`needDirection`), and `target="1"`+`radius>0` must map to `SHAPE_DESTINATION` (area around victim, `magic.cc:537`), not `Victim` (single target). (2) `monster_idle_spell_tiles` `Angle` used a target-vector signum line — wrong; 772 `AngleShapeSpell` (`magic.cc:550`) walks forward by **caster facing direction** with cone `±Forward*Angle/90`. Must `Rotate(Target)` first, then read the updated `direction`. (3) `parse_area_effect_name` was a stub returning `None` — `areaeffect` (firearea/blueshimmer/…) was parsed off the XML but **never sent**, so even correct shapes showed no visual. Now maps TFS names → 772 `CONST_ME_*` wire bytes (`tools.cpp:497` `magicEffectNames`, `const.h:11-35`, capped at 25 for the 772 client). **TFS↔772 spread mapping:** data-pack `spread` → 772 `Angle = spread * 10` (dragon.xml `spread=3` ↔ dragon.mon `Angle(30,8,7)`; demon `spread=0` ↔ `Angle(0,8,12)`); `length` → 772 `Range` 1:1. `area_effect` is now broadcast per tile for Origin/Angle/Destination and on the target tile for Victim (`GraphicalEffect` per `magic.cc:400/423/503/550`). Tests: `test_e0_dragon_fire_spells_shape_mapping`, `test_772_dragon_fire_wave_angle_casts_toward_target`; 635 pass (1 pre-existing fillmap/OTB failure). *(monster_combat.rs, idle_stimulus.rs, July 2026)*

177. **PC-5 skill tries — per-level storage + profile skillTuning** (`player/combat/skills.rs`, `formulas.rs` `SkillTriesTuning`, `crskill.cc:386` `Increase`): Runtime `PlayerSkills` holds per-level tries (DB schema) not 772 cumulative Exp — leveling outcomes match when `req_skill_tries` uses profile `skill_base`/`min_level` + vocation multipliers. Magic uses separate `magic_skill_base=1600`/`min_level=0`/`mana_multiplier`. Probe learning wires `Increase(1)` then LP--; shield learning only when `has_shield` (M12). Level-up must `Advance` current HP/mana (M13), not clamp-only. Death: AoL 2173 → no drop; else SOME (containers always, 10% else) into corpse 3128; bless bits reduce loss fraction (TFS domain). `fed_regen_params` returns zeros when vocation missing — no silent (12,1,6,2) fallback. *(PC-5, July 2026)*

178. **`rateSkill` / `rateMagic` are config multipliers, not formula knobs** (`config.rs` `rate_skill`/`rate_magic`/`scale_tries`, TFS `Player:onGainSkillTries`): `skillTuning` in `data/formulas/*.lua` sets tries *needed* per level; `config.lua` `rateSkill`/`rateMagic` multiply tries *gained* before `skill_increase`/`magic_increase` (`floor(base * rate)`). Exp already uses `rateExp` + optional `experienceStages`. Missing skill/magic keys default to `1.0` (same pattern as unset `rateExp`); `.dist` ships `3`/`3`. Do not put rates in `MechanicsProfile`. *(July 2026)*
